-invariant). In order to identify the

\mathfrak{m}_{w}
-module structure of
{\mathrm{RHom}}_{\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w}}\oplus\mathfrak{b}}(E(\lambda),\mathcal{O}_{P_{w}(B\cap\bar{U}_{P_{w}}),e})
we compute the cohomology in a
different way, by first considering
\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w}}
-cohomology,
and then
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology.

Certainly

{\mathrm{RHom}}_{\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w}}}(E,\mathcal{O}_{P_{w}(B\cap\bar{U}_{P_{w}}),e})=\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w}}\backslash P_{w}(B\cap\bar{U}_{P_{w}}),e}[0],

so it remains to show that

\mathrm{Ext}^{d-\ell(w)}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2}}(E(\lambda),\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w}}\backslash P_{w}(B\cap\bar{U}_{P_{w}}),e})=(\mathcal{O}_{M_{w}/U_{M_{w}},e}(-\lambda-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho-\rho))^{\mathfrak{h}}.

We will then be done, because the right hand side is
\hat{M}(\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{\vee}_{\lambda+w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho+\rho}
by Remark
3.4.4
and the proof of Proposition
3.4.13
.

We now show the claim. Note that
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{w}\oplus\mathfrak{b}\cap\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{P_{w}}
.
We first compute

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{*_{2}}(\mathfrak{b}\cap\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{P_{w}},\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w}}\backslash P_{w}(B\cap\bar{U}_{P_{w}}),e})=\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w}}\backslash P_{w},e}[0]=\mathcal{O}_{M_{w},e}[0].

We now observe that
\mathcal{O}_{M_{w},e}
is a

\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{w}
-representation, with
\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w}}
acting trivially.
We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{d-\ell(w)}_{\mathfrak{b}\cap{\mathfrak{p}}_{w},*_{2}}(E(\lambda),\mathcal{O}_{M_{w},e})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathfrak{b}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}_{w},*_{2}}(E(\lambda),\mathcal{O}_{M_{w},e}\otimes\wedge^{d-\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w}}\cap\mathfrak{b})^{\vee})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{O}_{M_{w}/U_{M_{w}},e}(-\lambda-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho-\rho))^{\mathfrak{h}}.\qed

3.6.
Localization and higher Coleman sheaves at singular weight

In this section we study localization
at a singular weight for
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and
P
is the Siegel parabolic
associated to the cocharacter
\mu=(-1/2,-1/2;1/2)\in X_{*}(T)_{E}
. We freely use our notation for
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
(see
1.8.8
).
We consider the Klingen parabolic
Q\supseteq B
attached to the simple root
\beta
. We denote by
M_{Q}
the associated Levi which is a group of semi-simple rank
1
.
It is important for us that
w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}\in{}^{M}W
; this implies that the stratum
C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
is the union of two
B
-orbits
C_{w_{0}^{M}}
and
C_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
.
We wish to study the localization
\operatorname{Loc}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}(-\lambda))|_{C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}}
for
\lambda=(1,1;w)
. We notice that in this singular weight the horizontal action is not semi-simple. We are going to study this action and describe the semi-simple part.

3.6.1.
Geometry of the strata

We consider the
Q
-orbit
C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
on
\mathcal{FL}
, which is
the union of the two Bruhat strata
C_{w_{0}^{M}}

and
C_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
.

Note that the map
Q\rightarrow C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
,
q\mapsto w_{0}^{M}q
induces an isomorphism
P_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap Q\backslash Q\rightarrow C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
.
The projection
Q\rightarrow M_{Q}
induces a map
(P_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap Q)\backslash Q\rightarrow(P_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap M_{Q})\backslash M_{Q}
. Since
M_{Q}
has semi-simple rank one and
(P_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap M_{Q})
is a Borel subgroup, we can identify
(P_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap M_{Q})\backslash M_{Q}
with
\mathbf{P}^{1}
, with
\infty
the image of
w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}
and
0
the image of
w_{0}^{M}
.
We therefore have a natural
map
\pi:C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}\to\mathbf{P}^{1}
, with
\pi^{-1}(\{\infty\})=C_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
and
\pi^{-1}(\mathbf{A}^{1})=C_{w_{0}^{M}}
; moreover,
B\cap s_{\beta}Bs_{\beta}^{-1}
acts transitively on
\pi^{-1}(\mathbf{G}_{m})=C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
.

3.6.2.
Sheaves on the union of strata

The inclusion
G_{e}\rtimes B\hookrightarrow G_{e}\rtimes Q
induces an
inclusion
\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(w_{0}^{M})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}(w_{0}^{M})
, which induces the
restriction map
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},Q)}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q})\rightarrow LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}})
. Similarly, conjugation by
s_{\beta}
gives an inclusion
\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}(w_{0}^{M})
, which induces the restriction map
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},Q)}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q})\rightarrow LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})
.

We also note that the image of
Q\cap P_{w_{0}^{M}}
in
M_{w_{0}^{M}}
is the Borel

B_{M_{w_{0}^{M}}}
(and the image of
Q\cap P_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
in

M_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
is the Borel
B_{M_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}}
). Therefore, the source
category
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}},\mathfrak{q}_{M_{w_{0}^{M}}})
for producing
sheaves on
C_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
(see Definition
3.4.17
) is
tautologically equal to
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w_{0}^{M}}})
.

Proposition 3.6.3
.

Let
\nu\in X^{*}(M_{Q})_{E}
.

(1)

Conjugation by
s_{\beta}
induces an equivalence of categories
s_{\beta}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w_{0}^{M}}})_{\nu\text{-}\mathrm{alg}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}})_{\nu\text{-}\mathrm{alg}}
.

(2)

For any
M\in{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w_{0}^{M}}})_{\nu\text{-}\mathrm{alg}}
, we have

HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\nu}(M)|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}}=HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\nu}(M),

HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\nu}(M)|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}}=HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\nu}(s_{\beta}M).

Proof.

The first point is obvious, and the second is immediate
from the definition of the functors
HCS
(i.e. from the
construction in the proof of Proposition
3.4.12
).
∎

3.6.4.
Singular localization

Write
j_{w_{0}^{M}}:C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\hookrightarrow C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
,
j_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}:C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}\hookrightarrow C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}
for the inclusions.
We take
\lambda=(1,1;w)\in X^{*}(T)
. Recall that
\rho=(-1,-2;0)
, so
that
\lambda+\rho=(0,-1;w)
is invariant under
s_{\beta}
. However, this character is not integral. Let us define
\eta=(0,-1;1)
which differs from
\lambda+\rho
by a character of the centre, and is still invariant under
s_{\beta}
.

Applying Proposition
3.6.3
with
\nu=\eta
, we have a short exact sequence of
(\mathfrak{g},Q)
-equivariant sheaves:

0\rightarrow(j_{w_{0}^{M}})_{!}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\rightarrow HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\rightarrow HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})\rightarrow 0

(3.6.5)

We want to study
\operatorname{Loc}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}(-\lambda))|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}}={\mathrm{RHom}}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2}}(\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})
.

Proposition 3.6.6
.

We have an exact triangle:

(j_{w_{0}^{M}})_{!}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\rightarrow Loc(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}(-\lambda))|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}}\rightarrow HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

Proof.

This is immediate from consequence of Theorem

3.5.11
together with Theorem
2.3.19

and Proposition
2.8.3
.
∎

There is a horizontal action
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}
of
Z({\mathfrak{m}})
on

\operatorname{Loc}(M_{\lambda}(-\lambda))|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}}
.
By Proposition
3.4.19
(see also
Remark
4.6.8
below), this action is via
\mu_{0}:=\langle-w_{0}\lambda,\mu\rangle
on both
HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})

and
HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})
(and in particular the action of
\mu
doesn’t split the triangle). Taking the derived invariants for
\mu-\mu_{0}
yields a triangle:

\displaystyle(j_{w_{0}^{M}})_{!}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\oplus(j_{w_{0}^{M}})_{!}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})[-1]\rightarrow

(3.6.7)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})\rightarrow

\displaystyle HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})[-1]\oplus HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})[-2]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

Taking the
H^{1}
yields the following short exact sequence of
(\mathfrak{g},B)
-equivariant sheaves:

0\rightarrow(j_{w_{0}^{M}})_{!}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})\rightarrow HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})\rightarrow 0

(3.6.8)

The main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 3.6.9
.

The two extensions of

(\mathfrak{g},B)
-equivariant sheaves (
3.6.8
) and (
3.6.5
) differ by a twist of the
B
-action by the character
\lambda-\eta
and multiplication by a scalar in
E^{\times}
.

Remark 3.6.10
.

This arguments in the remainder of this section admit a simpler analogue in the

\mathrm{GL}_{2}
-context; see
[
Pil24
, §6]
.

3.6.11.
Preparations for the proof of Theorem

3.6.9

We have commuting actions of
\mathfrak{b}
(via

*_{2}
) and
\mu
(via the horizontal action) on

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}
. We begin by isolating a certain sub-Lie
algebra of
\mathfrak{b}\oplus E\mu
whose cohomology on

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}}
is in degree
0
.

We use the usual standard basis elements
X_{\beta},X_{-\beta},H_{\beta}
with

H_{\beta}=[X_{\beta},X_{-\beta}]
. We set
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}:=\ker(\beta)
, so that
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology can be
obtained by first taking
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{Q}
-cohomology, and then
taking
EX_{\beta}\oplus EH_{\beta}
-cohomology. We write
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda|\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 3.6.12
.

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}:=\mathrm{Ext}_{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{Q},\mu}((\lambda^{\prime};\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})
is concentrated in degree
0
.

Proof.

We can do the computation separately on each of the
strata
C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}
and
C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
, so we reduce
to showing that the cohomology on the fibers at
w_{0}^{M}
and
w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}
vanishes in positive degrees. These fibers are
respectively
\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w_{0}^{M}}}\backslash G,e}
and

\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}}\backslash G,e}
, and the required vanishing
follows from a consideration of the actions of
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}

and
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}\cap\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{Q}
respectively.
(Note that
\mu=\operatorname{diag}(0,0,1,1)
, so that
w_{0}^{M}\mu=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,0,0)
and

s_{\beta}w_{0}^{M}\mu=\operatorname{diag}(0,1,0,1)
, and therefore
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\oplus E\cdot w_{0}^{M}\mu=\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\oplus E\cdot s_{\beta}w_{0}^{M}\mu=\mathfrak{h}
.)
∎

We thus see that

{\mathrm{RHom}}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})={\mathrm{RHom}}_{EX_{\beta}\oplus EH_{\beta}}(\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}})

(where the restriction of
\lambda
to
EX_{\beta}\oplus EH_{\beta}
takes
X_{\beta}\mapsto 0
). This cohomology is represented by the following Chevalley–Eilenberg complex

K^{\bullet}
(in degrees
0,1
and
2
):

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{pmatrix}X_{\beta}&H_{\beta}-\lambda(H_{\beta})\end{pmatrix}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}\otimes E(-\beta)\oplus\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta}-\lambda(H_{\beta})\\
-X_{\beta}\end{pmatrix}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}\otimes E(-\beta).

Since

HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})=\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}}),

we deduce that

s_{\beta}^{*}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(-\beta)=\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{s_{\beta}\mathfrak{b}}(s_{\beta}\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})\otimes E(-\beta)
. On this sheaf,

\mathfrak{h}
acts via
s_{\beta}\lambda-\beta=\lambda
(this is a crucial place where we use that the weight is singular), and
\mu
still acts via
\mu_{0}
. Thus, we can consider the map

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{s_{\beta}\mathfrak{b}}(s_{\beta}\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})\otimes E(-\beta)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle K^{1}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}\otimes E(-\beta)\oplus\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(s,0),

which induces a map

\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{s_{\beta}\mathfrak{b}}(s_{\beta}\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})\otimes E(-\beta)\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}).

(3.6.13)

We will show below that (
3.6.13
) is an isomorphism.
We begin by studying its restrictions to
C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
and to
C^{\dagger}_{{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}
; it is immediate from the definitions that the
former restriction is a map in
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta})
, and the latter is a map in
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})
.

Lemma 3.6.14
.

(1)

The restrictions of the left and right hand sides of (
3.6.13
) to
C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
are
isomorphic, and their endomorphism algebras in
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta})

are the scalars
E
.

(2)

The restrictions of the left and right hand sides of (
3.6.13
) to
C^{\dagger}_{{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}
are isomorphic, and their endomorphisms in
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})
are the scalars
E
.

Proof.

To begin, we note that it follows from the
Q
-equivariance of
HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})
that

s_{\beta}^{*}(HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(\lambda-\eta+\beta))=HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(\lambda-\eta).

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{s_{\beta}\mathfrak{b}}(s_{\beta}\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s_{\beta}^{*}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(-\beta)\ |_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\ |_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}.

On the other hand

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}=HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\ |_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}

(by
Proposition
3.6.3
and the proof of
Proposition
3.6.6
.)

We now check that the endomorphisms of the sheaf
HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\ |_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}}
in the category
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta})
are scalars.

Since
B\cap B_{s_{\beta}}
acts transitively on
C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
by section
3.6.1
,
an endomorphism is determined by its behavior at any fiber.
More precisely, let
x\in C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
. We consider the uniformization map

\displaystyle G_{e}\rtimes(B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}

\displaystyle(g,b)\mapsto xgb.

Let
\mathrm{Stab}(x)
be the stabilizer of
x
for this action. We have

\mathrm{Stab}(x)=\{(g,b),gb\in P_{x}\}
. Exactly as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.6
, this group is

(P_{x}\cap B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})_{e}\backslash[((P_{x})_{e}\times(B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})_{e})\rtimes(P_{x}\cap B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})].

The fiber we consider is the completion of a dual Verma module for

\mathfrak{m}_{x}
(by Definitions
3.4.17
and

4.6.6
), and the first part of the lemma follows from the
property that the endomorphisms of a Verma module are the scalars
(together with Theorem
2.2.39
).

The second part is proved in the same way, as follows. We first observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{0}_{s_{\beta}\mathfrak{b}}(s_{\beta}\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}})\otimes E(-\beta)|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}})

We reduce to showing that the endomorphisms of
HCS_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})_{s_{\beta}\lambda})
in the category
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})}(C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}})
are scalars which again follows from the property that endomorphisms of Verma modules are scalar.
∎

Proposition 3.6.15
.

The map (
3.6.13
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.6.14
it
suffices to show that (
3.6.13
) is nonzero on the
fiber at one point of
C_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}
and at one point of

C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
. It suffices in turn to prove that the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\beta}-\lambda(H_{\beta})}

induces an injective map on
the fibers at
w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}
and at
one point of
C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
.

By definition, the kernel of this map on the fiber at a
point
x
is

\Hom_{\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{Q},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}).

We first consider the fiber
at
x=w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}=\mathcal{O}_{U_{P_{{}^{2}w}}\backslash G,e}
. We have

\mathfrak{u}_{P_{w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}}}=EX_{\beta}\oplus EX_{-\alpha}\oplus EX_{-\delta},~~\mathfrak{u}_{Q}=EX_{\alpha}\oplus X_{\delta}\oplus X_{\gamma}.

For any
s\in\Phi
we write
U_{s}
for the corresponding

1-
parameter subgroup. We pick a coordinate
x_{s}
on
U_{s}
with
the property that the corresponding vector field is
X_{s}
. Then elements of
\Hom_{\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{Q},*_{2}}(\lambda,\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}_{x})

are germs of analytic functions on
U_{-\beta}U_{-\gamma}T
, which can
be written as

f(x_{-\beta},x_{-\gamma},t)=\sum_{k_{-\beta},k_{-\gamma}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t).

We need to show that if
(\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)-\mu_{0})f=0
, then
f=0
. By definition,
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)
acts on the left via the action of
-(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu\in\mathfrak{h}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu,\beta\rangle x_{-\beta}\partial_{x_{-\beta}}+\langle(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu,\gamma\rangle x_{-\gamma}\partial_{x_{-\gamma}}-\langle(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu,\lambda\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{-\beta}\partial_{x_{-\beta}}-\frac{w}{2}.

(We are using here that
(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu=(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2};\frac{1}{2})
,
\beta=(-2,0;0)
,
\gamma=(-1,-1;0)
,

\lambda=(1,1;w)
.) Thus
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)
acts by
k_{-\beta}-\frac{w}{2}
on
x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t)
. Since
\mu_{0}=-1-\frac{w}{2}
, we deduce that
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)-\mu_{0}
is injective.

We now consider the point
w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}g_{-\beta}\in C_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
, where
g_{-\beta}=\exp(X_{-\beta})
. We see that

(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta}g_{-\beta})^{-1}\mu=(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu+\langle(w_{0}^{M}s_{\beta})^{-1}\mu,\beta\rangle X_{-\beta}.

An element of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la},\lambda^{\prime},\mu_{0}}_{{}^{2}wg_{-\beta}}

can still be expressed as a germ of an analytic function on

U_{-\beta}U_{-\gamma}T
, and can thus be written as

f(x_{-\beta},x_{-\gamma},t)=\sum_{k_{-\beta},k_{-\gamma}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t).

We now find that
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)=x_{-\beta}\partial_{x_{-\beta}}-\frac{w}{2}+\partial_{x_{-\beta}}
, so that

\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t)=(k_{-\beta}-\frac{w}{2})x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t)+k_{-\beta}x_{-\beta}^{k_{-\beta}-1}x_{-\gamma}^{k_{-\gamma}}\lambda(t),

and
we again deduce that
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)-\mu_{0}
is injective.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.6.9
.

By Proposition
3.6.15
, the sheaf
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathfrak{b},*_{2};\mu}((\lambda,\mu_{0}),\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}}|_{C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M},Q}})
is
obtained by gluing
HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})
and
s_{\beta}^{*}HCS_{w_{0}^{M},\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(-\beta)
along
C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}\cap C^{\dagger}_{w_{0}^{M}}s_{\beta}
. The same is true of
HCS_{Q,w_{0}^{M},\eta}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w_{0}^{M}})_{\lambda})\otimes E(\lambda-\eta)
by construction. The gluing data is that of an isomorphism of

(\mathfrak{g},B\cap B_{s_{\beta}})
-equivariant sheaves, and by
Lemma
3.6.14
the space of
such isomorphisms identifies with
E^{\times}
, so there is (up to
isomorphism) a unique way to glue, as required.∎

4.
p
-adic Eichler–Shimura theory

4.1.
Introduction

The main goal of this section (as mentioned in §
1.4
)
is to relate higher Coleman
theory to completed cohomology, so that (ultimately) we can connect
the Galois-theoretic properties of a
p
-adic ordinary (overconvergent)
modular form (in terms of the action of the Sen operator) to
its classicality.

Before proceeding, we introduce some notation.
We fix a Hodge type Shimura datum
(G,X)
. We assume that
G_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
is quasi-split. Let
P
be the parabolic corresponding to
\mu
with Levi
M
. Let
B\subseteq G_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
be a Borel subgroup. We pick a maximal torus
T\subseteq B
. The relevant flag variety is
FL=P\backslash G
and we have the decomposition
FL=\coprod_{w\in{}^{M}W}P\backslash PwB
where
{}^{M}W\subseteq W
is the set of Kostant representatives in the absolute Weyl group. We also fix a coefficient field
E
which is a finite extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and admits a map from the reflex field of the Shimura datum.

4.1.1.
Higher Coleman theory

Higher Coleman theory
[
BP21
]
is a
theory of (higher) overconvergent modular forms. The different higher Coleman
theories are parameterized by two parameters: an element
w\in{}^{M}W
, and a weight.

Remark 4.1.2
.

We note that
{}^{M}W
parametrizes chambers in the weight space which are
M
-dominant, and the
w
-theory will interpolate those classical cohomologies whose weights belong to the
w
-chamber.

To describe the weight parameter, we fix
w\in{}^{M}W
. Let
\mathfrak{m}=\mathrm{Lie}(M)
, and let
\mathfrak{m}_{w}=w^{-1}\mathfrak{m}w
. Let
\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})
be the BGG category
{\mathcal{O}}
of
U(\mathfrak{m}_{w})
-modules for the Borel
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}}=\mathrm{Lie}(B)\cap\mathfrak{m}_{w}
. In the same way that weights of modular forms are finite dimensional representations of
M
, weights of (higher) overconvergent modular forms (parameterized by
w\in{}^{M}W
) are objects of
\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})
. For any
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)_{E}
, we let
\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
be the subcategory of
category
\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})
with weights in
\lambda+X^{*}(T)
.

We have higher Coleman functors (see Definition
4.6.35
):

HC_{w,\lambda},HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}:\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

(4.1.3)

where
\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)
is the category of locally analytic representations of
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
with
\mathfrak{b}
acting like
\lambda
. For example, if
\lambda=0
, this is just the category of smooth
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representations.

Remark 4.1.4
.

The parameter
\lambda
is there to specify the
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-action. If
\eta\in X^{*}(T)
, the categories

\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
and
\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda+\eta-\mathrm{alg}}
are canonically the same and one has
HC_{w,\lambda+\eta}(M)=HC_{w,\lambda}(M)\otimes E(\eta)
where
-\otimes E(\eta)
means a twist of the
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-action by
\eta
.

The functors (
4.1.3
) are defined by first attaching to every object
M
of

\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
a “quasi-coherent”
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant sheaf over the pullback of the Bruhat stratum
P\backslash PwB
via the Hodge–Tate period map and taking its cohomology with suitable support condition.

We also have a finite slope part functor
D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))
and we can speak of the finite slope part of higher Coleman functors (Section
4.6.54
):

HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,\lambda},HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}:\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)).

Remark 4.1.5
.

Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)^{+,M}
. Let
L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa}
be the finite dimensional representation of
\mathfrak{m}_{w}
of of highest weight
-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa
. We show that

HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})

is the direct sum of the
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
of
[
BP21
]
(Theorem
4.6.56
). These are higher Coleman theories with value in the classical sheaf of weight
\kappa
.
On the other hand, if
M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa}
denotes the Verma of highest weight
{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa}
, then

HC_{w,0}^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})

corresponds to higher Coleman theory with value in the big “induction” sheaf (Theorem
4.6.57
). The surjective map
M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa}\rightarrow L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa}
induces a
map

HC_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})\rightarrow HC_{w,\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})

and similarly on the finite slope part.
In summary, the improvements on
[
BP21
]
are the following:

•

We extend the definitions to the infinite slope part (in
[
BP21
]
, only the finite slope part was canonically defined).

•

We introduce a more functorial perspective on the weights. In

[
BP21
]
we allowed weights to be either finite dimensional
representations or Verma modules, which of course generate the BGG category.

Our main results on higher Coleman theory can be summarized as follows.

Theorem 4.1.6

(Theorems
4.6.45
,
4.6.58
, and
4.6.60
)
.

(1)

HC_{w,\lambda}
,
HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,\lambda}
have cohomological amplitude
[\ell(w),d]
and
HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}
,
HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}
have cohomological amplitude
[0,\ell(w)]
.

(2)

Let
M\in{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
be a module generated by a highest weight vector of weight
\nu
. Assume that the Shimura variety is proper or that we are in the Siegel case.
The slopes appearing in
HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}(M)
and
HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,\lambda}(M)
are
\geq\lambda-\nu+w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho+\rho
.

Remark 4.1.7
.

In the proper case, the functors
HC_{w,\lambda}
and
HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,\lambda}
are exact.

4.1.8.
Completed cohomology

We let
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
be completed cohomology and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
denote completed cohomology with compact support.

We let
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b})
be the BGG category
{\mathcal{O}}
for
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{b}
.
We define functors (Section
4.7
):

\displaystyle CC_{\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}})

\displaystyle CC_{\mathrm{cusp},\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M,\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}})

Remark 4.1.9
.

The first natural example is to apply these functors to
M
a finite dimensional representation of
G
, in which case we recover classical étale cohomology with weight
M^{\vee}
(it is natural to take
\lambda=0
).
For a non-classical example, we can take
M=U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{b})}\lambda
to be a Verma module of weight
\lambda
so that we are computing
\mathfrak{b}
-cohomology.

4.1.10.
p
-adic Eichler Shimura

We are now ready to state our main
result
comparing completed cohomology and higher Coleman theory.
It holds under a non-Liouville condition on
\lambda
(see Definition
2.3.25
). We observe that if
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
is algebraic, it is non-Liouville.

Theorem 4.1.11

(Theorem
4.7.1
)
.

Assume
\lambda
is non-Liouville and
M
is an object of
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
. We have a spectral sequence

E_{1}^{p,q}=\oplus_{w\in{}^{M}W,\ell(w)=p}H^{p+q}(HC_{w,\lambda}(M\otimes_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}}^{L}E))

converging to
H^{p+q}(CC_{\lambda}(M))\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{p}
. Moreover, the Sen operator is given by
w\mu\in Z({\mathfrak{m}_{w}})
acting on
H_{*}(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}},M)
.

Remark 4.1.12
.

The functor
-\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}})}E:D({\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b}))\rightarrow D({\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{m}_{w}}))
is the Lie algebra homology of the unipotent radical
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
of
\mathfrak{p}_{w}=w^{-1}\mathrm{Lie}(P)w
.
It is computed by the Koszul complex (in degree
-d
to
0
):

0\rightarrow M\otimes\Lambda^{d}\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0

Remark 4.1.13
.

In Section
3.5
we attached to
M\in{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{b})
a certain twisted
D
-module
\operatorname{Loc}(M)
on the flag variety. This is a version of Beilinson–Bernstein localization. This twisted
D
-module completely encodes the
p
-adic Eichler–Shimura theory (see Theorem
4.7.1
for a precise statement). We observe that
\operatorname{Loc}(M)
is “constant” on each Bruhat stratum
C_{w}
and its restriction to each
C_{w}
is determined (in the non-Liouville case) by the Lie algebra homology
M\otimes_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}}^{L}E
.

Under favorable “genericity” assumptions, the spectral sequence simplifies a lot. Let us denote by
M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}=U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{b})}\lambda
the Verma module of weight
\lambda
. We adopt a similar notation to denote Verma modules for other reductive Lie algebras.

Corollary 4.1.14

(Corollary
4.7.3
)
.

Assume that
\lambda
is non-Liouville and antidominant in the sense of
Remark
3.5.21
, and that the Shimura variety is proper. Then
CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda})
is concentrated in the middle degree
d
and moreover, it has a decreasing filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}\mathrm{H}^{d}(CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}))
with

•

\mathrm{Fil}^{d+1}\mathrm{H}^{d}(CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}))=0
,

•

\mathrm{Fil}^{0}\mathrm{H}^{d}(CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}))=\mathrm{H}^{d}(CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}))
,

•

\mathrm{Gr}^{p}\mathrm{H}^{d}(CC_{\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{g})_{\lambda}))=\oplus_{w\in{}^{M}W,\ell(w)=p}H^{p}(HC_{w,\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{\lambda+w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho+\rho}))
.

We also refer to Theorem
4.7.5
for a similar result in the ordinary case.

4.2.
Perfectoid Shimura varieties

We consider a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G,X)
. We also fix a map from the reflex field of the Shimura datum to

E
. We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{rat}
be the analytic space over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
attached to the Shimura variety of level
K_{p}K^{p}
over
\Spec~E
. We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{rat}\times_{\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})}\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{p}})
.
We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
be a toroidal compactification of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{rat}
over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
for a specific choice of cone decomposition
\Sigma
(see
[
FC90
]
,
[
Lan13
]
). For
K_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{p}
, we have a natural map
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
.
We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}=\lim_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
. By
[
Sch15
]
(and
[
PS16a
]
,
[
Lan22
]
for
the extension to the compactification), this is a perfectoid space. We note that the same cone
decomposition
\Sigma
is used at each stage of the limit and that there is some restriction on the choice of cone decomposition if we are not in the Siegel case.
Concretely, the underlying topological space of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
is the inverse limit of the topological spaces of the
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
, and there is a basis of affinoid opens
\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
, which are pull backs of affinoid opens
\mathrm{Spa}(A_{K^{\prime}_{p}},A_{K^{\prime}_{p}}^{+})
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
for small enough
K^{\prime}_{p}
, and such that
A^{+}
is the
p
-adic completion of
\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}A_{K^{\prime}_{p}}^{+}
.

We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{rat}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
be the open subspace
\lim_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p}}^{rat}
.
We let

\pi^{rat}_{HT,\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow\mathcal{FL}^{rat}
be
the Hodge–Tate period map, where
\mathcal{FL}^{rat}
is the Flag
variety over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
.

We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}
be the base change to
\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{p}})
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}
(which therefore
carries an action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
). We similarly define

\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}
and
\mathcal{FL}
, and the period map

\pi_{HT,\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow\mathcal{FL}

which is
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
-equivariant.

The period map is also
K_{p}
-equivariant.
The action of
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}
does not extend to an action on

\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}
but for a general
g\in G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
we still have diagrams:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},g\Sigma}^{\mathrm{tor}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{FL}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{FL}}

Remark 4.2.1
.

The choice of a specific
\Sigma
does not usually play any role. If no confusion is likely to arise, we fix a
\Sigma
and drop it from the notation. We will eventually allow ourselves to change
\Sigma
. It is also important to note that all the cohomologies we will consider (coherent cohomology, completed cohomology) do not depend on
\Sigma
.

4.3.
Smooth and locally analytic vectors of the structure sheaf

We let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
be the structure sheaf of the
perfectoid space. If
U\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
is a quasi-compact
open, then
U
is stabilized by an open subgroup
K_{p}
of
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
and we get a continuous action of
K_{p}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}(U)
. One can speak of the smooth and locally analytic vectors of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}(U)
. We thus obtain subsheaves of smooth and locally analytic vectors:

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

For any compact open subgroup
K^{\prime}_{p}\subseteq G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, let
\pi_{K^{\prime}_{p}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
be the natural map. The pullback map
\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\pi_{K^{\prime}_{p}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We consider the map of sites
\nu:(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}})_{\operatorname{prok\'{e}t}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}})_{\mathrm{ket}}
from the pro-Kummer-étale site to the
Kummer-étale site. It follows from
[
Sch13b
, Coro. 6.19]
(which is
easily extended to the Kummer-étale case via the machinery of

[
DLLZ23
]
) that
\nu_{*}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
. We are going to see that the proposition follows directly from this statement.
We consider the pro-Kummer étale cover
\pi_{K_{p}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
. Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
is perfectoid, it
follows from
[
DLLZ23
, Thm. 5.4.3]
that for any open affine

U\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
,
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}(U)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}(U)
. Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}=K_{p}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
, we deduce that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=\nu_{*}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=H^{0}(K_{p},\pi_{K_{p},*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{tor}})
. The proposition
follows by taking the colimit over
K_{p}
.
∎

We let
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
be the ideal of the (reduced) boundary
D_{K_{p}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
. This is an invertible ideal in
\mathscr{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
that we also denote by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}(-D_{K_{p}})
.
We let
\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
.

We let
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
be the ideal of the boundary
in the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
. Its subsheaf of
locally analytic vectors is denoted by

\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}^{\mathrm{la}}
and turns out to be equal to

\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}}\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
by the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

The natural map:
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}}\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}^{\mathrm{la}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
[
RC23
, Thm. 3.4.1.]
.∎

4.4.
Completed cohomology

Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{alg}
denote the Shimura variety, viewed as a scheme, defined over its reflex field
E(G,X)
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{alg}=\lim_{K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{alg}
. The limit exists as a scheme since the transition maps are affine. We define completed cohomology with
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
coefficients to be
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{proet}}(\mathrm{Sh}^{alg}_{K^{p},\bar{{\mathbf{Q}}}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. The cohomology groups of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{proet}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p},\bar{{\mathbf{Q}}}}^{alg},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
identify with the usual completed
cohomology groups with
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-coefficients (as defined
for example in
[
CE12
]
). This cohomology has a
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-action, an action of the Hecke algebra away from
p
, and an action of
G_{E(G,X)}
, the absolute Galois group of
E(G,X)
.

Using comparison theorems in
[
Hub96
, page 30]
and
[
RC22
, Cor. 6.1.7]
,
completed cohomology with
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-coefficients identifies with
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{prok\'{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. Here the subscript
\operatorname{prok\'{e}t}
is
is short for “pro-Kummer-étale”, in the sense
of
[
DLLZ23
]
; this modification of the
pro-étale site is needed because our Shimura varieties are not
compact. We usually omit this subscript from now on. Similarly, we identify
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
with the completed
cohomology with compact support.

We let
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}
be the
(derived) locally analytic vectors in completed cohomology. Similarly, we let
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}
be the (derived) locally analytic vectors in completed cohomology with compact support.
We remark that both
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
and

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
have admissible
cohomology groups, and that the passage to locally analytic vectors
is an exact functor on admissible representations by
[
ST03
]
,
Thm. 7.1 (see also
[
RJRC22
, Prop. 4.48]
). Therefore, the cohomology groups of
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}
are the locally analytic vectors in the completed cohomology groups.

Theorem 4.4.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}){\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}){\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})

Proof.

The first part is immediate from
[
DLLZ23
, Thm. 6.2.1]
, by
passing to the limit as in the proof of
[
Sch15
, Thm. 4.2.1]
. The second part is
[
RC22
, Thm. 6.2.6]
.
∎

Remark 4.4.2
.

The main ideas in the proof of
Theorem
4.4.1
are due to Scholze and Pan. More
precisely, the statements regarding completed cohomology (before taking locally
analytic vectors) are a consequence of Scholze’s primitive
comparison theorem; see e.g.
[
Sch15
, Thm. 4.2.1]
. For the locally analytic vectors, in the case of usual (i.e. not compactly supported) cohomology it
is a consequence of the fact that

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{Rla}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}

where
\mathrm{Rla}
are the derived locally analytic vectors (in the sense
of
[
RJRC22
]
). This was proved for modular curves in

[
Pan22a
]
, Thm. 4.4.6. In
loc. cit.

it is also proved for modular curves that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathcal{FL},(\pi_{HT})_{*}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})
. We do not need (and have not proved) this
fact more generally.

4.5.
The functor
VB

We now introduce a functor which turns equivariant sheaves on the flag variety into sheaves on the perfectoid Shimura variety.

4.5.1.
Definition of the functor and main properties

Let us briefly reintroduce
\Sigma
to the notation (see Remark
4.2.1
).
Let
U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}}
be a quasi-compact open subset of
\mathcal{FL}^{rat}

and let
U_{\mathcal{FL}}
be its base change to
\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{p}})
. We
write
U_{K^{p},\Sigma}:=\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})=\lim_{K^{\prime}_{p}}U_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}
where
U_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}
is a
quasi-compact open subset of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}
for
K^{\prime}_{p}
small
enough. In Definition
3.2.6
(see also Remark
3.2.29
) we have defined the categories
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
and

LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
and there is a base change functor

LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})\rightarrow LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
. We define a functor

\displaystyle VB^{0}_{\Sigma}:LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}})

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}((\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}})^{K^{\prime}_{p}}.

Remark 4.5.2
.

Note that this functor is (well-) defined, because for any quasi-compact open
subset
V_{\mathcal{FL}}\subseteq U_{\mathcal{FL}}
, we have

\mathscr{F}(V_{\mathcal{FL}})=\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\mathscr{F}(V_{\mathcal{FL}})_{K^{\prime}_{p}}
where

\mathscr{F}(V_{\mathcal{FL}})_{K^{\prime}_{p}}
is a submodule of

\mathscr{F}(V_{\mathcal{FL}})
where the action of
\mathfrak{g}
integrates
to an action of
K^{\prime}_{p}
(compatibly for the transition maps).

We also need a derived version of this functor. We recall that in Definition

3.2.4
we introduced abelian categories

\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
and

\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
which respectively contain
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
and
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
. Moreover there is a base change functor
\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})\rightarrow\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
. We define a functor

\displaystyle VB_{\Sigma}:D(\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}))

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}_{p}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(K_{p}^{\prime},(\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathscr{F}){\otimes}^{L}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}).

Remark 4.5.3
.

The pullback functor

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathscr{F}){\otimes}^{L}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}

in the definition of
VB_{\Sigma}
is exact on the category

LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
(essentially by the definition of

LB
-sheaves). Thus on
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
,
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}

is left exact, and we may think of
VB_{\Sigma}
as its right derived functor.

It might be more natural to denote this pullback by
\pi^{*}_{\mathrm{HT},\Sigma}
, but
we reserve this notation below for the underived pullback

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}.

Since the Hodge–Tate period map is
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
-equivariant, if
\mathscr{F}
is an object of
\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
which comes from
\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
by base change, then
VB_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})
carries a semi-linear
action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
.
One can study this action as follows. We can consider the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}
-modules, carrying a semi-linear continuous Galois action.
We let
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm},\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)-\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}
be the
subsheaf of smooth vectors for the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
. We
remark that

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm},\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)-\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{rat,\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}\otimes_{E}\bar{E}
.

We define an arithmetic Sen functor:

\displaystyle S^{\operatorname{arit}}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm},\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)-\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{E^{\prime}}\mathscr{F}^{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}),\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})-an}

where the colimit goes over all finite extensions
E^{\prime}/E
and the superscript

(-)^{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}),\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})-an}

means the
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}})
-fixed and
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})
-analytic vectors (where
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})
is viewed as a subgroup of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
via the cyclotomic character).
We observe that
S^{\operatorname{arit}}(\mathscr{F})
carries an
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm},\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)-\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}
-linear arithmetic Sen
operator, obtained by differentiating the
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})
-action on

(\mathscr{F})^{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}),\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})-an}
and passing to the colimit.

The following theorem is implicit in
[
Pan22a
]
in the modular curve
case, see
[
Pil24
]
for a formulation in this spirit. In higher
dimension it is essentially a direct consequence of the results

[
RC22
,
RC23
]
, as we will see in
the course of the proof.

Theorem 4.5.4
.

(1)

For any
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
,
there exists a covering by open affinoids
U_{K^{p},\Sigma}=\cup_{i}V_{i}

with
V_{i}=\lim_{K_{p}}V_{i,K_{p}}
(for
K_{p}
small enough; here the
V_{i,K_{p}}
are open affinoid) a
sequence of compact open subgroups
\{K_{p,r}\}_{r\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
and summand of orthonormalizable Banach sheaves
VB^{0}_{\Sigma,K_{p,r},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})
over
V_{i,K_{p,r}}
such that we have:

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})|_{V_{i}}=\operatorname{colim}_{r}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}^{\mathrm{sm}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,K_{p,r}}}}VB^{0}_{\Sigma,K_{p,r},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F}).

Moreover, we have

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}=(\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathcal{FL}}})}\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}.

(4.5.5)

(2)

The restriction of the functor
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
to the category

LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
is an exact functor, and for an object
\mathscr{F}
of
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
we have

VB_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})=VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\oplus_{i=0}^{d}\Omega^{i}_{U_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}}(\mathrm{log}(D_{K_{p},\Sigma}))[-i]).

(3)

Let
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
. Assume that for all
i
, we have
H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},\mathscr{F})\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
.
Then we have isomorphisms:

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},\mathscr{F}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{i}(VB_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F}))
.

(4)

Let
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. Assume that
\mathscr{F}
arises from an object of
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
. Then we have

S^{\operatorname{arit}}(VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm},\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)-\mathrm{sm}}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}}\mathcal{O}^{sm}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}=VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})

and the action of
\mu
via
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}
is an arithmetic Sen
operator on
S^{\operatorname{arit}}(VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F}))
.

More precisely, in the notation of
(1)
, we can suppose that the covering
U_{K^{p},\Sigma}=\cup_{i}V_{i}
comes from a covering
U^{rat}_{K^{p},\Sigma}=\cup_{i}V^{rat}_{i}
, and there exists a finite extension
E_{i,n}
of
E
such that

VB^{0}_{\Sigma,K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})=VB^{0}_{\Sigma,K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})^{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E_{i,n,cycl}),\mathrm{Gal}(E_{i,n,cycl}/E_{i,n})-an}\otimes_{E_{i,n}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}.

Proof.

We explain why the theorem follows from the results of
[
RC22
]
and
[
RC23
]
. The statement is local so we can assume that
\mathscr{F}=\operatorname{colim}\mathscr{F}_{r}
is a colimit of orthonormalizable Banach sheaves with injective
transition maps and that
G_{r}
acts on each
\mathscr{F}_{r}
. We can assume that

U_{\mathcal{FL}}=\mathrm{Spa}(C,C^{+})
is affinoid. We can also consider

V_{K^{p},\Sigma}=\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})
an open affinoid subset of

U_{K^{p},\Sigma}
. The open subset
V_{K^{p},\Sigma}
descends to
V_{K^{p}K_{p},\Sigma}=\mathrm{Spa}(B_{K_{p}},B_{K_{p}}^{+})
for
K_{p}
small enough.

For
K_{p}
small enough (such that
K_{p}\subseteq G_{r}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
), the pull
back
\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{r}
to
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}
carries a
K_{p}
-action
Let us put
F_{r}:=\mathscr{F}_{r}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
. We pick a
C^{+}
lattice
F_{r}^{+}
(that is,
F_{r}^{+}
is the completion of a free
C^{+}
-module and
F_{r}^{+}\otimes_{C^{+}}C=F_{r}
). The action of
G_{r}
amounts to a co-action map
c:F_{r}\rightarrow F_{r}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{G_{r}}
. By continuity, there exists
n
such that
c(F_{r}^{+})\subseteq p^{-n}F_{r}^{+}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{+}_{G_{r}}
.
We claim that for
r^{\prime}=r+n+1
the restriction of the co-action
map
c^{\prime}:F_{r}\rightarrow F_{r}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{G_{r^{\prime}}}
induces a
map
F^{+}_{r}\rightarrow F^{+}_{r}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{G_{r^{\prime}}}
and
moreover, this co-action map is trivial modulo
p
. To see this, we may write (for example by using the exponential map)
\mathcal{O}_{G_{r}}={\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{t}\rangle

so that
\mathcal{O}_{G_{r^{\prime}}}={\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle p^{-n-1}X_{1},\cdots,p^{-n-1}X_{t}\rangle
. For any
f\in F_{r}^{+}
, we write
c(f)=\sum_{\underline{i}}f_{\underline{i}}X^{\underline{i}}
, where
f_{\underline{0}}=f
and
f_{\underline{i}}\in p^{-n}F_{r}^{+}
is tending to
0
. Our claim is thus clear. By shrinking
K_{p}
, we can assume that
K_{p}\subseteq G_{r^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
.
We remark that we have in particular checked that the action of
K_{p}
is “locally analytic” in the sense of
[
RC23
, Defn. 1.0.1]
; more precisely, the pro-Kummer-étale

\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{V_{K^{p}K_{p},\Sigma}}
-module corresponding to
\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{r}
is
relatively analytic ON Banach in the sense of
[
RC23
, Defn. 1.0.1]
. Note that this is the familiar smallness
condition in
p
-adic Simpson theory (see
[
RC23
, Rem. 1.0.1]
).

We let:

•

\mathbf{T}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T,T^{-1}\rangle,{\mathbf{C}}_{p}^{+}\langle T,T^{-1}\rangle)
,

•

\mathbf{T}_{n}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T^{p^{-n}},T^{-p^{-n}}\rangle,{\mathbf{C}}_{p}^{+}\langle T^{p^{-n}},T^{-p^{-n}}\rangle)
for any
n\geq 1
.

•

\mathbf{D}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T\rangle,{\mathbf{C}}_{p}^{+}\langle T\rangle)
,

•

\mathbf{D}_{n}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T^{p^{-n}}\rangle,{\mathbf{C}}_{p}^{+}\langle T^{p^{-n}}\rangle)
for any
n\geq 1
.

We can also assume (after shrinking
V_{K^{p},\Sigma}
and taking
K_{p}
small
enough) that we have a toric chart (in the sense of
[
DLLZ23
, Prop. 3.1.10]
)
V_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}^{e}\times\mathbf{D}^{d-e}
.
We let
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{K_{p},n},B_{K_{p},n}^{+})=U_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}\times_{\mathbf{T}^{e}\times\mathbf{D}^{d-e}}\mathbf{T}^{e}_{n}\times\mathbf{D}^{d-e}_{n}
. We let
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{K_{p},\infty},B_{K_{p},\infty}^{+})=\lim_{n}\mathrm{Spa}(B_{K_{p},n},B_{K_{p},n}^{+})
. We let
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{n},B_{n}^{+})=U_{K^{p},\Sigma}\times_{\mathbf{T}^{e}\times\mathbf{D}^{d-e}}\mathbf{T}^{e}_{n}\times\mathbf{D}^{d-e}_{n}
. We let
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{\infty},B_{\infty}^{+})=\lim_{n}\mathrm{Spa}(B_{n},B_{n}^{+})
.
After making a choice of compatible
p
-power roots of unity, We let
\Gamma={\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{d}
, acting on
\mathbf{T}_{n}^{e}\times\mathbf{D}_{n}^{d-e}
. We thus have an action of
K_{p}\times\Gamma
on

B_{\infty}
. By
[
RC23
, Prop. 3.2.3]
, the triple
(B_{\infty},K_{p}\times\Gamma,pr_{2}:K_{p}\times\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma)
is a strongly decomposable Sen theory in the sense of
[
RC23
, Defn. 2.2.6]
.

We consider the semi-linear representation of
K_{p}
,
B\otimes_{C}F_{r}
. Our
goal is to compute
\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}H^{i}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})
using Sen
theory. By almost purity, we have

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(K_{p}\times\Gamma,B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F_{r})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\Gamma,H^{0}(K_{p},B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F_{r})).

(4.5.6)

By
[
RC23
, Thm. 2.4.3]
, we have (after possibly shrinking

K_{p}
and for all
n
large enough) the Sen module

S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}):=(B\otimes_{C}F_{r})^{K_{p},p^{n}\Gamma-an}

(4.5.7)

which is obtained by taking the
K_{p}
-invariants and
the
p^{n}\Gamma
-analytic vectors. This is an orthonormalizable Banach

B_{K_{p},n}
-module with a locally analytic action of
\Gamma
, and it satisfies

B_{\infty}\otimes_{B_{K_{p},n}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})=B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F_{r}.

(4.5.8)

In addition
by (
4.5.6
) we have

H^{0}(\Gamma,S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))=H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r}).

(4.5.9)

We have an action of
\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma)
on
S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
, which are the
“geometric” Sen operators, and by
[
RJRC22
, Thm. 1.7]
we have

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\Gamma,H^{0}(K_{p},B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F_{r}))=H^{0}(\Gamma,\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})))

(4.5.10)

where
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))
is a complex of
smooth
\Gamma
-modules and
H^{0}(\Gamma,-)
is the exact functor of

\Gamma
-invariants on smooth
\Gamma
-modules.
It is a consequence of
[
RC22
, Thm. 1.1.5]
that these Sen
operators are induced by functoriality from the map
\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}\otimes\mathscr{F}_{r}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{r}
, using the identification

\pi_{HT}^{*}\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}\simeq\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{V_{K^{p},\Sigma}}
.

Since the transition maps in the colimit
{\mathcal{F}}=\operatorname{colim}{\mathcal{F}}_{r}
are injective, it
follows in particular that
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
if and only if the geometric Sen operator of each
\mathscr{F}_{r}
is trivial.
Let us assume that this is the case.
Then the action of
\Gamma
on
S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
is smooth, and this action
factors through
\Gamma/p^{n}\Gamma
. By finite étale descent, we find that

B_{K_{p},n}\otimes_{B_{K_{p}}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})^{\Gamma}=S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})

(4.5.11)

and that

S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})^{\Gamma}
is a direct summand of the orthonormalizable Banach

B_{K_{p}}
-module
S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
. Taking
p^{n}\Gamma
-invariants
in (
4.5.8
), we obtain

B_{n}\otimes_{B_{K_{p},n}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})=B_{n}\otimes_{C}F_{r}

and thus
(using (
4.5.11
))

B_{n}\otimes_{B_{K_{p}}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})^{\Gamma}=B_{n}\otimes_{C}F_{r}.

Taking
\Gamma
-invariants and
using (
4.5.9
),
we deduce that

B\otimes_{B_{K_{p}}}H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})=B\otimes_{C}F_{r}.

(4.5.12)

Passing to the colimit over
r
and
K_{p}
, we obtain (
4.5.5
). This completes the
proof of (1) (taking
VB^{0}_{\Sigma,K_{p,r},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})
to be the
sheaf associated to

H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})
, and
K_{p,r}
to be
K_{p}
).

We now prove (2). We have

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))=\oplus S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})\otimes\Lambda^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma))^{\vee}[-i]

from which we deduce that

VB_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})=VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\oplus_{i=0}^{d}\Omega^{i}_{U_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}}(\mathrm{log}(D_{K_{p},\Sigma}))[-i]).

(4.5.13)

Let
0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{L}\rightarrow\mathscr{H}\rightarrow 0
be an exact sequence in
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. Applying
VB_{\Sigma}
yields exact sequences:

0\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{L})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{H})

and

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\mathrm{sm}}}VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\Lambda^{d}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0})^{\vee})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\mathrm{sm}}}VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\Lambda^{d}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0})^{\vee})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{H})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\mathrm{sm}}}VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\Lambda^{d}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0})^{\vee})\rightarrow 0.

Since
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\Lambda^{d}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0})^{\vee})

is an invertible sheaf, we conclude that
0\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{L})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{H})\rightarrow 0
is exact, as required.

We now turn to (3), so we no longer assume that
\mathscr{F}
is killed by

\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}
. We let
S_{K_{p}}(F_{r})=\operatorname{colim}_{n}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
, we let

S(F_{r})=\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}S_{K_{p}}(F_{r})
and finally we let
S(F)=\operatorname{colim}_{r}S(F_{r})
.
We claim that

H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S(F))=S(H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F)).

(4.5.14)

Granting (
4.5.14
), we claim that taking

\Gamma
-invariants gives

\operatorname{colim}_{r,K_{p}}H^{i}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})=\operatorname{colim}_{r,K_{p}}H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F_{r})),

(4.5.15)

which immediately gives (3). Indeed,
by
4.5.10
and (
4.5.6
) we have

H^{0}(\Gamma,H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})))=H^{i}(\Gamma,H^{0}(K_{p},B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F_{r}))=H^{i}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r}).

On the other hand, passing to colimits
in (
4.5.9
) we see that

\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}F_{r})=H^{0}(\Gamma,S(F_{r})),

(4.5.16)

so that (replacing
F_{r}
by
H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F_{r})
)

H^{0}(\Gamma,S(H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F)))=\operatorname{colim}_{r,K_{p}}H^{0}(K_{p},B\otimes_{C}H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F_{r})),

as required.

We now establish (
4.5.14
). Firstly, we claim that

B_{K_{p},\infty}
is an orthonormalizable
B_{K_{p},n}
-module. Indeed the algebra
of
\mathbf{T}_{\infty}=\lim_{n}\mathbf{T}_{n}
has a topological basis
\{T^{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}}
over
{\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T,T^{-1}\rangle
, and similarly the algebra of
\mathbf{D}_{\infty}\lim_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}
has a topological basis
\{T^{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}}
over
{\mathbf{C}}_{p}\langle T\rangle
.
We next claim that
B_{\infty}
is a direct summand of an orthonormalizable

B_{K_{p},\infty}
-module. To see this,
let us fix a decreasing sequence of compact open subgroups
\{K_{p,r}\}_{r\geq 0}
tending to
\{e\}
with
K_{p,0}=K_{p}
. Since
B^{+}_{K_{p,r},\infty}\rightarrow B^{+}_{K_{p,r+1},\infty}
is almost étale, there exist finite

B^{+}_{K_{p,r},\infty}
modules
X_{K_{p,r+1}}
and
Y_{K_{p,r+1}}
together
with:

•

an injective map
B^{+}_{K_{p,r},\infty}\oplus X_{K_{p,r+1}}\rightarrow B^{+}_{K_{p,r+1},\infty}
whose cokernel is annihilated by
p
, and

•

an integer
n_{r}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and an injective map
X_{K_{p,r+1}}\oplus Y_{K_{p,r+1}}\rightarrow(B^{+}_{K_{p},r,\infty})^{n_{r}}
whose cokernel is annihilated by
p
.

We deduce that
B_{\infty}\bigoplus(\widehat{\oplus_{r}Y_{K_{p,r+1}}}[1/p])

is orthonormalizable over
B_{K_{p},\infty}
, as required.

In particular, we have shown that
B_{\infty}
is flat over
B_{K_{p},n}
.
We deduce that
H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),B_{\infty}\otimes_{B_{K_{p},n}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))=H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),B_{\infty}\otimes_{B_{K_{p},n}}S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))
, and passing to the colimit over
K_{p},n,r
we obtain that (for
B_{\infty}^{\mathrm{sm}}
the subring of smooth vectors for the
K_{p}\times\Gamma
-action):

H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),B_{\infty}\otimes_{B^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\infty}}S(F))=B_{\infty}\otimes_{B^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\infty}}H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S(F)).

On the other hand,
H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}F)=B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F)
since all the
H^{j}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F)
are flat over
C
(here we use our assumption that
H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},\mathscr{F})\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})
).
Recalling (
4.5.8
), we deduce that we have
K_{p}\times\Gamma
- equivariant isomorphisms:

B_{\infty}\otimes_{B^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\infty}}H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S(F))=B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F)=B_{\infty}\otimes_{B^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\infty}}S(H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F))

Taking
K_{p}\times\Gamma
smooth vectors yields
H^{i}(\mathrm{Lie}(\Gamma),S(F))=S(H^{i}(\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0},F))
, as required.

We now prove the last point. We take
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, arising from an
object of
LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U^{rat}_{\mathcal{FL}})
. We can therefore choose the
{\mathcal{F}}_{r}
to be defined over
E
, so that there is a semi-linear
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
-action on
B\otimes_{C}F_{r}
. Moreover, taking a topological basis
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
F_{r}
defined over
E
, we see that
the Galois action is trivial in this basis. Assuming that
K_{p}
acts trivially
modulo
p^{2}
on
F_{r}^{+}
(which we can always arrange after shrinking
K_{p}
), we
deduce that
S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
has a topological basis
\{v^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
where
the change of basis matrix (for the isomorphism (
4.5.8
)) from
\{v_{i}\}
to
\{v^{\prime}_{i}\}
is congruent to
1

modulo
p
(see
[
RC23
, Thm. 2.4.3, (1),
(b)]
). As a result, the
matrix of the Galois action on
S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
is congruent to
1
modulo

p
. One can therefore apply Sen theory to the extension
B^{rat}_{K_{p},n}\rightarrow B_{K_{p},n}
where
B_{K_{p},n}^{rat}=(B_{K_{p},n})^{\mathrm{Gal}{\bar{E}/E(\zeta_{p^{n}})}}
. We let
S^{\operatorname{arit},s}(S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))=S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})^{G_{L^{\operatorname{cycl}}},G_{L(\zeta_{p^{s}})}-an}
. For
s
large enough,
S^{\operatorname{arit},s}(S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r}))\otimes_{B^{rat}_{K_{p},n}(\zeta_{p^{s}})}B_{K_{p},n}=S_{K_{p},n}(F_{r})
and the
derivative of the
\mathrm{Gal}(E_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E(\zeta_{p^{s}}))
action provides an
arithmetic Sen operator.

In order to prove (4), it only remains to identify this Sen operator with the operator
\mu
coming from the horizontal action.
The orbit map provides an embedding
F_{r}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})
,
f\mapsto[k\mapsto k.f]
. It intertwines the action of
K_{p}
on
F_{r}
with the action of
K_{p}
on functions
h(-)\in\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})
via
k*_{2}h(-)=h(-k)
(therefore the
*_{2}
action does not depend on the
K_{p}
-action on
F_{r}
). Note that
\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})
is a
C
-module as there is a orbit map
C\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},C)
and
\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},C)
acts on
\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})
naturally.
Moreover, the embedding
F_{r}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})
factors through

\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. It therefore suffices to identify the arithmetic Sen operator of
\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},F_{r})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. Since
F_{r}
has a topological basis over
C
we can reduce to understanding the Sen operator of
\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
.
The orbit map
C\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},C)
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},C)
. Moreover, the subspace of algebraic functions

\mathcal{C}^{alg}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
is
dense, and it induces a dense map
(\mathcal{C}^{alg}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. Viewing the arithmetic Sen operator as an endomorphism of

B_{\infty}\otimes_{B_{K_{p},n}}S_{K_{p},n}(\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}})=B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}(\mathcal{C}^{an}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}

we deduce that it suffices to prove it coincides with
\mu
on
B_{\infty}\otimes_{C}(\mathcal{C}^{alg}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}C)^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
. Since
\mathcal{C}^{alg}(K_{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})=\oplus_{\kappa\in X^{*}(T)^{+}}V_{\kappa}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{\vee}
and

(V_{\kappa}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}=\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}V^{\vee}_{\kappa}
we deduce that it suffices to understand the Sen operator of the
classical automorphic vector bundles. It therefore suffices to show that

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{L}_{\kappa})=\omega^{\kappa,sm}(\kappa(\mu))
, where

\kappa(\mu)
is the Tate twist and
\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}=\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}}
is the colimit of the automorphic vector bundles defined
over
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}K_{p}}
(equipped with their rational structure). This
follows from an inspection of the rationality properties of the Hodge–Tate map
and of the universal
M
-torsor, see

[
RC22
, Thm. 4.2.1]
. ∎

Observe that by (
4.5.13
)

VB_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathcal{FL}}})=\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\oplus_{i=0}^{d}\Omega^{i}_{U_{K_{p}K^{p},\Sigma}}(\mathrm{log}(D_{K_{p},\Sigma}))[-i]

is a DG
algebra and admits an augmentation map to

\mathcal{O}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}^{\mathrm{sm}}[0]
. We can therefore make the following definition.

Definition 4.5.17
.

We let
VB^{\operatorname{red}}_{\Sigma}:D(\Mod_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}}))\to D(\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{U_{K^{p},\Sigma}}))
be the functor given by

VB^{\operatorname{red}}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F}):=VB_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\otimes^{L}_{VB_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathcal{FL}}})}\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\mathrm{sm}}[0].

Remark 4.5.18
.

By Theorem
4.5.4
(
2
), if
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, then

VB^{\operatorname{red}}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})=VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})[0]
. Consequently
given a complex of objects in

LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(U_{\mathcal{FL}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, we
can evaluate
VB^{\operatorname{red}}_{\Sigma}
by applying
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
termwise.

4.5.19.
Variants

We now introduce variants of the above
functor carrying extra structure. Let us define
\mathrm{Mod}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
to be the category of sheaves
(\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma})_{\Sigma}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}}
-modules with the properties:

(1)

For any refinement
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
, inducing a map
\pi_{\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}
, we have an isomorphism
\pi_{\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma^{\prime}}}
-modules (and these isomorphisms are compatible).

(2)

For any
g\in G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, inducing an isomorphism
g:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},g\Sigma}
, there is an isomorphism
g^{*}\mathscr{F}_{g\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p},\Sigma}}
-modules (and they satisfy the usual cocycle condition).

Then we have a functor

VB^{0}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}),

constructed
as follows. Composing the functor
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}

with the forgetful functor
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})\to LB_{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathcal{FL})
gives a functor
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})\to\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
. For each
g\in G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, there is a map
g:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},g\Sigma}
and a map
g^{*}VB^{0}_{g\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})
satisfying the usual cocycle condition.
The various
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
thus define a functor
VB^{0}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
as claimed. Note that
in practice, we fix some
\Sigma
and really work with the functor

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
(but see Remark
4.2.1
for our notational convention).

We recall the stratification into
B
-orbits
\mathcal{FL}=\coprod_{w\in{}^{M}W}C_{w}
, with
C_{w}=P\backslash P{w}B
.
We let
j_{w}:C_{w}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{FL}
be the locally closed immersion. It induces
j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}}:\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(C_{w})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}
.
Instead of working on the whole Shimura variety, we can
also work over
\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(C_{w}^{\dagger})
for any
w\in{}^{M}W
. We recall that this is a ringed space, whose underlying topological space is
\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(C_{w})
and whose structure sheaf is
j^{-1}_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}}
. In this case, we can consider functors:

VB^{0}_{\Sigma}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}(C_{w}^{\dagger})}).

If
b\in B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, there is a map
b:\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\pi_{HT,b\Sigma}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}
and a map
b^{*}VB^{0}_{b\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow VB^{0}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{F})
satisfying the usual cocycle condition. This leads us to consider the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})
whose objects are collections of sheaves
(\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma})_{\Sigma}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}
-modules, such that for any refinement
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
, inducing a map
\pi_{\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p},\Sigma}
, we have an isomorphism
\pi_{\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT,\Sigma^{\prime}}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}
-modules (and these isomorphisms are compatible),
and such that for any
b\in B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, there is a map
b^{*}\mathscr{F}_{b\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{\Sigma}
satisfying the usual cocycle condition.
The various
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
thus define a functor
VB^{0}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})
. Again, in practice, we fix some
\Sigma
and really
work with the functor
VB^{0}_{\Sigma}
(but drop
\Sigma
from the notation).

We also remark that we have
E
-rational structures
\mathcal{FL}^{rat}
on
\mathcal{FL}
and
C^{rat}_{w}
on
C_{w}
, and we can consider the categories
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL}^{rat})
and
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{rat,{\dagger}})
which admit base change functors to the categories
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})
and
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{{\dagger}})
.

Theorem 4.5.20
.

(1)

The functor

VB^{0}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})

is an exact functor.

(2)

For any
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, we have an analytic covering
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}=\cup V_{i}
, a sequence of compact open subgroups
K_{p,n}
and summand of orthonormalizable Banach sheaves
VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})
over
V_{i,K_{p,n}}
such that

VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})|_{V_{i}}=\operatorname{colim}_{n}VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,K_{p,n}}}}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}^{\mathrm{sm}}.

Moreover, there is a compact open subgroup
K_{p}
fixing
V_{i}
such that all sheaves
VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,K_{p,n}}}}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
are
K_{p}
-equivariant (compatibly with
n
) and this induces in the limit the
K_{p}
-equivariant structure on
VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})|_{V_{i}}
.

(3)

For any
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
