, we have

VB^{0}(\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}\mathscr{F}{\otimes}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}.

(4)

We have that
VB^{0}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}}
.

(5)

The functor

VB^{0}:LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})

is an exact functor.

(6)

For any
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, there exists an analytic covering by quasi-compact subsets
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})=\cup_{i}V_{i}
, a cofinal decreasing family of quasi-compact strict neighborhoods of
V_{i}
:
V_{i,n}=\lim_{K_{p}}V_{i,n,K_{p}}
, compact open subgroups
K_{p,n}
, and summand of orthonormalizable Banach sheaves
VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i,n}}(\mathscr{F})
over
V_{i,n,K_{p,n}}
such that

VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})|_{V_{i}}=\operatorname{colim}_{n}VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i,n}}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n,K_{p,n}}}}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n}^{\mathrm{sm}}}

Moreover, for each
i
, there is a compact open subgroup
K_{B}\subseteq B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
stabilizing
V_{i}
and all
V_{i,n}
and such that the sheaves
VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i,n}}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n,K_{p,n}}}}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n}^{\mathrm{sm}}}
are
K_{B}
-equivariant, compatibly in
n
, and this induces in the limit the
K_{B}
-equivariant structure on
VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})|_{V_{i}}
.

(7)

For any
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, we have that

VB^{0}(\mathscr{F}){\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}=\pi_{\mathrm{HT}}^{-1}\mathscr{F}{\otimes}_{\pi_{\mathrm{HT}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{C_{w}^{\dagger}}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}.

(8)

The following diagram of functors is commutative (where the horizontal
functors are given by
VB^{0}
, and the vertical functors are the natural
restriction functors):

\textstyle{LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})}

(9)

If
\mathscr{F}
arises from
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL}^{rat})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
or
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{rat,{\dagger}})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
, the action of
\mu
via
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}
is an arithmetic Sen operator on
VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})
.

Proof.

Everything but part (
4
) is an
immediate consequence of Theorem
4.5.4
. To
see (
4
), note firstly that since
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{u}_{P},{\mathcal{O}}_{G,e})={\mathcal{O}}_{U_{P_{w}}\backslash G,e}[0]

(
cf.
Lemma
2.3.4
), it follows from Theorem

4.5.4
(
3
) that
VB(\mathcal{O}_{G,e}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}})=VB^{0}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}})[0]
. On the other hand, bearing in mind Remark
4.5.2
, we see that

VB(\mathcal{O}_{G,e}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{Rla}},

where
\mathrm{Rla}
is the
functor of derived locally analytic vectors defined in
[
RJRC22
, Defn. 4.40]
. The result follows immediately. ∎

Remark 4.5.21
.

In particular the proof of Theorem
4.5.20
showed that

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{Rla}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
, confirming
Remark
4.4.2
.

Proposition 4.5.22
.

(1)

Assume that
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},G)}(\mathcal{FL})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
is such that the

\mathfrak{g}
-action is the derivative of the
G
-action. Then
VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})\in\Mod^{\mathrm{sm}}_{G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Sh}}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
.

(2)

Assume that
\mathscr{F}\in LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{P}^{0}}
is such that the restriction to
\mathfrak{b}

of the
\mathfrak{g}
-action is the derivative of the
B
-action. Then
VB^{0}(\mathscr{F})\in\Mod^{\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})
.

Proof.

This is immediate from the definition of
VB_{\Sigma}

(and of the
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-action).
∎

We now address the existence of an arithmetic Sen operator on the locally analytic vectors in completed cohomology. One can consider the category

\Mod_{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)}({\mathbf{C}}_{p})
of semi-linear
{\mathbf{C}}_{p}
-representations and define a Sen module functor

\displaystyle S^{\operatorname{arit}}:\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)}({\mathbf{C}}_{p})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}(\bar{E})

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{E^{\prime}}(V)^{\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}),\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{\operatorname{cycl}}/E^{\prime})-an}

We still denote by
S^{\operatorname{arit}}
the right derived functor. The following is

[
RC22
, Cor. 6.3.6]
.

Theorem 4.5.23
.

(1)

We have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S^{\operatorname{arit}}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p})\otimes_{\bar{E}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p},

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S^{\operatorname{arit}}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p})\otimes_{\bar{E}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}.

(2)

The action of
\mu
via
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}
on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\textrm{an}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}})
is the arithmetic Sen operator for the semilinear action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
(whose existence was guaranteed by (1)).

Proof.

We can take an affinoid covering
\mathcal{V}=\{V_{i}\}
of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
with the property that
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}|_{V_{i}}
is a colimit
of acyclic sheaves
VB^{0}_{K_{p,n},V_{i}}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{la}})
.
It follows that the Čech complex
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})
represents
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}
and carries a semi-linear
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
-action. It follows from Theorem
4.5.4
(4), that we have

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},S^{\operatorname{arit}}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}))\otimes_{\bar{E}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p},

where
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},S^{\operatorname{arit}}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}))
is therefore the sub-complex of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E^{\operatorname{cycl}})
-smooth and
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{E}/E)
-locally analytic vectors. We deduce that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\mathrm{la}}{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}
admits a Sen operator and it is given by
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}(\mu)
by Theorem
4.5.20
, (9).
∎

4.6.
Higher Coleman theory

4.6.1.
Automorphic vector bundles

One can apply the functor
VB^{0}
to the
G
-equivariant locally free sheaves of finite rank which are parameterized by finite dimensional representations of
M
.
Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)^{M,+}
. We let
\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}=VB^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{\kappa})
(where
\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}
is constructed in Example
3.2.18
). The sheaf
\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}
descends to a sheaf
\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}}
on the Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
(the usual sheaf of modular forms of weight
\kappa
).
By construction
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}})
is a complex of smooth admissible
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representations, equal to
\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}})
.
Recall that we have denoted by
D_{K_{p}}
the divisor of the boundary in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
. We then consider the cuspidal subsheaf
\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}}(-D_{K_{p}})
. Passing to the limit, we get

\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}(-D)=\operatorname{colim}\pi_{K_{p}}^{-1}\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}}(-D_{K_{p}})=\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\mathscr{I}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}.

Similarly,
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}}(-D))
is a complex of smooth admissible
G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representations, equal to
\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}},\omega^{\kappa}_{K_{p}}(-D_{K_{p}}))
.

Remark 4.6.2
.

For
G=\GSp_{4}
, the tautological exact sequence over
\mathcal{FL}
is

0\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{(0,-1;1)}\rightarrow St\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{FL}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{(1,0;1)}\rightarrow 0

which pulls back to

0\rightarrow Lie(A)_{K_{p}}(1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\rightarrow T_{p}A\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\rightarrow(\omega_{A^{t}})_{K_{p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\rightarrow 0

for any level
K_{p}
.
We deduce that
\omega^{(0,-1;1),\mathrm{sm}}=Lie(A)_{K_{p}}(1)\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}
and thus that

\omega^{(1,0;-1),\mathrm{sm}}=(\omega_{A})_{K_{p}}(-1)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{tor}}}

by
duality; so our normalization of the weights of Siegel
modular forms is the standard one.

4.6.3.
Higher Coleman sheaves

We now fix
w\in{}^{M}W
. Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)_{E}
. We consider the exact functor:

VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}\rightarrow LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}).

Lemma 4.6.4
.

The functor
VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,\lambda}
factors through the category
\mathrm{Mod}^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})
.

Proof.

This is a combination of Proposition
4.5.22
and of Remark
3.3.13
.
∎

Lemma 4.6.5
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)^{M,+}
. Let
L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}\in{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{0-\mathrm{alg}}
be the finite dimensional irreducible representation of highest weight
-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda
. Then
VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda})=\omega^{\lambda,sm}|_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}}
.

Proof.

. Let
V_{\lambda}
be the highest weight

\lambda
-representation of
M
. Then
V_{\lambda}
identifies
with
L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{w^{-1}\lambda}
if we conjugate
\mathfrak{m}_{w}
to
\mathfrak{m}
. It follows from the definitions that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{C_{w}^{\dagger}}=HCS_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda})
and the conclusion follows from applying
VB^{0}
.
∎

As in
Definition
2.3.16
, we have the Verma module

M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{\lambda}

of weight
\lambda
.

Definition 4.6.6
.

We define the following object of
\mathrm{Mod}^{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}C_{w}^{\dagger}})
:

\omega^{{\dagger},\lambda}_{w}:=VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}).

Remark 4.6.7
.

This definition compares with
[
BP21
, §6.3]
as follows.
In that reference we (GB+VP) defined Banach sheaves
\mathcal{V}_{\nu}^{n-an}
for characters
\nu:T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow{\mathbf{C}}_{p}^{\times}
,
n
large enough, over certain quasi-compact open subspaces
\pi^{-1}_{HT}(]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K_{p})
of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}K_{p}}
(for
K_{p}
small enough), where
]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K_{p}
is a quasi-compact open subset of
C_{w}
.
The colimit over
K_{p}
, over all
\nu
with
\mathrm{d\nu}=\lambda
and over
all
n
of the
\mathcal{V}_{\nu}^{n-an}
identifies canonically with the germ
of
\omega^{{\dagger},\lambda}_{w}
at
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\{w\})
. A slight change of
perspective from
[
BP21
]
is therefore this passage to the limit,
and the fact that we define the sheaf
\omega^{{\dagger},\lambda}_{w}
over the
entire
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})
. For the definition and computation of the finite
slope part of higher Coleman theory, the sheaves
\mathcal{V}_{\nu}^{n-an}
are
however sufficient (and seemed to us easier to define in the first place). See
Section
4.6.55
for further details.

One reason for the twist from
\lambda
to

-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda
is in order to obtain
Remark
4.6.8
and
Proposition
4.6.9
; more
conceptually, the multiplication by
w^{-1}
is justified by the change of base
point in the flag variety, and the appearance of
-w_{0,M}
is due to the usual duality
involution on highest weights which comes from the contravariance of

HCS_{w,\lambda}
.

Remark 4.6.8
.

We see from Proposition
3.4.19
that the
\Theta_{\mathrm{hor}}
-action on

HCS_{w,-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}(M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda})

is via
\lambda
. (We recall from
Remark
2.3.10
that
HC_{\mathfrak{m}}\iota=-w_{0,M}HC_{\mathfrak{m}}
.)
Thus, the arithmetic Sen operator acts via
\langle\mu,\lambda\rangle
on
\omega^{{\dagger},\lambda}_{w}
.

Proposition 4.6.9
.

Assume
that
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)^{M,+}
. We have an injective,
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant map:

\omega^{\lambda,\mathrm{sm}}|_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}^{\dagger})}\otimes E(-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda)\rightarrow\omega^{{\dagger},\lambda}_{w}.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.6.5
,
\omega^{\lambda,\mathrm{sm}}|_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}^{\dagger})}=VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda})
. The map of the proposition is thus given by applying
VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}
to the surjection
M(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}\rightarrow L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\lambda}
, using Proposition

3.4.18
.∎

4.6.10.
The Bruhat stratification and a Cousin spectral sequence

We recall the stratification into
B
-orbits
\mathcal{FL}=\coprod_{w\in{}^{M}W}C_{w}
, with
C_{w}=P\backslash P{w}B
.
We let
X_{w}=\cup_{w^{\prime}\leq w}C_{w^{\prime}}
be the Schubert variety. We let
j_{w}:C_{w}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{FL}
be the locally closed immersion. It induces
j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}:\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
.

Definition 4.6.11
.

If
\mathscr{F}
is any sheaf of solid
E
-modules on
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})
, we let

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},(j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})_{!}\mathscr{F}).

In this definition,
(j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})_{!}
is the extension by zero functor on abelian sheaves of solid abelian groups. By abuse of notation, if
\mathscr{F}
is defined over any subset of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
containing
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})
, we let

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},(j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})_{!}\mathscr{F}|_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})})
.

We now explain that if
\mathscr{F}
carries a
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant structure, the cohomology is a
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representation.

Lemma 4.6.12
.

The functor

\displaystyle\Mod(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod(E))

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})

can be upgraded to functors:

\displaystyle\Mod_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)).

\displaystyle\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)).

Proof.

The existence of
\Mod_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))\rightarrow D(\Mod_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))
follows from the fact that

\Mod_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))
has enough injectives (see
Lemma
3.2.10
).

We verify that this induces a functor
\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}))\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))
. We can reduce to
the case that
\lambda=0
.
Then
(j_{w,\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}})_{!}\mathscr{F}
is a smooth
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant sheaf on

\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
, which is quasi-compact, so by definition the
global sections
H^{0}_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})\in\Mod^{\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)
are a smooth
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representation. The
result follows by applying this to an injective resolution.
∎

Proposition 4.6.13
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a solid abelian sheaf defined over
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
.
We have a spectral sequence (Cousin spectral sequence):

E_{1}^{p,q}=\oplus_{w\in{}^{M}W,\ell(w)=p}H^{p+q}_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})\Rightarrow H^{p+q}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F}).

Proof.

See e.g.
[
BP21
, §2.3]
.
∎

Remark 4.6.14
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}
in Proposition
4.6.13

is
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant, then so (by construction) is the Cousin spectral sequence.

4.6.15.
Tools for computing the cohomology

In this section, we give a few basic tools for computing cohomology. All adic
spaces are locally of finite type over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
and are separated
unless specifically mentioned (thus they correspond to separated rigid analytic
spaces in the sense of Tate). We will often consider the case of Stein or
quasi-Stein spaces, which we recalled in Definition
2.2.17
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an adic space over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
. Let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent sheaf over
\mathcal{X}
. We can think of
\mathscr{F}
as an abelian sheaf of solid
E
-modules and compute
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})
in the derived category of solid
E
-modules,
D(\Mod(E))
.

Theorem 4.6.16

(Tate acyclicity)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an affinoid adic space. Let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent sheaf over
\mathcal{X}
.
The cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\in D(\Mod(E))
is concentrated in degree
0
.

Proof.

Let
\{\mathcal{U}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a finite affinoid covering of
\mathcal{X}
. We know by
[
Hub94
, Thm. 2.5]
that the augmented Čech complex of
\{\mathcal{U}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a long exact sequence of classical Banach spaces. Therefore, it is also an exact sequence of solid Banach spaces (see Remark
2.2.3
). One concludes by
[
Gro57
, 3.8, coro. 4]
.
∎

Corollary 4.6.17
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a finite type, separated adic space over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
. Let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent sheaf over
\mathcal{X}
. The cohomology of
\mathscr{F}
agrees with Čech cohomology.

Proof.

This is an application of
[
Gro57
, 3.8, coro. 4]
and Theorem
4.6.16
.
∎

Corollary 4.6.18
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a quasi-Stein affinoid adic space. Let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent sheaf over
\mathcal{X}
.
The cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\in D(\Mod(E))
is concentrated in degree
0
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.6.17
, the cohomology is given by

\mathrm{R}\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F})
. One knows by

[
Kie67
]
that the maps
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n+1},\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F})
of
classical Banach spaces have dense image. Then the topological Mittag-Leffler

[
RJRC22
, Lem. 3.27]
shows that
\mathrm{R}\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F})=\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F})
, as required.
∎

By
[
RJRC22
, Lem. 3.21]
a Banach or Smith space over
E
is flat.

Corollary 4.6.19
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a finite type, separated adic space over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
, and let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent sheaf. Let
V
be a Banach or Smith space over
E
. Then
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V
.

Proof.

In the affinoid case, we check that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V[0]
. To see this, we simply check it on the
Čech cohomology of arbitrary finite affinoid covers, and this follows from the
flatness of
V
. For a general
\mathcal{X}
, we deduce (again using
[
Gro57
, 3.8, coro. 4]
) that the cohomology of

\mathscr{F}\otimes V
is computed by the Čech cohomology of a finite
affinoid cover, and we use one more time that
V
is flat. ∎

Corollary 4.6.20
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a coherent
sheaf over
\mathcal{X}
, and let
V
be a Banach or Smith space
over
L
. Suppose that
\mathcal{X}
is covered by finitely many
quasi-Stein spaces. Then
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V
, and if
\mathcal{X}
is itself a quasi-Stein space, then we have
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=H^{0}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V[0]
.

Proof.

If
{\mathcal{X}}
is quasi-Stein, then by Corollary

4.6.19
, the cohomology is given by

\mathrm{R}\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V)
. By the topological Mittag-Leffler and
[
RJRC22
, Lem. 3.28]
, this limit is simply
(\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathscr{F}))\otimes_{E}V
. From this, we deduce that
if
\mathcal{X}
is covered by finitely many
quasi-Stein spaces, then the cohomology of
\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V

is computed by the Čech cohomology of any finite quasi-Stein cover,
and the claim follows again from flatness of
V
.
∎

We also consider duality and cohomology with compact support. Let
\mathcal{X}
be an adic space. Let
\mathscr{F}
be a solid sheaf of abelian groups. Following
[
Hub96
, 5.2]
, we
let
H^{0}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{Z}}H^{0}_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathscr{F})
be the space of sections with compact support where
\mathcal{Z}
runs through the poset of closed subsets of
\mathcal{X}
which are proper over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
.
We let
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})
be the cohomology
with compact
support. (Here
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})
is by definition equal to

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},(i_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{*}i_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{!}\mathscr{F})
, where
i_{{\mathcal{Z}}}:{\mathcal{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}
.)

Remark 4.6.21
.

For example,
\mathcal{Z}
is proper if it arises as the inverse image of a proper subset of a formal model of a quasi-compact open subset of
\mathcal{X}
.

Consider a quasi-compact and separated adic space of finite type
\mathcal{X}
. Let
j:\mathcal{U}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}
be an open adic subspace of
\mathcal{X}
. We assume that
\mathcal{U}
admits an increasing covering by quasi-compact opens
\mathcal{U}=\cup_{n}\mathcal{U}_{n}
with the property that
\mathcal{U}_{n}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
.

Example 4.6.22
.

One can take
\mathcal{X}=\mathbf{P}^{1}
,
\mathcal{U}=\mathbf{A}^{1,an}
. More generally, one can take
\mathcal{X}
a proper finite
type analytic space and
\mathcal{U}
be the complement of a Zariski closed
subset of
\mathcal{X}
(see
[
L9̈0
, 5.9]
). One can also take

\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Spa}(L\langle T\rangle,{\mathcal{O}}_{L}\langle T\rangle)
(the
closed unit ball of radius
1
), with
\mathcal{U}
the open unit ball of radius
1
inside
\mathcal{X}
.

Lemma 4.6.23
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a
solid abelian sheaf over
\mathcal{U}
as above. Then
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},j_{!}\mathscr{F})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{F})
where
{\mathcal{Z}}
runs through all closed subsets of
\mathcal{X}
contained in
\mathcal{U}
.
Moreover, there exists an increasing family of closed subspaces
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}_{n+1}
, each with quasi-compact complement in
\mathcal{X}
, such that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{F})
.

Proof.

Firstly, we check that a subset
{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
is closed
in
{\mathcal{X}}
if and only if
{\mathcal{Z}}
is proper over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{Z}}
is a closed subset of
\mathcal{X}
contained in
\mathcal{U}
. Then
{\mathcal{Z}}
is quasi-compact (as it is a closed subset of

\mathcal{X}
). Let
\mathcal{U}=\cup_{n}\mathcal{U}_{n}
. By
assumption,
\overline{\mathcal{U}_{n}}
(the closure of
\mathcal{U}_{n}

in
\mathcal{U}
) is proper over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
. Since
{\mathcal{Z}}
is
quasi-compact,
{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{n}
for
n
large
enough. Hence,
{\mathcal{Z}}
is closed in
\overline{\mathcal{U}_{n}}
, thus
partially proper. Conversely, if
{\mathcal{Z}}
is a subset of
\mathcal{U}
,
proper over
\mathrm{Spa}(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
, then
{{\mathcal{Z}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is
proper, as claimed.

For any closed subset
{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
, the counit of adjunction for
i_{{\mathcal{Z}}}:{\mathcal{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{X}}
gives a map
(i_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{*}i_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{!}j_{!}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow j_{!}\mathscr{F}
which induces a map
\operatorname{colim}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(i_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{*}i_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{!}j_{!}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow j_{!}\mathscr{F}
. This map is injective
(both sheaves are subsheaves of
j_{*}\mathscr{F}
), and both sheaves
restrict to
\mathscr{F}
on
\mathcal{U}
and have zero stalk at points of

\mathcal{X}\smallsetminus\mathcal{U}
; thus the map is an isomorphism. Since

\mathcal{X}
is quasi-compact and separated, this implies that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathcal{X},j_{!}\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{{Z}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\mathcal{X},j_{!}\mathscr{F})
.
Moreover,
\operatorname{colim}_{{{\mathcal{Z}}}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\mathcal{X},j_{!}\mathscr{F})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{F})
by definition. We finally claim that for each
n
there exists a closed subspace
\mathcal{U}_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{n+1}
with quasi-compact complement in
\mathcal{X}
. We let
\cup_{i\in I}V_{i}
be a covering by quasi-compact opens of
\overline{\mathcal{U}_{n}}^{c}
. We claim that there exists a finite subset
I^{\prime}
of
I
such that
\cup_{i\in I^{\prime}}V_{i}
contains
\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{c}
. This follows from endowing
\mathcal{X}
with the constructible topology and noticing that
\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{c}
is compact in this topology. We can take
{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}=(\cup_{i\in I^{\prime}}V_{i})^{c}
. The
{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}
are clearly cofinal among all
{\mathcal{Z}}
’s.
∎

The basic duality statement is the following.

Theorem 4.6.24

(
[
Chi90
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a locally free coherent sheaf defined over a smooth Stein space
\mathcal{X}
of dimension
d
. The cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})
is concentrated in degree
d
and is an
LB
-space of compact type. Moreover,

H^{d}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})=\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F})),E)

where

D(\mathscr{F})=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathscr{F},\Omega^{d}_{\mathcal{X}})
.

Remark 4.6.25
.

Although we use the formalism of compactly supported cohomology for abelian sheaves, we get the correct “coherent duality”. This is a favorable property of Stein spaces.

We now extend this result to orthonormalizable Banach sheaves.

Corollary 4.6.26
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a locally free
coherent sheaf defined over a Stein space
\mathcal{X}
of dimension
d
. Let
V
be a Smith space over
E
. We have that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V
is concentrated in degree
d
and moreover,

H^{d}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes V)=\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F})\otimes V^{\vee}),E).

Proof.

We let
\mathcal{X}=\cup_{n}\mathcal{X}_{n}
be an open cover by
affinoids, with
{\mathcal{X}_{n}}
relatively compact in

\mathcal{X}_{n+1}
. We also let
\mathcal{X}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{n+1}
be closed subspaces with quasi-compact
complement as in the statement of Lemma
4.6.23

(with
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}={\mathcal{X}}_{n}
). It follows from Lemma
4.6.23
that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}(\mathcal{X}_{n+1},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)
. We observe that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}(\mathcal{X}_{n+1},\mathscr{F}\otimes V)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}(\mathcal{X}_{n+1},\mathscr{F})\otimes V
by an easy reduction to Corollary
4.6.19
.
We deduce that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes_{E}V)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{E}V
.
Next, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F})\otimes V^{\vee}),E)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F}))\otimes V^{\vee},E)~\textrm{by Corollary \ref{cor-cohosteinV}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F})),V)~\textrm{by adjunction and Proposition \ref{prop: antieq Smith Banach}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(H^{0}(\mathcal{X},D(\mathscr{F})),E)\otimes_{E}V~\textrm{by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{MR4475468}{}{}, Thm.\ 3.40]}}

\displaystyle=H^{d}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes V~\textrm{by Theorem \ref{thm-Chiarellotto}}

\displaystyle=H^{d}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{F}\otimes V)~\textrm{by the first point.}\qed

4.6.27.
On the computation of the local cohomology

We want to give some formulas for computing
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})
.

The Bruhat cell
C_{w}
is an affine space of dimension
\ell(w)
and
its closure
X_{w}
is a compactification of this affine space. We
recall (see
[
BP21
, Lemma 3.1.3]
for example) that
C_{w}=w\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M}}U_{\alpha}
where
U_{\alpha}
is the
\alpha
-root space, isomorphic to
\mathbf{A}^{1,an}
.
We also have a neighborhood of
C_{w}
,
U_{w}=w\prod_{\alpha\in w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M}}U_{\alpha}
. Let us pick a coordinate
u_{\alpha}
on each
U_{\alpha}
.

Definition 4.6.28
.

We now define certain subsets of
Z_{n},Z_{n,m}
,
U_{n}
and
U_{n,m}
of
C_{w}
, for
n,m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
Let
n_{0}\geq 0
be fixed. We take:

•

Z_{n}=\{x\in C_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}<|p|^{n_{0}-n}_{x}\}
.

•

U_{n}=\{x\in C_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{n_{0}-n-1}_{x}\}
.

•

Z_{n,m}=\{x\in U_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}<|p|^{n_{0}-n}_{x},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}<|p|^{m}_{x}\}
,

•

U_{n,m}=\{x\in U_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{n_{0}-n-1}_{x},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{m}_{x}\}
.

Here are some obvious properties of these sets.

•

\cup_{n}Z_{n}=\cup_{n}U_{n}=C_{w}
,

•

The complement of
Z_{n}
in
X_{w}
is a quasi-compact open subset,

•

U_{n}
is a quasi-compact open subset of
X_{w}
,

•

U_{n}=\cap_{m}U_{n,m}
, and
U_{n,m}\cap X_{w}=U_{n}
,

•

U_{n,m}
is a quasi-compact open of
\mathcal{FL}
,

•

Z_{n,m}
is a closed subset of
U_{n,m}
with quasi-compact complement,

•

Z_{n}=U_{n}\cap Z_{n,m}
.

We let
F_{n}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(Z_{n})
,
V_{n}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{n})
,
F_{n,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(Z_{n,m})
and
V_{n,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{n,m})
.

Lemma 4.6.29
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a solid abelian sheaf over
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})
. We have
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n}}(V_{n},\mathscr{F})
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Lemma

4.6.23
(since the
V_{n}
give a cofinal system of
closed subsets of
Z_{w}
).

∎

Lemma 4.6.30
.

Let
m_{0}\geq 0
and let
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
be a sheaf on
V_{n,m_{0}}
and let
\mathscr{F}=i^{-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
on
V_{n}
for
i:V_{n}\rightarrow V_{n,m_{0}}
. Then we have:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n}}(V_{n},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{m\geq m_{0}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n,m}}(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We consider the triangle:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n,m}}(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m}\smallsetminus F_{n,m}),\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

Passing to the colimit over
m
, we need to see that
\operatorname{colim}_{m}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n},\mathscr{F})
and

\operatorname{colim}_{m}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m}\smallsetminus F_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n}\smallsetminus F_{n},\mathscr{F})
. This follows from the
fact that all spaces involved are quasi-compact.
∎

The combination of lemmas
4.6.29
and
4.6.30
allows us to compute the cohomology as a colimit of cohomologies of the shape
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n,m}}(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
where

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n,m}}(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
fits in a triangle:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n,m}}(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{n,m}\smallsetminus F_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

and both
V_{n,m}
and
V_{n,m}\smallsetminus F_{n,m}
are quasi-compact opens.

We now give another similar presentation of the cohomology which puts emphasis on the cohomology with compact support. We will be using Stein spaces because of remark
4.6.25
.

Definition 4.6.31
.

We define sets
X_{n}
,
X_{n,m}
and
T_{n,m,r}
for
n,m,r\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

•

For any
\epsilon\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{>0}
, we put
X_{n,\epsilon}=\{x\in C_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{n_{0}-n+\epsilon}_{x}\}
and set
X_{n}=\cup_{\epsilon>0}X_{n,\epsilon}
.

•

X_{n,m,\epsilon}=\{x\in U_{w},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{n_{0}-n+\epsilon}_{x},\forall\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\leq|p|^{m+\epsilon}_{x}\}
, and set
X_{n,m}=\cup_{\epsilon>0}X_{n,m,\epsilon}
.

•

We also put
T_{n,m,r,\eta}=\{x\in X_{n,m},\exists\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap w^{-1}\Phi^{-,M},|u_{\alpha}|_{x}\geq|p|^{r-\eta}\}
. We let
T_{n,m,r}=\cup_{\eta>0}T_{n,m,r,\eta}
.

We see that
X_{n}
is an increasing sequence of Stein subsets of
C_{w}

with the property that
C_{w}=\cup_{n}X_{n}
and that each
X_{n}
is
included in a quasi-compact open subset
Z_{n}
of
C_{w}
. We see that

X_{n,m}
is a decreasing (in
m
) family of Stein open subsets of

\mathcal{FL}
with
X_{n}=X_{n,m}\cap X_{w}
and
\cap_{m}X_{n,m}=X_{n}
. We have
\cup_{r>0}T_{n,m,r}=X_{n,m}\smallsetminus X_{n}
.

Let
Y_{n}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(X_{n})
. We let
Y_{n,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(X_{n,m})
and
W_{n,m,r}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(T_{m,n,r})
.

Lemma 4.6.32
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a solid abelian sheaf over
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w})
. We have
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n},\mathscr{F})
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma

4.6.29
. Indeed the two inductive systems
are equivalent as
Y_{n}\subseteq F_{n}\subseteq Y_{n+1}
, and we
have a series of natural maps
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n}}(V_{n},\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n},\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{n+1}}(V_{n+1},\mathscr{F})
.
∎

Lemma 4.6.33
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
be a sheaf on
Y_{n,m}
and let
\mathscr{F}=i^{-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
on
Y_{n}
. Then we have a triangle:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n,m}\smallsetminus Y_{n},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n,m},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n},\mathscr{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

Moreover,
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{n,m}\smallsetminus Y_{n},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})=\operatorname{colim}_{r}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(W_{n,m,r},\mathscr{F}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let
m\geq 0
. We consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{Y_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{n}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\pi_{HT}^{-1}(X_{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y_{n,m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{n,m}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}}
\textstyle{Y_{n,m}\smallsetminus Y_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{i_{n,m}}

We have to prove that we have a triangle

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},(i_{n,m})_{!}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},(j_{n,m})!\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\pi_{HT}^{-1}(X_{w}),(j_{n})_{!}\mathscr{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

We have the triangle
j_{!}j^{-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\rightarrow i_{*}\mathscr{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}
. We apply
(j_{n,m})_{!}
, and we get a triangle:

(i_{n,m})_{!}j^{-1}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\rightarrow(j_{n,m})_{!}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\rightarrow(j_{n,m})_{!}i_{*}\mathscr{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}
.
Observe that
(j_{n,m})_{!}i_{*}\mathscr{F}=i^{\prime}_{*}(j_{n})_{!}\mathscr{F}
(as
i_{*}=i_{!}
and
i^{\prime}_{*}=i^{\prime}_{!}
). We then apply
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},-)
.
∎

4.6.34.
Higher Coleman functors

Definition 4.6.35
.

Using Lemma
4.6.12
, we define the contravariant higher Coleman
functor:

\displaystyle HC_{w,\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M)))

and the contravariant cuspidal higher Coleman functor:

\displaystyle HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M)(-D))).

We extend these functors to the derived category

HC_{w,\lambda},HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}:D^{b}({\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}})\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

by putting
HC_{w,\lambda}(M)=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},VB^{\operatorname{red}}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M)))
and similarly in the cuspidal case.
We note that
HCS_{w,\lambda}
is an exact functor
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{m}_{w},\mathfrak{b}_{M_{w}})_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}\rightarrow LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}}
, and
VB^{0}
is also exact on
LB_{(\mathfrak{g},B)}(C_{w}^{\dagger})^{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}}
. Therefore, if
M
is a complex,
VB^{\operatorname{red}}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M))
is computed by applying
VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(-))
to each term of a complex representing
M
. Moreover, its
i
-th cohomology sheaf
\underline{H}^{i}(VB^{\operatorname{red}}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M))
is
VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(H^{i}(M)))
.

4.6.36.
Formal models and the Hodge–Tate period map

Our goal is to compute the cohomological amplitude of the higher Coleman functors. The main source of vanishing is the affineness of the Hodge–Tate period map. To perform the argument, we need to consider formal models for some of the spaces and sheaves introduced so far.
We can consider the following diagram where
\mathrm{Sh}^{*}_{K^{p}}
is the minimal compactification.

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{HT}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}^{*}_{K^{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}_{HT}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{FL}}

In
[
BP21
]
, sect. 4.4.31, we constructed a formal model of this diagram:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K^{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{FL}}

Moreover
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}}=\lim_{K_{p}}\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
and
\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K^{p}}=\lim_{K_{p}}\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
for all
K_{p}
small enough, where
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is a formal model of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
and
\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is a formal model of
\mathrm{Sh}^{*}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a quasi-compact open of
\mathcal{FL}
. Let

\mathfrak{U}\rightarrow\mathfrak{FL}
be a formal model for the map

\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{FL}
. Using the notation of
[
BP21
]
, sect. 4.4.31, we have a formal model for
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
, denoted by
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}
as well as a formal model for
(\pi^{*}_{HT})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
denoted by
\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}
. There is a diagram:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{U}}

Moreover, we have
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})=\lim_{K_{p}}\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})_{K_{p}}
for
K_{p}
small enough and
accordingly
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}=\lim_{K_{p}}\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}
. Similarly, for
K_{p}
small enough
(\pi^{*}_{HT})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})=\lim_{K_{p}}(\pi^{*}_{HT})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})_{K_{p}}
and
\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}=\lim_{K_{p}}\mathfrak{Sh}^{*,mod}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}}
.

4.6.37.
Formal Banach sheaves and formal Smith sheaves

In this
section we consider a flat
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
which locally of topologically finite
type over
\Spf~{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
(in other words, Zariski locally,
\mathfrak{X}
is
\Spf~A
where
A
is a
quotient of
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}\rangle
). We write
{\mathcal{X}}
for the
generic fiber of
\mathfrak{X}
. The sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a sheaf
of solid
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
-modules, as are all the sheaves we will consider. We define the scheme
X_{n}=\mathfrak{X}\times_{\Spf~{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}\Spec~{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p^{n}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
.

Definition 4.6.38
.

(1)

A locally trivial formal Banach sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}
is a sheaf

\mathfrak{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-modules which is flat as
an
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
-module, and such that
\mathfrak{F}=\lim_{n}\mathscr{F}_{n}
for
\mathscr{F}_{n}=\mathfrak{F}/p^{n}\mathfrak{F}
, and there exists a covering
\mathfrak{X}=\cup_{i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}
and sets

I_{i}
and such that

\mathscr{F}_{n}|_{V_{i,n}}=\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p^{n}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}(\oplus_{s\in I_{i}}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p^{n}{\mathcal{O}}_{E})
and the transition maps
\mathscr{F}_{n}|_{V_{i,n}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{n-1}|_{V_{i,{n-1}}}
are the obvious ones.

(2)

A very small formal Banach sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}
is a sheaf

\mathfrak{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-modules which is flat as
an
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
-module, and such that
\mathfrak{F}=\lim_{n}\mathscr{F}_{n}
for
\mathscr{F}_{n}=\mathfrak{F}/p^{n}\mathfrak{F}
, and
\mathscr{F}_{1}=\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}(\oplus_{s\in I}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p)
for some coherent sheaf
\mathscr{G}_{1}
and some set
I
.

Definition 4.6.39
.

(1)

A locally trivial formal Smith sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}
a sheaf

\mathfrak{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-modules which is flat as an

{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
-module, such that
\mathfrak{F}=\lim_{n}\mathscr{F}_{n}
for
\mathscr{F}_{n}=\mathfrak{F}/p^{n}\mathfrak{F}
, and there exists a covering
\mathfrak{X}=\cup_{i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}
and sets
I_{i}
such that

\mathscr{F}_{n}=\mathcal{O}_{V_{i,n}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p^{n}{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}/p^{n}{\mathcal{O}}_{E})^{I_{i}}
with the obvious transition maps
\mathscr{F}_{n}|_{V_{i,n}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{n-1}|_{V_{i,n-1}}
.

(2)

A very small formal Smith sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}
is a sheaf

\mathscr{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-modules which is flat as an

{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
-module, such that
\mathfrak{F}=\lim_{n}\mathscr{F}_{n}
for
\mathscr{F}_{n}=\mathfrak{F}/p^{n}\mathfrak{F}
, and
\mathscr{F}_{1}=\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes_{E}(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p\mathcal{O}_{E})^{I}
for a coherent sheaf
\mathscr{G}_{1}
and some set
I
.

If
\mathfrak{F}
is a locally trivial formal Smith sheaf, then it
has a generic fiber
\mathscr{F}
over
\mathcal{X}
defined as
follows: we take a covering
\mathcal{X}=\cup_{i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}
,
such that
\mathfrak{F}|_{\mathfrak{V}_{i}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{E}^{I_{i}}
. Over the generic fibre
\mathcal{V}_{i}
of
\mathfrak{V}_{i}
, we define
\mathscr{F}|_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}\otimes_{E}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}^{I_{i}}[1/p])
and we use the gluing data of
\mathfrak{F}
to glue the
\mathscr{F}|_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}
.

A similar construction applies to a locally trivial Banach sheaf. See
also
[
BP21
, Thm. 2.5.9]
for a more general
statement.

Proposition 4.6.40
.

If
\mathfrak{F}
is a very small formal Banach sheaf or a very small formal Smith sheaf,
\mathfrak{X}
admits an ample invertible sheaf and
\mathcal{X}
is affinoid, then
H^{i}(\mathfrak{X},\mathscr{F})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}E=0
for all
i>0
.

Proof.

The formal Banach case is

[
BP21
, Thm. 2.5.8]
. The same proof with minor modification applies to the Smith case.
∎

4.6.41.
Cohomological amplitude of the higher Coleman functors

Let
M\in\mathcal{O}_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
. Let us consider the sheaf

{\mathcal{F}}:=VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M))
. Theorem
4.5.20
addresses the local
structure of this sheaf. We also need to produce some integral structure, as in
the following lemma.

Lemma 4.6.42
.

There exists a quasi-compact open subset

U_{0}\subseteq C_{w}
containing
w
such that if we set
V_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0})
, then we can write
\mathscr{F}|_{V_{0}}=\operatorname{colim}i_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{m}
where:

(1)

U_{0}=\cap_{m}U_{0,m}
where
\{U_{0,m}\}_{m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
is a decreasing sequence of quasi-compact open affinoid subsets of
\mathcal{FL}
;

(2)

V_{0,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0,m})
is a quasi-compact open subset of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
, stable under a compact open subgroup
K_{p,m}\subseteq G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
;

(3)

i_{m}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0,m}
is the natural inclusion;

(4)

\mathscr{F}_{m}
is a sheaf over
V_{0,m}
;

(5)

V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}K_{p,m}}
is a quasi-compact open which descends
V_{0,m}
to finite level
K_{p,m}
, and
\pi_{K_{p,m}}:V_{0,m}\rightarrow V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}
is the induced projection;

(6)

We have a Banach sheaf
\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
on
V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}
and
\mathscr{F}_{m}=\pi_{K_{p,m}}^{-1}\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}\otimes_{\pi_{K_{p,m}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}}}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{V_{0,m}}
;

(7)

The sheaves
\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
arise as the generic fibers of
locally trivial, very small, formal Banach sheaves

\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
over

\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p,m},\mathfrak{U}_{0,m}}
.

Proof.

The first
6
points follow from Theorem
4.5.20
. We need to give a more explicit construction of
\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
in order to be able to produce an integral structure. We will follow closely the proof of

[
BP21
, Lem. 6.6.2]
. As
M\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
, we deduce that
\hat{M}^{\vee}(\lambda)=\operatorname{colim}_{r}M_{r}
where each
M_{r}
is a Banach space representation of
the group denoted
\mathrm{Stab}(w)_{r,s}
in Definition

3.3.10
(with
Q=B
). Unraveling the definition, we deduce in particular that
M_{r}
is a Banach space representation of
M_{w,r}U_{M_{w},s}=:M_{w,r,s}\hookrightarrow M_{w}
. Moreover, after possibly changing
r
and
s
, we can also assume that there is a lattice
M_{r}^{+}\subseteq M_{r}
with the property that the co-action map
M_{r}\rightarrow M_{r}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{M_{w,r,s}}
induces a map
M^{+}_{r}\rightarrow M^{+}_{r}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{+}_{M_{w,r,s}}
, trivial on
M_{r}^{+}/pM_{r}^{+}
.
We consider the
M_{w,r,s}
-torsor
w(U_{P_{w}}\cap G_{r}U_{s})\backslash G_{r}U_{s}\rightarrow w({P_{w}}\cap G_{r}U_{s})\backslash G_{r}U_{s}
. This is
a reduction of structure of the standard
M
-torsor
U_{P}\backslash G\rightarrow P\backslash G
over
w(P_{w}\cap G_{r}U_{s})\backslash G_{r}U_{s}
. Let us write
\mathcal{U}_{r}=w(P_{w}\cap G_{r}U_{s})\backslash G_{r}U_{s}
, which is a quasi-compact open subset of

\mathcal{FL}
. Over
\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
, we pull back to get a
M_{w,r,s}
-torsor that we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{dR,r,s,\mathcal{U}_{r}}
. For
K_{p}
small enough, by
[
BP21
, prop. 4.6.12]
, it descends to a

M_{w,r,s}
-torsor
\mathcal{M}_{dR,r,s,\mathcal{U}_{r},K_{p}}
over

\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{r})_{K_{p}}
. Moreover, by

[
BP21
, prop. 4.6.15]
for any affinoid open subset

V_{K_{p}}\subseteq\pi_{HT}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})_{K_{p}}
, we can find

K_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{p}
such that the torsor
\mathcal{M}_{dR,r,s,\mathcal{U}_{r},K^{\prime}_{p}}|_{V_{K_{p}^{\prime}}}
is trivial. After
rescaling, we can assume that
U_{0,r}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{r}
. We now attach to
M_{r}
a locally projective small formal Banach sheaf
on the formal model
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p,r},\mathfrak{U}_{0,r}}
of
V_{0,r,K_{p,r}}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}({U}_{0,r})_{K_{p,r}}
for a small enough
K_{p,r}
. Indeed, we pick a finite affine covering
\cup_{i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}=\Spf(A_{i})
of
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p,r},\mathfrak{U}_{0,r}}
. After replacing
K_{p,r}
by a smaller compact open, we can assume that
\mathcal{M}_{dR,r,s,{U}_{0,r},K_{p,r}}
is trivial over the generic fiber of every
\mathfrak{V}_{i}
. It follows that the torsor is described by a 1-cocycle
\{m_{i,j}\in M_{w,r,s}((A_{i,j}[1/p],A_{i,j}))\}_{i,j}
(where
\mathfrak{V}_{i,j}=\mathfrak{V}_{i}\cap\mathfrak{V}_{j}=\Spf(A_{i,j})
). We can use the elements
m_{i,j}
to glue the trivial formal Banach sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{i}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}M_{r}^{+}
to get the very small, locally trivial, formal Banach sheaf
\mathfrak{G}_{r,K_{p,r}}
. The very smallness property comes from the fact that the
m_{i,j}
act trivially on
M_{r}^{+}/p
.
∎

We give a technical variant of this description, using formal Smith sheaves instead.

Lemma 4.6.43
.

There exists a quasi-compact open subset

U_{0}\subseteq C_{w}
containing
w
such that if we set
V_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0})
, then we can write
\mathscr{F}|_{V_{0}}=\operatorname{colim}i_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{m}
where:

(1)

U_{0}=\cap_{m}U_{0,m}
where
\{U_{0,m}\}_{m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
is a decreasing sequence of quasi-compact open affinoid subsets of
\mathcal{FL}
,

(2)

V_{0,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0,m})
is a quasi-compact open subset of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}}
, stable under a compact open subgroup
K_{p,m}\subseteq G({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
,

(3)

i_{m}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0,m}
is the natural inclusion,

(4)

\mathscr{F}_{m}
is a sheaf over
V_{0,m}
,

(5)

V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}K_{p,m}}
is a quasi-compact open which descends
V_{0,m}
to finite level
K_{p,m}
, and
\pi_{K_{p,m}}:V_{0,m}\rightarrow V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}
is the induced projection,

(6)

We have a sheaf
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p,m}}
on
V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}
and
\mathscr{F}_{m}=\pi_{K_{p,m}}^{-1}\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}\otimes_{\pi_{K_{p,m}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}}}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{V_{0,m}}
.

(7)

The sheaves
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p,m}}
arise as the generic fibers of
locally trivial, very small, formal Smith sheaves

\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
over

\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p,m},\mathfrak{U}_{0,m}}
.

Proof.

The argument is almost identical to the proof of Lemma
4.6.42
. We have that
\hat{M}^{\vee}(\lambda)=\operatorname{colim}_{r}M_{r}
where each
M_{r}
is a Banach space representation of the group denoted
\mathrm{Stab}(w)_{r,s}
in Definition
3.3.10
. Since this is a
LB
-space of compact type, it also admits a presentation as a
LS
-space of compact type,
\hat{M}^{\vee}(\lambda)=\operatorname{colim}_{r}M^{\prime}_{r}
where each
M^{\prime}_{r}
is a Smith space representation of
\mathrm{Stab}(w)_{r,s}
(see
[
RJRC22
, Cor. 3.38]
). We then pick lattices
(M^{\prime}_{r})^{+}
in
M^{\prime}_{r}
and glue the sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{i}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}}(M^{\prime}_{r})^{+}
to get the very small formal Smith sheaf
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{r,K_{p,r}}
.
∎

For any
K_{p}\subseteq K_{p,m}
, we have maps
\pi_{K_{p},K_{p,m}}:\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p},\mathfrak{U}_{0,m}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor},mod}_{K^{p}K_{p,m},\mathfrak{U}_{0,m}}
and we write

\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}:=\pi_{K_{p},K_{p,m}}^{*}\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}

(a locally trivial, very small formal Banach sheaf) and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}:=\pi_{K_{p},K_{p,m}}^{*}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p,m}}
(a locally trivial, very
small formal Smith sheaf). We denote their generic fibers by
\mathcal{G}_{m,K_{p}}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}
.

Lemma 4.6.44
.

(1)

For any affinoid
U^{\prime}\subseteq U_{0,m}
, any compact open subgroup
K_{p}\subseteq K_{p,m^{\prime}}
fixing
U^{\prime}
, and letting
V^{\prime}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
, we have
H^{i}(V^{\prime}_{K_{p}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))=0
for all
i>0
.

(2)

For any Stein space
S^{\prime}\subseteq U_{0,m}
stable under a compact open subgroup
K_{p}\subseteq K_{p,m^{\prime}}
, let
V^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(S^{\prime})
. We have
H^{i}_{c}(V_{K_{p}}^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})=0
for all
i\neq d
.

Proof.

The first part follows as in lemma 6.6.2 of

[
BP21
]
. We briefly recall the argument. We take a
formal model
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{FL}
of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{FL}
. By Lemma
4.6.42
, we have a locally trivial, very small, formal Banach sheaf
\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}
over
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}
.
Let
\pi:\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Sh}^{*}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}
. We have that

\mathrm{R}^{i}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D)=0
for all
i>0
. Indeed, using the very smallness, this reduces to the vanishing
(
[
BP21
, Thm. 4.4.37]
) of
\mathrm{R}^{i}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}}(-D)
. We deduce
that
\pi_{*}\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D)
is a very small formal Banach
sheaf and thus Proposition
4.6.40
implies that

H^{i}(\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))\otimes E=0
for all
i>0
.

We claim that
H^{i}(\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))\otimes E=H^{i}(V^{\prime}_{K_{p}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))
. To see this, take a Zariski open affine cover
\{\mathfrak{W}_{s}\}_{s}
of
\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}
with the property that
\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D)|_{\mathfrak{W}_{s}}
is a trivial formal Banach sheaf.
By Corollary
4.6.19

H^{i}(V^{\prime}_{K_{p}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))
is computed by the Čech complex associated to the generic fiber of this cover, which computes
H^{i}(\mathfrak{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{p}K^{p},\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))\otimes E
. We
deduce that
H^{i}(V^{\prime}_{K_{p}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p}}(-D))=0
for all

i>0
, as required.

The second part follows from a certain form of duality. Let us define
the “Serre dual” of
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}
,

D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{0,m,K_{p}}}}(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}},\Omega^{d}_{V_{0,m,K_{p}}}),

by which we mean the following.
We take a finite Stein covering
V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p}}=\cup_{i}V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}
with the
property that
\Omega^{d}_{V_{m,K_{p}}}|_{V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}}
is trivial and that

\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}=\mathcal{O}_{V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}}\otimes M_{i}
for a Smith space

M_{i}
. Then
D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})|_{V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}}=\mathcal{O}_{V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}}\otimes M^{\vee}_{i}
where
M^{\vee}_{i}
is a Banach space. We consider the Čech complex of Fréchet spaces:

C:\prod_{i}H^{0}(V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i},D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}))\rightarrow\prod_{i,j}H^{0}(V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i}\cap V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i},D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}))\rightarrow\cdots

as well as the Čech complex of
LS
-spaces:

D:H^{d}_{c}(\cap_{i}V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})\rightarrow\prod_{j}H^{d}_{c}(\cap_{i\neq j}V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p},i},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})\rightarrow\cdots

The two complexes are termwise dual of each other by the duality theory (see Corollary
4.6.26
). The complex
D
computes
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p}},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
and the complex
C
computes
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V^{\prime\prime}_{K_{p}},D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}))
by Corollary
4.6.20
and
4.6.26
.
Thus, it suffices to prove that the complex
C
has cohomology concentrated in
degree
0
. Write
S^{\prime}=\cup S^{\prime}_{r}
as a countable increasing union of
affinoids, and let
V^{\prime\prime}_{r}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(S^{\prime}_{r})
and
V^{\prime\prime}_{r}=\cup_{i}V^{\prime\prime}_{r,i}
. It follows from Lemma
4.6.43
that
D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
admits a formal model which is a very small formal Banach sheaf over a formal model of
V^{\prime\prime}_{r,K_{p}}
. We deduce that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V^{\prime\prime}_{r,K_{p}},D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}))
is concentrated in degree
0
by the same argument as for the first point of the lemma. Thus, the Čech complex
C_{r}
of
D(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
with respect to
\{V^{\prime\prime}_{r,i,K_{p}}\}
has only cohomology in degree
0
. We then use topological Mittag-Leffler to deduce that
C=\lim_{r}C_{r}
is concentrated in degree
0
.
∎

Theorem 4.6.45
.

For any
M\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}
,
HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}(M)
has amplitude
[0,\ell(w)]
and
HC_{w,\lambda}(M)
has amplitude
[\ell(w),d]
.

Proof.

We first prove the claim regarding
HC_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}(M)
. Let us denote
\mathscr{F}=VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M))(-D)
.
Let us fix a closed neighborhood
Z_{0}
of
w
in
C_{w}
and an open
neighborhood
U_{0}
of
Z_{0}
in
C_{w}
as in definition

4.6.28
. Let
t\in T^{++}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. It is easy to see that
\cup_{n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}Z_{0}t^{n}=C_{w}
. As above, we put
V_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0})
and
F_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}Z_{0}
. By Lemma
4.6.29
(noting that
Z_{0}t^{n}
and
Z_{n}
are cofinal), we have
that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}t^{n}}(V_{0}t^{n},\mathscr{F})
. It follows that each
H^{i}_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})
is generated as a
B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-module by the image of
H^{i}_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F})
in
H^{i}_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\mathscr{F})
. Thus it suffices to see that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F})
has amplitude
[0,\ell(w)]
.

We are free to chose
n_{0}
in definition
4.6.28
, and
to make
F_{0}
and
V_{0}
arbitrarily small. By Lemma

4.6.42
, we can assume that
\mathscr{F}|_{V_{0}}=\operatorname{colim}\mathscr{F}_{m}
where
\mathscr{F}_{m}=\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}}}\mathcal{O}_{V_{0,m}}(-D)
where
\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,m}}
is a Banach sheaf admitting a locally trivial, very small, formal model over
V_{0,m,K_{p,m}}
. By Lemma
4.6.30
, we have that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}_{m}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F}_{m})
and

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F}_{m})=\operatorname{colim}_{s,n}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0,s,K_{p,n}}}(V_{0,s,K_{p,n}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,n}}(-D))
where
K_{p,n}
tends to
\{e\}
. Thus it suffices to prove that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0,s,K_{p,n}}}(V_{0,s,K_{p,n}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,n}}(-D))
has cohomological amplitude in
[0,\ell(w)]
. Recall from definition
4.6.28
that
F_{0,s}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(Z_{0,s})
and
V_{0,s}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0,s})
for
s
large
enough (and a fixed choice of
n_{0}
big enough). We observe that
U_{0,s}
is
affinoid while
U_{0,s}\smallsetminus Z_{0,s}
is covered by
\ell(w)
affinoids.
Using the triangle:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0,s,K_{p,n}}}(V_{0,s,K_{p,n}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,n}}(-D))\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{0,s,K_{p,n}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,n}}(-D))

\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(V_{0,s,K_{p,n}}\smallsetminus F_{0,s,K_{p,n}},\mathscr{G}_{m,K_{p,n}}(-D))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

together with Lemma
4.6.44
, we arrive at the desired
conclusion.

We now turn to the case of usual cohomology, which follows along
similar lines to the above, using the presentation via cohomology with
compact support. Let us denote now
\mathscr{F}=VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M))
. We first see by lemma

4.6.32
and a similar argument using the

B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-action, that it is enough to check that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0},\mathscr{F})
is concentrated in degrees

[\ell(w),d]
where
Y_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}X_{0}
and
X_{0}
is a Stein open
neighborhood of
w
in
C_{w}
(see Definition

4.6.31
). Next, we use Lemma

4.6.43
to see that
\mathscr{F}|_{Y_{0}}=\operatorname{colim}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{m}
where
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{m}=\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p,m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{0,m,K_{p,m}}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{0,m}}
and
Y_{0,m}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(X_{0,m})
for

X_{0,m}
a Stein neighborhood of
X_{0}
in
\mathcal{FL}
. We deduce
that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0},\mathscr{F})=\operatorname{colim}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0},\mathscr{F}_{m}^{\prime})
. Let
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m}=\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}
where
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}}
is the pull back of
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p,m}}
to
Y_{0,m,K_{p}}
for
K_{p}\subseteq K_{p,m}
and
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m}
is viewed as a sheaf on
Y_{0,m}
. We deduce from lemma
4.6.33
that we have
a triangle

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0,m}\smallsetminus Y_{0},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0,m},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0},\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}.

Recall that
W_{0,m,r}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(T_{0,m,r})
(see definition
4.6.31
).
We have that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0,m},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m})=\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0,m,K_{p}},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
is concentrated in degree
d
by Lemma
4.6.44
.
Similarly,
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(Y_{0,m}\smallsetminus Y_{0},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m})=\operatorname{colim}_{r}\operatorname{colim}_{K_{p}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(W_{0,m,r,K_{p}},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
, and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{c}(W_{0,m,r,K_{p}},\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{m,K_{p}})
has cohomology concentrated in degrees

[\ell(w)+1,d]
by Lemma
4.6.44
(as
T_{0,m,r}
is the union of
\ell(w)
Stein spaces).
∎

4.6.46.
Finite slope projector

We follow the notation introduced in Section
1.8.5
. Let
\mathcal{Z}
be the character space of
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. Fixing an
isomorphism
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}^{r}\times T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})
(with
r
the rank of
T^{d}
, the maximal split torus in
T
), we see that

\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{W}\times(\mathbf{G}_{m}^{an})^{r}
with

\mathcal{W}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{Z}}_{p}\llbracket T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket,{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}\llbracket T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket)\times_{\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})}\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p})
.

We fix an affinoid increasing covering of the Stein space
\mathcal{Z}=\cup_{n}\mathcal{Z}_{n}
. As in
[
And21
]
, we let

D(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
denote the category
D((\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}^{+})_{\blacksquare})
, and we let
D(\mathcal{Z})
be the derived category of
quasi-coherent sheaves over
\mathcal{Z}
(see

[
And21
, Thm. 1.6]
).
We have defined in Section
1.8.5
the monoids
T^{++}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\subseteq T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\subseteq T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
.
Note that
T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})=T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\times{\mathbf{Z}}^{s}\times{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{r-s}
, where
s
is the rank of the maximal
split torus in
Z(G)
. We let
({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]
be the solidification of

{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]
, and we let
({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]
the solidification of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]
.
The categories of solid modules over these rings are denoted by
\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
and
\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
(this is consistent with definition
2.2.21
).
Their derived categories are denoted
D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
and
D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
.

We define a functor
f_{n}^{*}:D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))\rightarrow D(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
,
f_{n}^{*}M=M\otimes_{({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}^{+})_{\blacksquare}
. This functor
has a right adjoint given by the forgetful functor
(f_{n})_{*}:D(\mathcal{Z}_{n})\rightarrow D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
.
These functors induce adjoint functors
f^{*}:D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))\rightarrow D(\mathcal{Z})
and
f_{*}:D(\mathcal{Z})\rightarrow D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
.

We finally define the finite slope functor:

(-)^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}=f_{*}f^{*}:D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))\rightarrow D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})).

The unit of adjunction
gives a natural map
M\rightarrow M^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
in
D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
. Note that
M^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}=\lim_{n}(f_{n})_{*}f_{n}^{*}M
.

Remark 4.6.47
.

The finite slope functor is a localization functor, which factors over the functor
-\otimes_{({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]
.
However, it is a stronger form of localization. For example, let us
(abusively!) consider the case that
T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}
.
In this case,
({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X]
and
({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\blacksquare}[T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})]={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X,X^{-1}]
. We can also suppose that
\mathcal{Z}_{n}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle,{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}\langle p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle)
. We claim that the module
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}((X))
is a solid
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X,X^{-1}]
-module, whose finite slope part is trivial. By definition we need to show that
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}((X))\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}[X]}{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}\langle p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle=0
for every
n
.
To see this, we first show note that
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}((X))
is a solid
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{-s}X\rangle
for every
s\geq 0
. Indeed
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X]/(X^{\ell})
is a
solid
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{-s}X\rangle
-module for any
\ell,s\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}

and so
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\llbracket X\rrbracket=\lim_{\ell}{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X]/(X^{\ell})
is also a
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{-s}X\rangle
-module, and thus
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}((X))
is a solid
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{-s}X\rangle
-module, as claimed. It remains to observe that if
s\geq n
, then
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{-s}X\rangle\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X]}{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle=0
. To see this, note that this is represented by the following complex:

[{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle U,p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{s-n}Up^{n}X^{-1}-1}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\langle U,p^{n}X,p^{n}X^{-1}\rangle].

But
1-p^{s-n}U(p^{n}X^{-1})
is invertible, with inverse
\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(p^{s-n}U(p^{n}X^{-1}))^{\ell}
.

Sometimes, one wants to consider not only the finite slope part, but to specify
the slope. For any rank one point
\mathrm{Spa}(C,{\mathcal{O}}_{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}

corresponding to a character
\chi:T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\rightarrow C^{\times}
, we define
the slope of
\chi
as follows. Let
v:C\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
be the valuation, normalized by
v(p)=1
. Composing the valuation and

\chi
we obtain a map
v(\chi):T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}
. On the other
hand, via the exact sequence (
1.8.6
) we can think of
v(\chi)
as an element of
X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{R}}
(see also
[
BP21
, Sect. 5.9]
).
This defines a continuous “slope” map
s:\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{R}}
(which factors through the Berkovich space of
{\mathcal{Z}}
).

Remark 4.6.48
.

If (again abusively) we consider the case
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}
, then
\mathcal{Z}=\mathbf{G}_{m}^{an}
, and
s:\mathbf{G}_{m}^{an}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}
extends the
p
-adic valuation on classical rigid analytic points.

We have a partial order relation on
X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{R}}
where
\lambda\geq\lambda^{\prime}
if
\lambda(v(t))-\lambda^{\prime}(v(t))\geq 0
for any
t\in T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. For any
\lambda\in X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{{\mathbf{Q}}}}
, we can consider the cone
\{\lambda^{\prime}\in X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{R}},\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\}
.
The subset
s^{-1}(\{\lambda^{\prime}\in X^{*}(T^{d})_{\mathbf{R}},\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\})
is the closure of a unique rational open that we denote by
\mathcal{Z}_{\leq\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{\lambda}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{Z}
.
We have functors

f_{\leq\lambda}^{*}:D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))\rightarrow D(\mathcal{Z}_{\leq\lambda})
,
f_{\leq\lambda}^{*}M=i_{\lambda}^{*}f^{*}M
. We also have a forgetful functor
(f_{\leq\lambda})_{*}:D(\mathcal{Z}_{\leq\lambda})\rightarrow D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
.
We finally define the slope
\leq\lambda
functor:

(-)^{\leq\lambda}=(f_{\leq\lambda})_{*}f_{\leq\lambda}^{*}:D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))\rightarrow D(\Mod_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})).

If
M\in D(\Mod_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}))
, then we have
\lim_{\lambda}M^{\leq\lambda}=M^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
.
We leave it to the reader to define the slope
\lambda
functor
M\mapsto M^{=\lambda}
and the slope
\geq\lambda
functor,
M\mapsto M^{\geq\lambda}
.

Remark 4.6.49
.

Let us assume that
M
is a Banach
space equipped with a compact endomorphism
X
. In other words, we are
(again, abusively) in the situation
T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})={\mathbf{Z}}
, and
1\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
acts like
X
. It follows from
[
Ser62
, Prop. 12]
that for any
\lambda\in\mathbf{{\mathbf{Q}}}
,
M^{\leq\lambda}
is a direct summand of
M
and is finite dimensional. In particular,
f^{*}M
defines a coherent sheaf on
\mathcal{Z}
and
M^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
is a pro-finite vector space.

4.6.50.
Hecke algebra action

Let
K_{U}
be a compact open subgroup of
U({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\subseteq B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
which
admits an Iwahori factorization. Let

\lambda\in X^{*}(T)_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
. For any
M\in\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)
, we consider the
submodule
M^{K_{U}}
of
K_{U}
-invariants. It is canonically a direct summand, since one
can define a trace
\mathrm{Tr}_{K_{U}}:M\rightarrow M^{K_{U}}
. Indeed, we have

M=\operatorname{colim}_{K^{\prime}}M^{K^{\prime}}
where
K^{\prime}
runs through the compact open subgroups of

U({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. Then for any
K_{U}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{U}
we can define a normalized
trace

\operatorname{Tr}_{K^{\prime}_{U}/K_{U}}=\frac{1}{[K_{U}:K_{U}^{\prime}]}\left(\sum_{k\in K_{U}/K^{\prime}_{U}}k\right):M^{K^{\prime}_{U}}\rightarrow M^{K_{U}},

and passing to the inductive limit
over
\underline{K}_{U}
yields the map
\mathrm{Tr}_{K_{U}}
.

Let
t\in T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. We define an action of
t
on
M^{K_{U}}
as follows:

M^{K_{U}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{tK_{U}t^{-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{Tr}_{tK_{U}t^{-1}/K_{U}}}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{K_{U}}

Lemma 4.6.51
.

The above rule defines an action of the commutative monoid
T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
on
M^{K_{U}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Cas
, Lem. 4.1.5]
.
∎

We deduce that we have an exact functor

(-)^{K_{U}}:D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))\rightarrow D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E)).

Lemma 4.6.52
.

Let
K^{\prime}_{U}\subseteq K_{U}
. We have a natural transformation

(-)^{K^{\prime}_{U}}\Rightarrow(-)^{K_{U}}
, induced by the trace.

Proof.

We consider the map
\mathrm{Tr}_{K^{\prime}_{U}/K_{U}}:M^{K^{\prime}_{U}}\rightarrow M^{K_{U}}
.
It is elementary to check that this map commutes with the action of
t
.
∎

We can now consider the composite functor:

(-)^{K_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}:D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{B({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)^{K_{U}}}}{{\rightarrow}}D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)^{f\kern-0.44165pt{s}}}}{{\rightarrow}}D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

Lemma 4.6.53
.

Let
K^{\prime}_{U}\subseteq K_{U}
. The natural transformation
(-)^{K^{\prime}_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}\Rightarrow(-)^{K_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We pick
t\in T^{++}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
such that
tK_{U}t^{-1}\subseteq K^{\prime}_{U}
.
We have a commutative diagram (where the vertical maps are trace maps, and the
diagonal map is the composite
M^{K_{U}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{tK_{U}t^{-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{Tr}_{tK_{U}t^{-1}/K^{\prime}_{U}}}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{K_{U}}
):

\textstyle{M^{K_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{M^{K_{U}}}
\textstyle{M^{K^{\prime}_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{M^{K^{\prime}_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

On the finite slope quotient, the horizontal maps
t
are isomorphisms, showing that the maps
M^{K^{\prime}_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}\rightarrow M^{K_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
are isomorphisms.
∎

In view of this lemma, we often simply write
M^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
instead of
M^{K_{U},f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
, as the choice of
K_{U}
is irrelevant.

4.6.54.
The finite slope part of the higher Coleman functor

We define the finite slope part of the higher Coleman functor:

\displaystyle HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{w,\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M)))^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}

\displaystyle HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{\mathrm{cusp},w,\lambda}:{\mathcal{O}}_{\lambda-\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle D(\Mod^{\lambda-\mathrm{sm}}_{T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(E))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},VB^{0}(HCS_{w,\lambda}(M)(-D)))^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}

4.6.55.
Comparison with higher Coleman theory
[
BP21
]

Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)^{M,+}
and
\chi:T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
be a finite order character. We have defined in
[
BP21
, Sect. 5]
cohomology theories
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,\mathrm{cusp})^{+,f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}
.

Theorem 4.6.56
.

We have canonical isomorphisms of smooth
T({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-modules (where the decomposition on the right hand side corresponds to the decomposition into isotypic parts for the action of
T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})
):

\displaystyle HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{\mathrm{cusp},0,w}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oplus_{\chi:T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,\mathrm{cusp})^{+,f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}

\displaystyle HC^{f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}_{0,w}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oplus_{\chi:T({\mathbf{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,f\kern-0.49164pt{s}}

Proof.

By Lemma
4.6.5
, we have
VB^{0}\circ HCS_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})=\omega^{\kappa,sm}|_{\pi_{HT}^{-1}(C_{w}^{\dagger})}
, so that by definition we have
HC_{w,0}(L(\mathfrak{m}_{w})_{-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\kappa})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}},\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}})
.
Let
Z_{0}
be a closed neighborhood of
w
in
C_{w}
stable under a compact open
subgroup
K_{U}\subseteq U({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
(admitting an Iwahori factorization) and let
U_{0}
be an open neighborhood of
Z_{0}
in
C_{w}
. We let
F_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(Z_{0})
and
V_{0}=\pi_{HT}^{-1}(U_{0})
. We can define an action of
t\in T^{+}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
on

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}})
as follows (where the second
and third maps are respectively given by restriction and the trace):

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}}(V_{0},\mathscr{F})^{K_{U}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{F_{0}t^{-1}}(V_{0}t^{-1},\omega^{\kappa,\mathrm{sm}})^{tK_{U}t^{-1}}\rightarrow
