-algebra. Furthermore,
if for each
v\in T\smallsetminus S_{2}
the group
U_{v}
is sufficiently
deep in the sense of Definition
5.7.13
,
then the morphism

R^{T,\ord}\to\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}))_{\mathfrak{m}}
given by (
5.7.10
) has nilpotent kernel, i.e.

(R^{T,\ord})^{\operatorname{red}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}))_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Remark 5.7.15
.

The assumption in Theorem
5.7.14
(
5
) that

\overline{\rho}(G_{F})
contains a regular semi-simple element is used in
order to ensure that the automorphic forms that we consider are
of neat level.

Proof of Theorem
5.7.14
.

We will begin by making a succession of solvable extensions of
CM fields to put ourselves into a situation where we can apply
the Taylor–Wiles patching method. In order to keep the notation
compatible with that above we will continue to denote our CM
field by
F
until the end of the argument, where we will descend
to our original
F
.

We can
and do replace
F
with a solvable extension (and replace
T
with
the set of places lying over places in
T
) and enlarge our coefficient field
E
so that in addition
to the hypotheses of the theorem, we have:

•

F=F^{+}(\sqrt{-1})
.

•

if
v\in T
then
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}}
is trivial.

•

if
v\in S_{2}
then
[F_{v}^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}]>n(n-1)/2+1
.

•

if
v\in T\smallsetminus S_{2}
then:

–

\pi_{\widetilde{v}}^{\mathrm{Iw}({\widetilde{v}})}\neq 0
.

–

if
2^{N}\|(q_{v}-1)
then
2^{N}>n
and
{\mathcal{O}}
contains
a
2^{N}
th root of unity.

(Note that if
(F^{\prime})^{+}/F^{+}
is a solvable extension of totally
real fields then
(F^{\prime})^{+}F/F
is a solvable extension of CM fields, so we
can choose a solvable CM extension to realize any finite set of
local extensions. All of these conditions are local except for
the first condition that
F=F^{+}(\sqrt{-1})
. Since arranging this
only involves a quadratic extension, and
\overline{\rho}(G_{F})
has no
normal subgroups of index
2
by the assumption that it is nearly
adequate (which requires in particular that

H^{1}(\overline{\rho}(G_{F}),k)=0
), this quadratic extension
leaves
\overline{\rho}(G_{F})
unchanged.) Choose a finite
place
v_{1}\notin T
of
F^{+}
which splits in
F

as
v={\widetilde{v}}_{1}{\widetilde{v}}_{1}^{c}
, for which

\overline{\rho}(\mathrm{Frob}_{{\widetilde{v}}_{1}})
is regular semi-simple. (There are
infinitely many such
v_{1}
by our assumption (
5
).)

We replace
T
by
T\cup\{v_{1}\}
, and
write
T=S_{2}\sqcup\{v_{1}\}\sqcup R
. Note in particular that
Hypotheses
5.7.1

and
5.7.5
hold. For each
v\in R
we choose pairwise distinct characters

\chi_{v,1},\dots,\chi_{v,n}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
which become trivial after reduction modulo

\varpi
. (We can do this by the conditions arranged in our
initial base change.) We have the global deformation problem

{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}
defined in (
5.7.6
), and
we write
{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}
for the global deformation problem
defined in the same way but with all of the
characters
\chi_{v,i}
replaced by the trivial character. By the
definitions of the local deformation problems
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\chi_{v}}

for
v\in R
, we can fix compatible isomorphisms
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}/\varpi\cong R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}/\varpi
and
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}/\varpi\cong R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}/\varpi
.

We now specify open compact subgroups
U_{v}\subset G(F_{v}^{+})
as follows:

(1)

U_{v}=G({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}})
if
v\not\in T
is split in
F
.

(2)

U_{v}
is a hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
G(F^{+}_{v})
if
v
is inert in
F
.

(3)

U_{v}=\mathrm{Iw}({\widetilde{v}})
for
v\in R
.

(4)

U_{v_{1}}
is any torsion-free compact open subgroup
of
G(F^{+}_{v_{1}})
.

(Note in particular that the choice of
U_{v_{1}}
means that for
any
b,c
, the group
U(\mathfrak{l}^{b,c})
is sufficiently small in
the sense of Definition
5.7.2
. While we will not
explicitly use this below, it is implicitly used multiple times,
ultimately in the form of
[
Ger19
, Lem. 2.6]
.)

Since by assumption
\pi
is ordinary and unramified outside
of
R\cup S_{2}
, and since
\pi_{\widetilde{v}}^{\mathrm{Iw}({\widetilde{v}})}\neq 0
for all
v\in R
,
there is a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{1}
of

\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})
with residue field
k

such that
\overline{\rho}\cong\overline{r}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}|_{G_{F}}
. (As ever, we feel free to
enlarge
{\mathcal{O}}
if necessary.) Since the

\chi_{v}
are trivial modulo
\varpi
, we have

S^{\ord}_{0,\{\chi_{v}\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),k)=S^{\ord}_{0,\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),k),

(5.7.16)

so
\mathfrak{m}_{1}
induces a
unique maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\chi}
of
\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})
. After conjugating
we can and do assume that
\overline{r}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\chi}}=\overline{r}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}=\overline{\rho}
.

Write

H_{\chi}:=S^{\ord}_{0,\{\chi_{v}\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),E/{\mathcal{O}})^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{m}_{\chi}},\quad H_{1}:=S^{\ord}_{0,\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),E/{\mathcal{O}})^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}.

By
[
Ger19
, Cor. 2.21]
,
H_{1}

is a faithful
\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
-module, and is in particular
an
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
-module via (
5.7.10
).
Similarly
H_{\chi}
is an
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}
-module. By (
5.7.16
) we have a natural
isomorphism

H_{\chi}/\varpi\cong H_{1}/\varpi,

which is
compatible with the isomorphism
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}/\varpi\cong R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}/\varpi
.

Write
q=h^{1}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\perp},T}-1
and
g=q+\#T-1-[F^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}]n(n-1)/2
. Write

S_{\infty}:=\Lambda\llbracket X_{1},\cdots,X_{q+(n^{2}+1)\#T-1}\rrbracket

with augmentation ideal

\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{q+(n^{2}+1)\#T-1})
. (The
number of formal variables here is given by the number of
Taylor–Wiles primes plus the relative dimension of
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}

over
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}
.) We
set

R_{\chi,\infty}:=R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}\llbracket Y_{1},\dots,Y_{g}\rrbracket,\quad R_{1,\infty}:=R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}\llbracket Y_{1},\dots,Y_{g}\rrbracket.

By (
5.7.8
) we have

\displaystyle\dim R_{\chi,\infty}=\dim R_{1,\infty}

\displaystyle=1+n^{2}\#T+[F^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}]n(n+1)/2+g

(5.7.17)

\displaystyle=[F^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}]n+(n^{2}+1)\#T+q

\displaystyle=\dim S_{\infty}.

Using Proposition
5.5.6
in place of
[
Tho12
, Prop. 4.4]
, a
standard patching argument exactly as in the proof of
[
Tho12
, Thm. 8.6]
provides us with the following:

•

\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
-homomorphisms
S_{\infty}\to R_{1,\infty}
,

S_{\infty}\to R_{\chi,\infty}
.

•

An
R_{1,\infty}
-module
H_{1,\infty}
, and an

R_{\chi,\infty}
-module
H_{\chi,\infty}
, each of which is
free of finite rank over
S_{\infty}
.

•

A surjection of
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}
-algebras

R_{1,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
, which factors through
a
\Lambda
-algebra map

R_{1,\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
; and
similarly, a surjection of

R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}
-algebras

R_{\chi,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}
, which factors
through a
\Lambda
-algebra map

R_{\chi,\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}
.

•

Isomorphisms
H_{1,\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}\cong H_{1}
,
H_{\chi,\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}\cong H_{\chi}
compatible with the surjections
R_{1,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
,
R_{\chi,\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{\chi_{v}\}}}
.

•

Compatible identifications of all the above data for
1
and
for
\chi
after reducing modulo
\varpi
.

In particular since
H_{1,\infty}
is a finite free
S_{\infty}
-module,
we deduce from (
5.7.17
)
that

\depth_{R_{1,\infty}}H_{1,\infty}\geq\depth_{S_{\infty}}H_{1,\infty}=\dim S_{\infty}=\dim R_{1,\infty},

whence
\depth_{R_{1,\infty}}H_{1,\infty}=\dim R_{1,\infty}
, and the
support of
H_{1,\infty}
in
\Spec R_{1,\infty}
is a union of
irreducible components (see
[
Tay08
, Lem. 2.3]
). Similarly, the support of
H_{\chi,\infty}
in
\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}
is a union of
irreducible components.

We now examine the irreducible components of
\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}

and
\Spec R_{1,\infty}
. Bearing in mind
Propositions
5.6.4
and
5.6.6
, an identical argument to the proof of
[
BCG+25
, Lem. 3.2.4]
shows that for each minimal prime
Q

of
\Lambda
, we have the following properties.

(1)

The generic points of
R_{\chi,\infty}/Q
and
R_{1,\infty}/Q
all
have characteristic
0
.

(2)

The irreducible components
{\mathcal{C}}
of
\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}/Q
and

\Spec R_{1,\infty}/Q
biject with the products of the
corresponding sets of irreducible components
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
of the local
deformation rings for
v\in R\cup\{v_{1}\}
.

(3)

The irreducible components
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
of
\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}/(Q,\varpi)=\Spec R_{1,\infty}/(Q,\varpi)
biject with the products
over
T
of the corresponding sets of the irreducible components
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{v}
of
the special fibres of the deformation rings for
v\in R\cup\{v_{1}\}
.

In view of these statements we will use the notation
{\mathcal{C}}=\otimes_{v\in R\cup\{v_{1}\}}{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}=\otimes_{v\in R\cup\{v_{1}\}}\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{v}
.

(4)

The irreducible components of

\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}/Q
biject with the irreducible components of
\Spec R_{v_{1}}^{\square}
.

(5)

For each irreducible
component
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}=\otimes_{v\in R\cup\{v_{1}\}}\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}_{v}
of

\Spec R_{1,\infty}/(Q,\varpi)
, there are irreducible
components
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
of
\Spec R_{v}^{1}
for
v\in R
, and irreducible
components
{\mathcal{C}}_{v_{1}}^{1},\dots{\mathcal{C}}_{v_{1}}^{s}
of

\Spec R_{v_{1}}^{\square}
(for some
s\geq 1
), such that the
irreducible components of
\Spec R_{1,\infty}/Q

generalizing
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
are precisely the
s
components

{\mathcal{C}}_{v_{1}}^{i}\otimes_{v\in R}{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
.

Fix for the moment a minimal prime
Q
of
\Lambda
. The existence of
\pi

implies that the support of
H_{1,\infty}
in
\Spec R_{1,\infty}
is
nonempty. Using the comparison modulo
\varpi
, the same is true of
the support of
H_{\chi,\infty}
in
\Spec R_{\chi,\infty}
. By points
(
4
) and (
5
), we conclude that for each set
X
of irreducible
components
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
of
\Spec R_{v}^{1}
for
v\in R
, we can choose an
irreducible component
{\mathcal{C}}_{X,v_{1}}
of
\Spec R_{v_{1}}^{\square}
such
that
{\mathcal{C}}_{X,v_{1}}\otimes_{v\in R,{\mathcal{C}}_{v}\in X}{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
is in the
support of
H_{1,\infty}
. This choice of irreducible components
corresponds to a quotient
R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{X}
of
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}
, and if we
set

R_{X}:=R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}\otimes_{R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T}}R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{X},

then
\Spec R_{X}\subset\Spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
is contained in
the support of
H_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
; equivalently,
\Spec R_{X}
is contained
in the support of
H_{1}
in
\Spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}
.

In particular
(R_{X})^{\operatorname{red}}
is a quotient of a Hecke algebra

\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
, so it is
a finite
\Lambda
-algebra, so that
R_{X}
itself is a
finite
\Lambda
-algebra. (To see this, note by the topological
Nakayama lemma it suffices to observe
R_{X}/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
is Noetherian
and zero-dimensional, thus finite.) Since
R_{X}
has dimension at
least
\dim\Lambda
by Lemma
5.7.7
, we see that the morphism
\Spec R_{X}\to\Spec\Lambda/Q

is dominant. In particular, we can choose a weight
\lambda
such that

R_{X}/\wp_{\lambda}R_{X}
is a nonzero finite
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra of
dimension at least
1
, and thus has a
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{2}
-point. Since

(R_{X})^{\operatorname{red}}
is a quotient of

\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
, it
follows from (
5.7.4
) that the Galois representation
corresponding to this
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{2}
-point comes from an ordinary
RACSDC representation of weight
\lambda
.

Repeating this construction for all choices of
Q
, we conclude that
for each choice set
Y
of irreducible components
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
for
v\in R\cup S_{2}
, there is an ordinary RACSDC automorphic
representation
\pi_{Y}
of
\GL_{n}/F
such that:

•

\overline{r}_{\pi_{Y},2}\cong\overline{\rho}
,

•

r_{\pi_{Y},2}|_{G_{F_{w}}}
is unramified for all places
w
not
lying over a place in
T
,

•

and for each
v\in R\cup S_{2}
, the representation

r_{\pi_{Y},2}|_{G_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}}
lies on
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
and on no other
irreducible component (by the genericity of
\pi_{Y,v}
,
see
[
BLGGT14
, Lem. 1.3.2(1)]
).

By Lemma
5.6.3
,
we can and do choose a solvable CM extension
L/F
, linearly disjoint
from
\overline{F}^{\ker\overline{\rho}}
over
F
, with the following
property: for any
Y
as above, and any place
w_{1}
of
L^{+}
lying
over
v_{1}
, the representation

r_{\pi_{Y},2}|_{G_{F_{\widetilde{w}_{1}}}}
is
unramified (where we write
w_{1}=\widetilde{w}_{1}\widetilde{w}_{1}^{c}
).

We now repeat the patching argument above with
F
replaced
by
L
. More precisely, we:

•

replace
R
by the set
R^{\prime}
of places of
L
lying over places
in
R
;

•

choose a place
v_{1}^{\prime}\notin R^{\prime}\cup S_{2}
of
L^{+}
splitting in
L

as
{\widetilde{v}}_{1}^{\prime}({\widetilde{v}}_{1}^{\prime})^{c}
, with
\overline{\rho}(\mathrm{Frob}_{{\widetilde{v}}_{1}^{\prime}})
being regular
semi-simple;

•

and replace
T
by
T^{\prime}=R^{\prime}\cup\{v_{1}^{\prime}\}
.

Writing
H_{1}^{\prime}
,
R_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
for the corresponding objects
over
L
, we find in particular that we have the patched
module
H_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
, whose support in
\Spec R_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
is a
union of irreducible components. Again, we write these irreducible
components as
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}=\otimes_{v^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}
, and for each set
Y

as above we let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{Y}
denote the irreducible component determined
by letting
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{v_{1}^{\prime}}
be the (unique) unramified component of

\Spec R_{v_{1}^{\prime}}^{\square}
, and letting
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}
for

v^{\prime}|v\in R\cup S_{2}
be the image of the component
{\mathcal{C}}_{v}
for
Y
(via
the natural morphism
\Spec R_{v}^{\square}\to\Spec R_{v^{\prime}}^{\square}
).

By considering the base changes to
L
of the
\pi_{Y}
, we see that
each component
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{Y}
is in the support of
H_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
. The
union of the irreducible components
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{Y}
corresponds to a
quotient
R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}
of
R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}},T^{\prime}}
, and as
above, if we
set

R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}:=R^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\{1\}}}\otimes_{R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\{1\}},T^{\prime}}}R^{\operatorname{loc}}_{L/F,T^{\prime}},

then
\Spec R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}\subset\Spec R^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\{1\}}}
is contained
in the support of
H_{1,\infty}^{\prime}
; equivalently,
\Spec R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}

is contained in the support of
H^{\prime}_{1}
in
\Spec R^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\{1\}}}
. Thus
(R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}})^{\operatorname{red}}
is a quotient of a
Hecke algebra

\mathbf{T}^{T^{\prime},\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
, and in
particular every homomorphism
R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}
factors
through
\mathbf{T}^{T^{\prime},\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
. Furthermore, it follows as above that
R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}

is a finite
\Lambda^{\prime}
-algebra.

We now return to the original situation of the statement of the
theorem (so
F
is now the CM field that we started with, before we
made any base changes, and
T
is as in the statement of the
theorem). By the choice of
L
, we have a commutative
diagram

\textstyle{\Lambda^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{T,\ord}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}))_{\mathfrak{m}}}

The
morphism
R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}}\to R^{T,\ord}
is finite, by the obvious
generalization of
[
BLGGT14
, Lem. 1.2.3(1)]
to the case
p=2
,
which has an identical proof up to replacing the appeal to
[
CHT08
, Lem. 2.1.12]
with a citation of
[
Tho17
, Lem. 2.4]
. It
follows that
R^{T,\ord}
is a finite
\Lambda
-algebra, as
claimed.

Suppose now that the
U_{v}
for
v\in T\smallsetminus S_{2}
are sufficiently
deep. Since every irreducible component of
\Spec R^{T,\ord}
has dimension
at least that of
\Spec\Lambda
by Lemma
5.7.7
, it follows that each irreducible component
dominates an irreducible component of
\Spec\Lambda
. It follows that
the set of points
\Spec\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{2}\to\Spec R^{T,\ord}
which lie over
points of
\Lambda
given by the primes
\wp_{\lambda}
is dense
in
\Spec R^{T,\ord}
. It remains to show that each such point is in

\Spec\mathbf{T}^{T,\ord}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}))_{\mathfrak{m}}
. By (
5.7.4
) and the choice of
U
, it is enough to check that the
corresponding Galois representations are automorphic. By solvable base
change, it is enough to check this after restriction to
G_{L}
, where
it follows from another application of (
5.7.4
)
(and the observation above that
(R^{\prime}_{L/F,T^{\prime}})^{\operatorname{red}}
is a quotient
of
\mathbf{T}^{T^{\prime},\ord}_{\{1\}}(U(\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}),{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
).
∎

6.
Ordinary modularity lifting theorems for
\GSp_{4}
:
preliminaries

The goal of this section is — in part — to prove
an ordinary modularity lifting
theorem for
\GSp_{4}
for
p\geq 3
over totally real fields
in which
p
splits completely. Under suitable Taylor–Wiles hypotheses,
this can be used to show that
p
-adic Galois representations
coming from ordinary abelian surfaces give rise to quotients
of a certain
p
-adic Hecke algebra, but does not yet show that such classes are classical.
The modularity lifting theorem we prove in this section is
(in the language of
[
CG18
]
) of
l_{0}=0

type rather than
l_{0}>0
type, and so is precisely
amenable to the usual Taylor–Wiles method.
Under a stronger hypothesis (that
\overline{\rho}
is residually

p
-distinguished) our results are actually directly contained
in
[
BCGP21
]
(although that paper is generally concerned with the more subtle
l_{0}=1
situation),
and versions of this theorem go back as far as
[
Pil12
]
. The methods we use here follow along generally
similar grounds, with some important technical improvements
due in several cases to Whitmore
[
Whi22
]
.

In §
6.1
, we recall some general constructions and notation
for
\GSp_{4}
-deformation problems.
In §
6.2
, we introduce the corresponding ordinary local
deformation rings and study their local properties. In §
6.3
, we carry out the Taylor–Wiles argument
(in part following
[
BCGP21
]
and
[
Whi22
]
). Finally, in §
6.4
,
we explicitly analyze the subgroups of
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
which
satisfy our running collection of “big–image” conditions.

6.1.
Notation and definitions

We now turn to modularity lifting theorems for
\GSp_{4}
. Our
arguments have relatively little
direct
overlap with those of our earlier paper

[
BCGP21
]
, although we will occasionally make references to
it. In particular, in order to avoid confusing clashes of notation with our results
for unitary groups in Section
5
, we continue to write
F^{+}

for a totally real field (whereas totally real fields were denoted
F

in
[
BCGP21
]
).

Accordingly we let
F^{+}
denote a totally real field, and write
S_{p}
for the set of places of

F^{+}
above
p
. We fix a continuous absolutely
irreducible homomorphism
\overline{\rho}:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}(k)
with
similitude
\overline{\varepsilon}^{-1}
.
When
\overline{\rho}
is explicitly considered as a symplectic representation
(as in this section), we
denote by
\operatorname{ad}\overline{\rho}
and
\operatorname{ad}^{0}\overline{\rho}
the adjoint
G_{F^{+}}

action with respect to
\GSp_{4}
and
\Sp_{4}
respectively (so
\dim\operatorname{ad}\overline{\rho}=11

and
\dim\operatorname{ad}^{0}\overline{\rho}=10
). We warn the reader that there is some tension in
this definition
with the notation of §
5
where
\operatorname{ad}\overline{r}
denotes the adjoint action
with respect to
\GL_{n}
; we hope the precise meaning will always be clear from context.

Let
S
be a finite set of finite places of
F^{+}
containing
S_{p}

and
all places at which
\overline{\rho}
is ramified. We write
F^{+}_{S}
for the
maximal subextension of
\overline{F^{+}}/F^{+}
which is unramified outside

S
, and write
G_{F^{+},S}
for
\Gal(F^{+}_{S}/F^{+})
. For each
v\in S
, we fix

\Lambda_{v}\in\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
, and set

\Lambda=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S}\Lambda_{v}
, where the completed
tensor product is taken over
{\mathcal{O}}
. Then
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
is a
subcategory of
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda_{v}}
for each
v\in S
, via the canonical
map
\Lambda_{v}\to\Lambda
.

Definition 6.1.1
.

A
lift
, also called a
lifting
, of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}

is a continuous homomorphism

\rho:G_{F^{+}_{v}}\to\GSp_{4}(A)
to a

\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda_{v}}
-algebra
A
such that

\rho\bmod\mathfrak{m}_{A}=\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
and

\nu\circ\rho=\varepsilon^{-1}
.

We let
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square}
denote the set-valued functor on
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda_{v}}
that sends
A
to
the set of lifts of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
to
A
. This functor is
representable, and we denote the representing object by

R_{v}^{\square}
. We can identify
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square}(k[\epsilon])
with the group of
1-cocycles
Z^{1}(F_{v},\operatorname{ad}^{0}\overline{\rho})

by associating a cocycle
\phi
to
the lifting given
by

\rho(\sigma)=(1+\epsilon\phi(\sigma))\overline{\rho}(\sigma).

Note that
two such liftings are
\widehat{\GSp_{4}}(k[\epsilon])
-conjugate if and only if the
images of the corresponding 1-cocycles in
H^{1}(F_{v},\operatorname{ad}\overline{\rho})
are equal.

Definition 6.1.2
.

A
local deformation problem
for
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
is a subfunctor
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
of
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square}
satisfying the following:

•

{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
is represented by a quotient
R_{v}
of
R_{v}^{\square}
.

•

For all
A\in\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda_{v}}
,
\rho\in{\mathcal{D}}_{v}(A)
, and
a\in\widehat{\GSp}_{4}(A)
,
we have
a\rho a^{-1}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{v}(A)
.

Definition 6.1.3
.

A
global deformation problem
is a tuple

{\mathcal{S}}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{\Lambda_{v}\}_{v\in S},\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\}_{v\in S})

where:

•

\overline{\rho}
,
S
,
\{\Lambda_{v}\}_{v\in S}
are as
above.

•

For each
v\in S
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
is a local deformation problem for
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
.

As in the local case, a
lift
(or
lifting
) of
\overline{\rho}

is a continuous homomorphism
\rho:G_{F^{+},S}\to\GSp_{4}(A)

to a
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
-algebra
A
, such that

\rho\bmod\mathfrak{m}_{A}=\overline{\rho}
and
\nu\circ\rho=\varepsilon^{-1}
. We say that
two lifts
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}:G_{F^{+},S}\to\GSp_{4}(A)
are

strictly equivalent
if there is an

a\in\widehat{\GSp}_{4}(A)
such that

\rho_{2}=a\rho_{1}a^{-1}
. A
deformation of
\overline{\rho}
is a
strict equivalence class of lifts of
\overline{\rho}
.

For a global deformation problem

{\mathcal{S}}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{\Lambda_{v}\}_{v\in S},\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\}_{v\in S})

we say that a lift
\rho:G_{F^{+},S}\to\GSp_{4}(A)
is of
type
{\mathcal{S}}

if
\rho|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{v}(A)
for each
v\in S
. If
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
are strictly equivalent
lifts of
\overline{\rho}
, and
\rho_{1}
is of type
{\mathcal{S}}
, then so is

\rho_{2}
. A
deformation of type
{\mathcal{S}}
is a strict
equivalence class of lifts of type
{\mathcal{S}}
, and we denote by

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
the set-valued functor that takes a

\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
-algebra
A
to the set of lifts

\rho:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}(A)
of type
{\mathcal{S}}
.

Given a subset
T\subseteq S
, a
T
-framed lift of type

{\mathcal{S}}
is a tuple
(\rho,\{\gamma_{v}\}_{v\in T})
, where
\rho
is
a lift of type
{\mathcal{S}}
, and

\gamma_{v}\in\widehat{\GSp}_{4}(A)
for each
v\in T
. We
say that two
T
-framed lifts
(\rho_{1},\{\gamma_{v}\}_{v\in T})
and

(\rho_{2},\{\gamma^{\prime}_{v}\}_{v\in T})
to a
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
-algebra
A
are
strictly equivalent if there is an
a\in\widehat{\GSp}_{4}(A)

such that
\rho_{2}=a\rho_{1}a^{-1}
, and
\gamma^{\prime}_{v}=a\gamma_{v}
for
each
v\in T
. A strict equivalence class of
T
-framed lifts of
type
{\mathcal{S}}
is called a
T
-framed deformation of type
{\mathcal{S}}
.
We denote by
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
the set valued functor that sends a

\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
-algebra
A
to the set of
T
-framed deformations to

A
of type
{\mathcal{S}}
.

The functors
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
are representable (as we are assuming
that
\overline{\rho}
is absolutely irreducible), and we denote their
representing objects by
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}
and
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}

respectively. Assume now that
T
is chosen so that
\Lambda_{v}={\mathcal{O}}
for all
v\in S\smallsetminus T
. Write
R_{v}
for the
representing object of
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
,
and define
R_{{\mathcal{S}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in T}R_{v}
, with the completed tensor product being taken over
{\mathcal{O}}
.
It is canonically a
\Lambda
-algebra, via the canonical isomorphism
\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in T}\Lambda_{v}\cong\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S}\Lambda_{v}
.
For each
v\in T
, the natural transformation
\mathrm{Def}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{v}
given by
(\rho,\{\alpha_{v}\}_{v\in T})\mapsto\alpha_{v}^{-1}\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}\alpha_{v}

induces a morphism
R_{v}\rightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
in
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda_{v}}
.
We thus have a morphism
R_{{\mathcal{S}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}\rightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
in
\operatorname{CNL}_{\Lambda}
.

If
T
is
empty, then
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}=R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
, and otherwise the natural
map
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
is formally smooth of relative
dimension
11\#T-1
. Indeed
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
is
a torsor under

(\prod_{v\in T}\widehat{\GSp}_{4})/\widehat{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
.

Definition 6.1.4
.

Let

{\mathcal{T}}:=\Lambda\llbracket x_{1},\dots,x_{11\#T-1}\rrbracket

be the coordinate ring of

(\prod_{v\in T}\widehat{\GSp}_{4})/\widehat{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
over

\Lambda
.

The choice of a representative

\rho_{{\mathcal{S}}}\colon G_{F}\rightarrow\GSp_{4}(R_{{\mathcal{S}}})
for the
universal type
{\mathcal{S}}
deformation determines a splitting of the torsor
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
and a canonical
isomorphism

R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\cong R_{{\mathcal{S}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\Lambda}{\mathcal{T}}.

(6.1.5)

The following lemma and its proof are standard, but we include them in
order to reassure the reader that they remain valid for
p=2
.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

Suppose that
q_{v}\equiv 1\bmod p
, and that

\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
is unramified, with
\overline{\rho}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})
being regular
semi-simple with
(
ordered
)
eigenvalues
\overline{\alpha}_{v,1}
,
\overline{\alpha}_{v,2}
,

\overline{\alpha}_{v,2}^{-1}
,
\overline{\alpha}_{4}=\overline{\alpha}_{v,1}^{-1}
. Let
\rho:G_{F^{+}_{v}}\rightarrow\GSp_{4}(A)
be any lift of

\overline{\rho}
.

Then there are unique continuous characters

\gamma_{i}:G_{F^{+}_{v}}\rightarrow A^{\times}
for
i=1,2
, such that

\rho
is
\GSp_{4}(A)
-conjugate to a lift of the form

\gamma_{1}\oplus\gamma_{2}\oplus\gamma_{2}^{-1}\varepsilon^{-1}\oplus\gamma_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{-1},

where

(\gamma_{i}\bmod\mathfrak{m}_{A})(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})=\overline{\alpha}_{v,i}
for each

i=1,2
.

Proof.

Let
\phi
be a lifting of
\mathrm{Frob}_{v}

to
G_{F^{+}_{v}}
. Then
\rho(\phi)
is regular semi-simple, so is
contained in
T(A)
for a unique torus
T
, and we need to show that
for each
\sigma\in I_{F^{+}_{v}}
, we have
\rho(\sigma)\in T(A)
. To
do this we will prove by induction that for each
n\geq 1
we
have

(\rho\bmod\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{n})(\sigma)\in T(A/\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{n}),

the
case
n=1
being true by our hypotheses.

For the inductive step, we assume the result holds for
n
and deduce
it for
n+1
; replacing
A
by
A/\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{n+1}
, we may furthermore
assume that
\mathfrak{m}_{A}^{n+1}=0
. By the inductive hypothesis (and the
smoothness of
T
) we can write
\rho(\sigma)=tu
where
t\in T(A)

and
u\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}
. Since
\overline{\rho}(\sigma)=1
, we see
that
u
and
t
necessarily commute (as do
\rho(\phi)
and
t
, as
both are contained in
T(A)
).

Now, since
\rho
is tamely ramified, we have

\rho(\phi)\rho(\sigma)\rho(\phi)^{-1}=\rho(\sigma)^{q_{v}}
. Since
q_{v}\equiv 1\pmod{p}
, and
u\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}
, we see that
u^{q_{v}}=u
, and
thus that

\rho(\phi)u\rho(\phi)^{-1}=t^{q_{v}-1}u
. Using again that
u\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}
and that
\overline{\rho}(\phi)
is
regular semi-simple, it follows that
u=1
, as required.
∎

6.2.
Ordinary deformation rings

In this section we study some ordinary deformation rings for
\GSp_{4}
. We assume that
v
is a place of
F^{+}
lying over
p
such that
F^{+}_{v}={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.
Write

\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}={\mathcal{O}}\llbracket({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}(p))^{2}\rrbracket
, where

{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}(p)
denotes the pro-
p

completion of
{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}
. There is a canonical character

I_{F^{+}_{v}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}(p)
given by
{\operatorname{Art}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{-1}
, and we
define a pair of characters
\theta_{i}:I_{F^{+}_{v}}\to\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}
,

i=1,2
by letting
\theta_{i}
correspond to the embedding

{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}(p)\rightarrow({\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}_{v}}^{\times}(p))^{2}
given by the
i
th
copy. When
p>2
,
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}={\mathcal{O}}\llbracket x_{1},x_{2}\rrbracket
is formally smooth, while when
p=2
,
\Spec\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}
has 4 irreducible components but the generic fiber is regular.

Assume that
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+_{v}}}}
is ordinary, and fix a
p
-stabilization

(\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2})
of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+_{v}}}}
, so that
(\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2})
is an ordered pair of characters

G_{F^{+}_{v}}\to k^{\times}
. Then
for any
A\in\operatorname{CNL}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
there is an obvious bijection between homomorphisms

\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}\to A
and ordered pairs of characters

(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}):I_{F^{+}_{v}}\to A^{\times}
lifting
(\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2})
,
given by multiplying the characters
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
by the
Teichmüller lifts of
(\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2})
.

Similarly, write
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\GSp_{4},v}={\mathcal{O}}\llbracket\Gal(F^{+,\mathrm{ab}}_{v}/F^{+}_{v})(p)^{2}\rrbracket
,
where

\Gal(F^{+,\mathrm{ab}}_{v}/F^{+}_{v})(p)
is the
pro-
p
completion of
\Gal(F^{+,\mathrm{ab}}_{v}/F^{+}_{v})
. Then we have a universal
pair of characters
(\widetilde{\chi}_{1},\widetilde{\chi}_{2}):G_{F^{+}_{v}}\to\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\GSp_{4},v}

lifting
(\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2})
.

We now introduce the ordinary deformation ring we consider, following
[
Ger19
, §3]
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
denote the flag variety for
\GSp_{4}
over
{\mathcal{O}}
, i.e. the
variety whose
S
-points, for any
S/\Spec{\mathcal{O}}
, parameterize full
flags

0=\Fil_{0}\subset\Fil_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\Fil_{4}={\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{4}

with
\Fil_{i}
being locally free of rank
i
and locally a
direct summand, with the further property
that
(\Fil_{i})^{\perp}=\Fil_{4-i}
for each
i
(where
⟂

is with respect to our usual symplectic form
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{4}
).

Write
R_{v}^{\square}
for the ring
denoted
R_{v}^{\square}
in Section
6.1
when
\Lambda_{v}=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\GSp_{4},v}
. One shows as in the proof
of
[
Ger19
, Lem. 3.2]
that there is a closed subscheme

{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
of

{\mathcal{F}}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}}\Spec R_{v}^{\square}
, such
that for any
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
A
, the
A
-points of

{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
are exactly the pairs

(\Fil_{\bullet},R_{v}^{\square}\to A)
consisting
of a symplectic flag
\Fil_{\bullet}
on
A^{4}
and an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra morphism

R_{v}^{\square}\to A
, such that the
pushforward of the universal lifting over

R_{v}^{\square}\to A
preserves
\Fil_{\bullet}
,
and for
i=1,2,3,4
the action of
G_{F^{+}_{v}}
on

\Fil_{i}/\Fil_{i-1}
is via respectively (the pushforwards to
A

of) the characters
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{2}
,

\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}
,
\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}
.

We
write

R_{v}^{\triangle}:=\im\left(R_{v}^{\square}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{v}}({\mathcal{G}}_{v})\right),

so that
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
is the
scheme-theoretic image of the morphism
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}\to\Spec R_{v}^{\square}
. (Note that here we differ from
[
Ger19
]
by not
passing to the
p
-torsion-free quotient.) We denote by
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}
the corresponding deformation problem.

Exactly as in the proof of
[
Ger19
, Lem. 3.3]
, it follows
immediately from the properness of
{\mathcal{F}}
that
if
E^{\prime}/E
is a finite extension with ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}

and
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\GSp_{4},v}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}
is a morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras, then the

{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}
-points of
\Spf R_{v}^{\triangle}
are exactly those
lifts
\rho
of
\overline{\rho}
having the property that
there is a symplectic flag

0=\Fil_{0}\subset\Fil_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\Fil_{4}={\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}^{4}

as above such that for
i=1,2,3,4
the action of
G_{F^{+}_{v}}
on

\Fil_{i}/\Fil_{i-1}
is via respectively the characters
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}
,

\widetilde{\chi}_{2}
,
\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}
,

\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{1}^{-1}
. Equivalently, these are the lifts
\rho
which
are ordinary with
p
-stabilization
(\widetilde{\chi}_{1},\widetilde{\chi}_{2})
in the sense of Definition
1.8.10
.

Remark 6.2.1
.

As in Definition
1.8.10
, we say that
\overline{\rho}
is
residually
p
-distinguished
if the 4 characters

\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2},\varepsilon^{-1}\overline{\chi}_{2}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1}\overline{\chi}_{1}^{-1}

are pairwise distinct (if
p\not=2
this amounts to

\overline{\chi}_{1}\not=\overline{\chi}_{2}
). In this case the filtration
\Fil_{i}
in the
definition of
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
is uniquely determined by the Galois representation, and
it follows that the map
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}\to\Spec R_{v}^{\square}
is a closed immersion. In
[
BCGP21
, §7.3]
, we made this assumption and assumed
p>2
and studied
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
under the name
R_{v}^{B,\overline{\lx@scalerel@obj{\textlinb{{\char 99\relax} }}}_{v}}
.

Let
E^{\prime}/E
be a finite extension, and let

x:\Spec E^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{v}[1/p]
be a closed
point. Let
\rho_{x}:G_{F^{+}_{v}}\to\GSp_{4}(E^{\prime})
be the pushforward of
the universal lift coming from the composite

\Spec E^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{v}[1/p]\to\Spec R_{v}^{\square}
. Let
\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
denote the
adjoint representation with respect to
\Sp_{4}
, and define a decreasing
filtration
\Fil^{i}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
on
\rho_{x}

by

\Fil^{i}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}:=\{A\in\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}|A\Fil_{j}\rho_{x}\subseteq\Fil_{j-i}\rho_{x}\forall j\};

in
particular,
\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
is the subspace of
\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
preserving the flag
\Fil_{\bullet}\rho_{x}
.
We have
\dim\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}=10
, and
\dim\Fil^{i}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}=6,4,2,1,0
, for
i=0,\ldots,4
respectively.

Lemma 6.2.2
.

(1)

If
H^{2}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x})=0
then
x
is a regular point
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}[1/p]
; and
x
is contained
in a unique irreducible component of

{\mathcal{G}}_{v}[1/p]
, and this component has dimension
16
.
We have

H^{2}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x})=0

if and only if

H^{0}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\bigl(\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}/\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}\bigr)(1))=0.

(2)

The equivalent conditions of part (
1
) hold under any of the following circumstances:

(a)

none of the specializations at
x
of the
characters
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}^{2}\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{2}\varepsilon
,

\widetilde{\chi}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}
are equal
to
\varepsilon
.

(b)

\rho_{x}
is pure and

p
-distinguished.

(c)

\rho_{x}
is pure and
potentially crystalline.

(3)

If
\rho_{x}
is
p
-distinguished and the equivalent conditions of part (
1
) hold (in
particular, if
\rho_{x}
is
p
-distinguished and pure) then the image of
x
in
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
is a regular point which is contained in a unique irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
, which has relative (over
{\mathcal{O}}
) dimension 16.

Proof.

By a
standard tangent-obstruction calculation exactly as in the proof of
[
Ger19
, Lem. 3.7]
(see also
[
Til96
, §5.1]
for the case of general
algebraic groups), the tangent space to

{\mathcal{G}}_{\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}}[1/p]
at
x
has dimension

16+\dim_{E^{\prime}}H^{2}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}),

(6.2.3)

and there is an obstruction class in

H^{2}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x})
whose vanishing implies that
x
is a regular point
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}}[1/p]
.

For the remaining claim in (
1
), note that the space
\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
is self-dual under the trace pairing
(A,B)\rightarrow\operatorname{Tr}(AB)
(since we are in characteristic zero).
If
A\in\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
and
B\in\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
then
AB\in\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
and so
\operatorname{Tr}(AB)=0
. It follows that
\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}\subset(\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x})^{\perp}
. By considering the dimensions of these spaces (
6
and
10-4=6
respectively), we deduce that
this is an equality, and hence the claim follows by Tate local
duality.

For part (
2a
), note that
\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}

has a filtration with graded pieces of rank
1
,
and the characters through which
G_{F^{+}_{v}}
acts on these graded
pieces are
1
,
1
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}^{2}\varepsilon
,

\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{2}\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}\varepsilon
,
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}_{2}^{-1}
.
Part
2c
follows from part (
2a
)
because a potentially crystalline pure representation cannot contain
two Jordan–Hölder factors differing by a cyclotomic twist.

We now turn to part (
2b
), so that
\rho_{x}
is pure and
p
-distinguished, and (since
we have just established part
2c
) we can furthermore assume that we are not potentially
crystalline. Assume
for the sake of contradiction that
H^{0}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\bigl(\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}/\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}\bigr)(1))\neq 0
. We now argue as in the proof
of
[
Ger19
, Lem. 3.7(3)]
. By our assumption, there is some
A\in\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}-\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
such that for all
\sigma\in G_{F^{+}_{v}}
, we
have
\rho_{x}(\sigma)A\rho_{x}(\sigma)^{-1}=\varepsilon(\sigma)^{-1}A\bmod\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
; equivalently,

\varepsilon(\sigma)\rho_{x}(\sigma)A-A\rho_{x}(\sigma)\in\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}.

(6.2.4)

We can and do conjugate
\rho_{x}
so that
it is contained in the usual upper triangular Borel subgroup, so that each

\Fil_{x,i}
is generated by
e_{1},\dots,e_{i}
. Write

\rho_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,1}&*&*&*\\
0&\widetilde{\chi}_{x,2}&*&*\\
0&0&\widetilde{\chi}_{x,3}&*\\
0&0&0&\widetilde{\chi}_{x,4}\end{pmatrix}

(so

\widetilde{\chi}_{x,3}=\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,2}^{-1}
and

\widetilde{\chi}_{x,4}=\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,1}^{-1}
). Let
1\leq s\leq 4
be
minimal with
Ae_{s}\notin\Fil_{x,s-1}
(such an
s
exists by the
hypothesis that
A\notin\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}
), and let
r\geq s
be the
unique integer with
Ae_{s}\in\Fil_{x,r}-\Fil_{x,r-1}
. By (
6.2.4
) and the assumption
on
s
, we see that for all
\sigma\in G_{F^{+}_{v}}
, we
have

\rho_{x}(\sigma)(Ae_{s})\equiv\varepsilon(\sigma)^{-1}A\rho_{x}(\sigma)e_{s}\equiv(\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,s})(\sigma)(Ae_{s})\pmod{\Fil_{x,s-1}}.

Since
r\geq s
this congruence in particular holds modulo
\Fil_{x,r-1}
, whence

\widetilde{\chi}_{x,r}=\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,s}
, and consequently
r>s
;
and furthermore we see that
E\cdot(Ae_{s})+\Fil_{x,s-1}
is

G_{F^{+}_{v}}
-stable. More precisely, we see that the 2-dimensional subquotient

(E\cdot(Ae_{s})+\Fil_{x,s})/\Fil_{x,s-1}
of
\rho_{x}
is isomorphic
to
\varepsilon^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{x,s}\oplus\widetilde{\chi}_{x,s}
.

It is immediate from the
definition of purity that no twist of
\varepsilon\oplus 1
can be a
subrepresentation of
\rho_{x}
, so we must have
s=2

or
s=3
. Since
\Fil_{\bullet}
is symplectic, the possibility

(s,r)=(3,4)
is also ruled out (because we already saw that
(s,r)=(1,2)

is impossible), so we must have
s=2
and
r=3
or
4
. However, since we are
pure and not potentially crystalline, we see in either case that we have

\widetilde{\chi}_{x,1}=\widetilde{\chi}_{x,2}
, which contradicts our
assumption that
\rho_{x}
is
p
-distinguished. So
H^{0}(G_{F^{+}_{v}},\bigl(\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}/\Fil^{1}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho_{x}\bigr)(1))=0
after all,
as claimed.

Part (
3
) follows immediately as when
\rho_{x}
is
p
-distinguished, the map
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}\to\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
is an isomorphism in a neighborhood of
x
.
∎

In the rest of this section, we assume
p>2
. In
[
BCGP21
, Prop. 7.3.4]
, we
showed (in a somewhat hands-on manner) that if
\overline{\rho}
is residually

p
-distinguished, then
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}[1/p]
is irreducible. This was
used in the proof of our modularity lifting theorem. We expect that the same
holds in general, but we don’t prove this. Instead we explain a softer way to proceed. We prove a series of Lemmas which will be used in our modularity lifting theorems.

Lemma 6.2.5
.

Assume that
(\overline{\rho}\otimes\overline{\varepsilon})|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
is finite flat. Then any ordinary pure weight 2 crystalline lift lies on
a unique irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
, which is moreover
independent of the lift. This component has relative dimension 16
over
{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

We first show that a point
\rho
of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
corresponding to an ordinary pure weight 2 crystalline lift lies on a unique irreducible component (if
\rho
were
p
-distinguished this was already part of Lemma
6.2.2
(
3
)). Consider the fiber in
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
over
\rho
in
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
, or in other words consider the space of
G_{F_{v}^{+}}
-stable symplectic filtrations
\{\Fil_{i}\}
on
\rho
on which
G_{F_{v}^{+}}
acts on
\Fil_{i}/\Fil_{i-1}
by
\chi_{1},\chi_{2},\varepsilon^{-1}\chi_{2}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1}\chi_{1}^{-1}
for
i=1,2,3,4
. By assumption
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
are unramified and
\rho
has two dimensional inertia invariants, which hence must be
\Fil_{2}
. Then either
G_{F_{v}^{+}}
has scalar action on
\Fil_{2}
, in which case the fiber is the
\mathbf{P}^{1}
of possible
\Fil_{1}
’s, or there is a unique line on which
G_{F_{v}^{+}}
acts through
\chi_{1,x}
, and the fiber is a point. In particular either way this fiber is connected.

By Lemma
6.2.2
(
2c
) each point of this fiber is contained in a unique irreducible
component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
, and as the fiber is connected, the entire fiber is
contained in this component. It follows that the image of this component in

\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
is the unique irreducible component containing
\rho
.

Now we prove that all such
\rho
lie on the same irreducible component.
Consider the closed subscheme
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}^{\mathrm{flat}}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{v}
whose points
for any
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
A
are pairs
(\Fil_{\bullet},\rho)
where

\rho\otimes\varepsilon
is finite flat and
\Fil_{\bullet}
is a filtration with

\Fil_{2}
unramified. We claim that the formal completions of

{\mathcal{G}}_{v}^{\mathrm{flat}}
at
k^{\prime}
-points for
k^{\prime}/k
finite are formally smooth.
By a standard tangent-obstruction calculation as in Lemma
6.2.2
this amounts to the vanishing of

H^{2}_{\mathrm{flat}}(\Fil^{0}\operatorname{ad}^{0}\overline{\rho})
(
cf.
the proof of
[
Kis09
, Prop. 2.4.4]
).

We now consider
R_{v}^{\triangle,\mathrm{flat}}
, the scheme-theoretic image of
{\mathcal{G}}_{v}^{\mathrm{flat}}
in
R_{v}^{\triangle}
. It is irreducible by the same argument as above, as the fiber over
\overline{\rho}
is either a point or
\mathbf{P}^{1}
. As every pure weight 2 crystalline point lies on this irreducible locus, they all lie on the same unique irreducible component of
R_{v}^{\triangle}
.

Finally the dimension can be computed at any
p
-distinguished point using Lemma
6.2.2

∎

We finally prove a lemma which will help with “Ihara avoidance”. Let
Q\subset R_{v}^{\triangle}
be a minimal prime, corresponding to an irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
.

Lemma 6.2.6
.

Suppose that
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}/Q\to\Spec\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}
is surjective. Then there exists a minimal prime of
R_{v}^{\triangle}/(p)
which contains
Q
and no other minimal prime of
R_{v}^{\triangle}
. Moreover
R_{v}^{\triangle}/Q
has relative dimension 16 over
{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

By the hypothesis we can take an
{\mathbf{F}}_{q}((t))
valued point
x
of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}/Q
so that no ratio of the characters
\chi_{1,x},\chi_{2,x},\chi_{2,x}^{-1}\varepsilon^{-1},\chi_{1,x}^{-1}\varepsilon^{-1}
is
1
or
\varepsilon
(even on inertia). By the same argument as in Lemma
6.2.2
(
2a
) and (
3
) we have that the local ring
R_{v,x}^{\triangle}
is regular. We now take any irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}/(p)
containing
x
.
∎

We expect that the hypothesis in Lemma
6.2.6
is always satisfied. Rather than attempt a direct local proof of this fact we will check it by global means in the application in the next section.

6.3.
An ordinary modularity lifting theorem for
\GSp_{4}
,
p>2

We explain how to prove a modularity lifting theorem for a
p
-adic
Hida family of Hilbert–Siegel modular forms over a totally real
field
F^{+}
in which the odd prime
p
splits completely; this is a
slight adaptation of the arguments of our earlier paper

[
BCGP21
]
and their improvements by
Whitmore
[
Whi22
]
. Indeed,
under a residually
p
-distinguished hypothesis, our theorem is a very
special case of the theorems proved in those papers. (The entire
difficulty in
[
BCGP21
]
was about proving modularity lifting
theorems in the case
l_{0}>0
, but the
l_{0}=0
case that we needed here
is completely routine.) It would of course be more natural not to
include the assumption that
p
splits completely in
F^{+}
, but as
we do not know a reference for the relevant Hida families beyond this
case, we leave such
results for a future paper.

We let
F^{+}
be a totally real field in which
p>2
splits completely and let
\pi
be an ordinary cuspidal automorphic
representation for
\GSp_{4}/F^{+}
of central character
|\cdot|^{2}

and weight
((k_{v},l_{v};2))_{v\mid\infty}
. Using our fixed isomorphism

\imath:{\mathbf{C}}\cong\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
we identify the places
v\mid\infty
and
v\mid p
without further comment. Recall from Theorem
1.8.17

that there is a Galois representation
\rho_{\pi,p}:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})

associated to
\pi
. We let
\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}_{\pi,p}
. We fix
R
, a finite set of finite, prime to
p
places of
F^{+}
containing all the prime to
p
places where
\pi
is ramified.

We make the following assumptions:

Hypothesis 6.3.1
.

(1)

\overline{\rho}
is
\GSp_{4}
-reasonable, in the sense
of
[
Whi22
, Defn. 3.19]
. In
particular,
\overline{\rho}
is absolutely irreducible.

(2)

\overline{\rho}
is tidy, in the sense of
[
BCGP21
, Defn. 7.5.11]
.

(3)

For each
v|p
,

\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
is ordinary of weight 2, with a fixed
p
-stabilization

(\overline{\alpha}_{v},\overline{\beta}_{v})
which is compatible with a fixed choice of ordinary

p
-stabilization of
\pi_{v}
.

(4)

For each
v\in S_{p}
, the representation
\rho_{\pi,p}|_{G_{F_{v}^{+}}}
lies on a
unique irreducible component of
R_{v}^{\triangle}

(where
R_{v}^{\triangle}
is defined via the
p
-stabilization

(\overline{\alpha}_{v},\overline{\beta}_{v})
, and
\rho_{\pi,p}|_{G_{F_{v}^{+}}}
is a
point of
R_{v}^{\triangle}
via the chosen
p
-stabilization on
\pi_{v}
).

(5)

For each place
v\in R
we have:

•

\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}^{+}}}
is trivial.

•

q_{v}\equiv 1\pmod{p}
, and if
p=3
, then
q_{v}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
.

•

\pi_{v}^{\mathrm{Iw}(v)}\not=0
.

Note that we do not assume that
\pi
is ordinary of weight 2 (it will be in the main
application but we also allow
\pi
to have regular weight in order to prove
Lemma
10.4.1
).

Remark 6.3.2
.

The main result of this section will be a “minimal at
p
” modularity lifting theorem for ordinary
p
-adic modular forms. In particular hypothesis (
5
) will be used for Taylor’s Ihara avoidance argument, in order to have no minimality hypotheses away from
p
. In the application,
F^{+}/{\mathbf{Q}}
will be a solvable extension chosen to ensure this, and the main modularity lifting theorem for
{\mathbf{Q}}
will be deduced using base change.

We also note that the theorem is only “minimal at
p
” due to our failure to completely analyze the deformation rings
R_{v}^{\triangle}
in the previous section. However we emphasize that when
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F_{v}^{+}}}
is residually
p
-distinguished then
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}[1/p]
is irreducible and hypothesis (
4
) is automatic.

Remark 6.3.3
.

The most important applications of the
result of this section are in the case
p=3
and
\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}})\subseteq\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
. In this case the
condition that
\overline{\rho}
is
\GSp_{4}
-reasonable can be made completely
explicit, see §
6.4
.

By the assumption that
\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}})
is tidy, we can choose an unramified
place
w_{0}
of
F^{+}
of residue characteristic greater than
5
, with

q_{w_{0}}\not\equiv 1\pmod{p}
, and such that no two eigenvalues
of
\overline{\rho}(\mathrm{Frob}_{w_{0}})
have ratio
q_{w_{0}}
. We set
S=S_{p}\cup R\cup\{w_{0}\}
.

By the above hypotheses, we are in the situation of
[
BCGP21
, Hyp. 7.8.1]
, except that we have not assumed that we are
residually
p
-distinguished. In the notation of
[
BCGP21
, §7.8]

and
[
Whi22
, §7]
, we
take
I=\emptyset
, so that by definition the ring
\Lambda_{I}
is
equal to

\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}:=\widehat{\otimes}_{v|p}\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}
. The only change that we make to
the setup of
[
BCGP21
,
Whi22
]
is that for
v|p
we use the deformation problem
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}
, taking

\overline{\chi}_{1},\overline{\chi}_{2}
to be unramified with

\overline{\chi}_{1}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})=\overline{\alpha}_{p}
,
\overline{\chi}_{2}(\mathrm{Frob}_{v})=\overline{\beta}_{p}
. Note that in
the residually
p
-distinguished case that
\overline{\alpha}_{p}\neq\overline{\beta}_{p}
, this
agrees with the deformation problem denoted
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{B,\overline{\alpha}_{p}}
in

[
BCGP21
,
Whi22
]
.

We can then carry out all of the constructions made in
[
BCGP21
, §7.8]
and
[
Whi22
, §7]
, which for the most part
make no use of the hypothesis that
\overline{\alpha}_{p}\neq\overline{\beta}_{p}
: the proof of

[
Whi22
, Thm. 7.8]
generalizing
[
BCGP21
, Thm. 7.9.4]
only uses the
values of
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}}
for
E^{\prime}/E
a finite extension
(recall that in the non residually
p
-distinguished case we don’t necessarily
understand the values of
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}
on general complete Noetherian
local rings due to its definition as a scheme-theoretic image). We now recall
the key points, allowing ourselves to simplify the notation slightly in
comparison to that of
[
BCGP21
]
, by dropping the symbols “
I
” and
“

c

” appearing there.

In particular, we have the global deformation problem

{\mathcal{S}}_{1}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{O}}\}_{v\in S\smallsetminus S_{p}},\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{1}\}_{v\in R}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{w_{0}}^{\square}\}),

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{1}
is defined in
[
BCGP21
, §7.4.5]
(it corresponds to unipotently ramified
liftings). There is a
surjection of
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-algebras

R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\to\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
, where
\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
is the Hida Hecke
algebra considered in
[
BCGP21
, §7.9]
: it acts faithfully on a finite free

\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-module
M^{1}
, which is
obtained from the ordinary part of the coherent
H^{0}
of
Hilbert–Siegel Shimura varieties.

By Hypothesis
6.3.1
(
4
), for each
v|p
the
representation
\rho_{\pi,p}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
lies on a unique irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle}
, which we denote
\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}
. We let
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}
be the
deformation problem determined by this irreducible component, and
write

{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{O}}\}_{v\in S\smallsetminus S_{p}},\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{1}\}_{v\in R}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{w_{0}}^{\square}\}).

We let
\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}
denote

R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}\otimes_{R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}}\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
. These should be thought of as “
p
-minimal” deformation rings and Hecke algebras, see also Remark
6.3.2
.

Theorem 6.3.4
.

Assume that
we are in the above situation, so that in particular Hypothesis
6.3.1
holds. Then
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}
is a finite
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-algebra, and the
morphism
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}\to\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}
has nilpotent kernel, i.e.
(R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}})^{\operatorname{red}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}
.

Proof.

We first verify that for
v\mid p
, the irreducible components
R_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}
satisfy the hypothesis of Lemma
6.2.6
. For this consider a minimal prime
Q_{\pi}\subset\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
contained in the prime ideal corresponding to
\pi
and the chosen
p
-stabilizations, and consider the composition

R_{v}^{\triangle}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\to\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\to\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}/Q_{\pi}.

By Hypothesis
6.3.1
(
4
), the composite must factor through the component
R_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}
. We now claim that the composite

\Spec\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}/Q_{\pi}\to\Spec R_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}\to\Spec\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}

is surjective, and hence the second map is surjective, which is what we are trying to prove. To see this note that the composite is also

\Spec\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}/Q_{\pi}\to\Spec\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}\to\Spec\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}.

Here the second map is clearly surjective, while the first map is surjective because
\Spec\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}/Q_{\pi}
is finite and torsion free as a
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-module, since
\mathbf{T}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
acts faithfully on a finite free
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-module.

Now we proceed with the proof of the theorem. It is enough to prove that the
support of
M^{1}
in
\Spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}

contains
\Spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi}}
. As in
[
Whi22
, §7.3]
, we have a power
series ring
S_{\infty}
over
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
, and we write
\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}
for the augmentation ideal

\ker(S_{\infty}\to\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}})
. We also have a power
series ring
R^{1}_{\infty}
over

R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1},S}^{\operatorname{loc}}
. Set

R_{\infty,\pi}:=R^{1}_{\infty}\otimes_{R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1},S}^{\operatorname{loc}}}R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1,\pi},S}^{\operatorname{loc}}.

The patching argument
of
[
Whi22
, Prop. 7.11]

provides us in particular with:

•

\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}
-algebra morphisms
S_{\infty}\to R^{1}_{\infty}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
;

•

a
R^{1}_{\infty}
module
M^{1}_{\infty}
which is free as an
S_{\infty}
module (and hence has depth as an
R^{1}_{\infty}
equal to the dimension of
S_{\infty}
).

•

an isomorphism

M^{1}_{\infty}/\mathfrak{a}_{\infty}\cong M^{1}
;

•

a commutative diagram of
S_{\infty}
-algebras

\textstyle{R^{1}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\End_{S_{\infty}}(M^{1}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\otimes_{S_{\infty}}\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}}
\textstyle{R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\End_{\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}}(M^{1})}

It thus suffices to prove that the support of
M^{1}_{\infty}
contains every irreducible component of
R_{\infty,\pi}
. Exactly as in
the proof of Theorem
5.7.14
, we know that the support of
M^{1}_{\infty}/\varpi

contains
\Spec R_{\infty,\pi}/\varpi
. (This is Taylor’s “Ihara
avoidance” argument, using the data

M^{\chi}_{\infty}
etc. from
[
Whi22
, Prop. 7.11]
which we have
not recalled here.) Every irreducible component of the support of

M_{\infty}
has dimension equal to that of
S_{\infty}
, and as every
irreducible component of
\Spec R_{\infty,\pi}
has dimension equal to that
of
S_{\infty}
,
M_{\infty}
will be supported on an irreducible component of
\Spec R_{\infty,\pi}
as soon as it is supported on some point which is only contained in that irreducible component.

It thus suffices to show that for each irreducible component of
\Spec R_{\infty,\pi}
there is a point of
\Spec R_{\infty,\pi}/p
contained in
it and in no other component. Since
R_{\infty,\pi}
is a power series
ring over a completed tensor product of local deformation rings, it suffices to check the
same property for each factor, i.e. to check that the same holds for
R_{v}^{\triangle,\pi}
(for
v\in S_{p}
),
\Spec R_{v}^{1}
(for
v\in R
), and
\Spec R_{w_{0}}^{\square}
. The
first of these follows from Lemma
6.2.6
(noting that we have verified the hypothesis above), the second from
[
BCGP21
, Prop. 7.4.7]
, and the last from our choice of
w_{0}
, which
guarantees that
R_{w_{0}}^{\square}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
.
∎

6.4.
Subgroups of
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

In this section, we shall identify the precise subgroups of
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

we are allowing for our modularity lifting theorems.
We first identify the regular semi-simple elements in
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
.
We have:

Lemma 6.4.1
.

There are three conjugacy classes of elements
g\in\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

such that
\nu(g)=1
and
g
is regular semi-simple, namely:

(1)

The unique conjugacy class of elements of order
5
,

(2)

The unique conjugacy class of elements of order
10
,

(3)

The unique conjugacy class of elements of order
8
which lie in
\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
.

There are five conjugacy classes of elements
g\in\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

such that
\nu(g)=-1
and
g
is regular semi-simple, namely:

(1)

Both conjugacy classes of elements of order
20
,

(2)

Three of the five conjugacy classes of elements of order
8
,
namely those whose images in
\mathrm{PSp}_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
lie in the conjugacy
classes
4D
or
8A
but not
4C
in the notation
of Lemma
9.1.3
.

We now turn to reasonableness
[
Whi22
, Defn. 3.19]
. Although this definition does not
a priori

depend only on the image of
\overline{\rho}
, it shall turn out that that under our
running assumptions this will be true.

Lemma 6.4.2
.

Suppose that
A/{\mathbf{Q}}
is an abelian surface, and that
A
has good
reduction at some
p>2
. Then
the image of
\overline{\rho}_{A,p}|_{G_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{p})}}
coincides with the image
of
\overline{\rho}_{A,p}|_{G_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}}
for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Let
K/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
be the fixed field of the kernel of
\overline{\rho}_{A,p}|_{G_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}}
, which
certainly contains
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}(\zeta_{p})
. Since
\Gal({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}(\zeta_{p}))
is cyclic,
the lemma holds unless there exists an inclusion
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{2}})\subseteq K
. Assume such
an inclusion exists. It follows that
the root discriminant
\delta_{K}
is divisible by the root discriminant of
{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{2}})
,
which is
p^{(2p^{2}-3p)/p(p-1)}=p^{(2p-3)/(p-1)}
. The assumption that
A
has good reduction implies that
A[p]/{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}

is a finite flat group scheme, which by
[
Fon85
, 2.1 Thm. 1]
implies that the root discriminant
of
K
satisfies

v_{p}(\delta_{K})<1+\frac{1}{p-1}.

Since
p\geq 3
, this contradicts our lower bound:

v_{p}(\delta_{K})\geq v_{p}(\delta_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{p^{2}})})=1+\frac{1}{p-1}+\frac{p-3}{p-1}.\qed

We deduce:

Lemma 6.4.3
.

Let
A/{\mathbf{Q}}
be an abelian surface
with a prime to
3
polarization and good reduction at
3
, and let

\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}_{A,3}:G_{{\mathbf{Q}}}\rightarrow\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

denote the corresponding mod
3
representation. Then the following
hypotheses:

(1)

\overline{\rho}
is
\GSp_{4}
-reasonable in the sense of
[
Whi22
, Defn. 3.19]
,

(2)

\overline{\rho}
is tidy in the sense of
[
BCGP21
, Defn. 7.5.11]
,

(3)

\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3})})
contains a regular
semi-simple element,

(4)

\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}})\smallsetminus\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3})})
contains a regular
semi-simple element,

are satisfied precisely if
\Gamma^{\prime}=\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}})

in Table
6.4.4

has a tick, where otherwise
the cross
indicates the corresponding obstruction to condition (
1
),
(
2
), (
3
), or (
4
).
In particular, these
conditions are all satisfied if
\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}})=\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
.

\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cap\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

Table 6.4.4.
Conjugacy classes of subgroups
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

with
\nu(\Gamma^{\prime})\neq 1
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cap\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

absolutely irreducible. LMFDB labels determine the conjugacy class
of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, the small group labels
[
BEO01
]

determine
\Gamma
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
up to abstract isomorphism.

Proof.

Note that if
\Gamma^{\prime}=\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}})
,
and
\Gamma=\overline{\rho}(G_{{\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3})})
, then

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cap\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
. Furthermore, reasonableness (which a priori depends on the image of
\overline{\rho}|_{G({\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3^{n}}))}

for all
n
) only depends on the image of
\overline{\rho}|_{G({\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3}))}
by
Lemma
6.4.2
.
As noted in
[
Whi22
, §4.3]
, the spanning condition of reasonableness is satisfied
for all of these subgroups.
We have listed the abstract isomorphism types of
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma

according to the small groups database
[
BEO01
]
when they are of small order.
The LMFDB subgroup labels
[
LMF24
]

(which for proper subgroups are of the form
3.i.n
where
\texttt{i}=[\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3}):\Gamma^{\prime}]
) determine
\Gamma^{\prime}

up to conjugacy in
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
.
The groups of larger order are described more explicitly in
[
BCGP21
, Lemma 7.5.21]
.
The abstract isomorphism type of
\Gamma^{\prime}
is already enough to determine
\Gamma^{\prime}

up to conjugation in
\GSp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
(under the assumption that
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cap\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})

acts absolutely irreducibly) except for two pairs with
|\Gamma^{\prime}|=64
, one pair with
|\Gamma^{\prime}|=128
, and the pair of groups
with
|\Gamma^{\prime}|=768
, and in all such cases they can be distinguished by the abstract isomorphism type
of
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cap\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
.
∎

Remark 6.4.5

(
\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
is not
\GL_{4}
-adequate)
.

There is a natural inclusion

\overline{\rho}:\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})\hookrightarrow\GL_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3}).

(6.4.6)

It turns out that the image
G
of
\overline{\rho}
is
not
adequate
(in the sense of
[
Tho17
, Defn. 2.20]
), which is the reason
why, when
p=3
, we need to use
\GSp_{4}
modularity lifting theorems
rather than
U(4)
automorphy lifting theorems (in contrast to our treatment of
the case
p=2
in Section
5
). The failure of adequacy can be seen directly as
follows. The group
G\simeq\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3})
has exactly two irreducible
representations
V
,
V^{\sigma}
of dimension
4
over
{\mathbf{C}}
. The
representations are defined over the ring
{\mathbf{Z}}[\zeta_{3}]
and are
conjugate under the action of
\Gal({\mathbf{Q}}(\zeta_{3})/{\mathbf{Q}})

[
CCN+85
]
.
The mod
\pi=(1-\zeta_{3})
reduction of this representation
is
\overline{\rho}
. The corresponding mod
\pi^{2}=(3)
reduction:

\rho:G\rightarrow\SL_{4}({\mathbf{Z}}[\zeta]/3)=\SL_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

gives a non-trivial deformation of
\overline{\rho}
and a non-zero class
in
H^{1}(G,\mathfrak{sl}_{4})\subset H^{1}(G,\mathfrak{gl}_{4})
. The deformation
\rho
is not, however,
valued in
\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{3}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
; this reflects the fact that
V
is not self-dual in characteristic zero;
we have
V^{\vee}\simeq V^{\sigma}
.
In particular, identifying
\mathfrak{gl}_{4}
with
\Hom(\overline{\rho},\overline{\rho})\simeq\overline{\rho}\otimes\overline{\rho}\simeq\Sym^{2}(\overline{\rho})\oplus\wedge^{2}(\overline{\rho})
,
this cohomology class lives in
H^{1}(G,\wedge^{2}\overline{\rho})
. In contrast, for
\overline{\rho}
to be adequate as a symplectic representation, it suffices that

H^{1}(G,\mathfrak{sp}_{4})=0
where (in this example)
\mathfrak{sp}_{4}
may be identified with
\Sym^{2}(\overline{\rho})
.

This example is similar to the failure of the image of the map

\overline{\rho}:\SL_{2}({\mathbf{F}}_{5})\hookrightarrow\GL_{2}({\mathbf{F}}_{5})

(6.4.7)

to be adequate. This failure of adequacy for (
6.4.7
)
does not cause an issue in
[
Wil95
]
; one exploits the fact that the fixed field of
the kernel of the adjoint representation of
\overline{\rho}_{E,5}
for an elliptic curve
E/{\mathbf{Q}}

does not contain
\zeta_{5}
(see the proof
of
[
Wil95
, Prop 1.11]
). On the other hand, for an abelian surface
A
, the fixed of the kernel of
the adjoint representation of
\overline{\rho}_{A,3}
always contains
\zeta_{3}
, so there is no
way to avoid this cohomological obstruction. Hence this situation is more analogous to
the problem of proving
modularity lifting for elliptic curves over
{\mathbf{Q}}(\sqrt{5})

using
5
-adic modularity lifting theorems; see the introduction
to
[
KT17a
]
for an exposition of this case, and an explanation
of why there are classes in the dual Selmer group (so-called “Lie
classes”) that cannot be killed by Taylor–Wiles primes.

7.
Multiplicity one
theorems

Our classicality theorems in low weight (in particular Theorem
4.12.4
)
require as input a multiplicity one theorem in characteristic zero.
The main goal of this section is to prove
such a theorem. Note that multiplicity one really consists
of two separate statements — firstly that the multiplicity is at least one
(a
p
-adic modularity statement), and secondly that the multiplicity
is at most one.

One approach to proving multiplicity one (following
Diamond
[
Dia97
]
) would be to prove an
R=\mathbf{T}
theorem
for the corresponding ordinary Hida family and then, assuming
the local deformation ring at
p
is formally smooth, deduce
that the corresponding module
M
of modular forms is free,
and moreover free of rank one by specialization at classical points.
Such an argument would work
if we made the additional
hypotheses that
p>2
and that
\overline{\rho}
was
p
-distinguished (as is
done in
[
BCGP21
]
). Since we are not making such assumptions,
a further argument is required.
The first point to note is that we are working in characteristic
zero and hence we only need prove that
M
is free (and non-zero) after localizing
at a height one prime
\mathfrak{q}
corresponding to our characteristic zero
representation. To show that
M
is non-zero when
p=2
we are able to
appeal to the
R=\mathbf{T}
theorem for unitary groups that we proved in Section
5
, while for
p>2
we use the
R=\mathbf{T}
theorems
for
\GSp_{4}
proved in Section
6.3
. In either case, Diamond’s argument applies (at
least in principle) providing
that the formal completion of the local deformation ring at
\mathfrak{q}

is regular, something that is ultimately true under our hypotheses.

More precisely, what is ultimately required for our arguments is the following. First, we need
an
R^{\operatorname{red}}[1/p]=\mathbf{T}[1/p]
theorem in our higher Hida theory (not yet classical) situation.
(In truth, when
p>2
,
we get away with a weaker version of
such a theorem in a neighbourhood of the prime
\mathfrak{q}
, at the cost of some further local
complications already considered in §
6
.) This proves
that
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
is non-zero. Second, we want to control the relative
tangent space of
R
at the prime
\mathfrak{q}
. This is closely related
to establishing the vanishing of the adjoint Bloch-Selmer group (in
characteristic zero) of our characteristic zero representation.
Theorems of this kind were proved by Newton
and Thorne
[
NT23
]
in some generality for Galois
representations associated to automorphic
representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
of unitary type,
and we follow their arguments closely.
In fact our situation
is for the most part simpler than theirs, since as we are
assuming that
\overline{\rho}
is absolutely irreducible we
do not have to use pseudorepresentations.
Finally, we need enough local properties of the local deformation
ring at
p
in characteristic zero at
\mathfrak{q}
, and this is what ultimately
requires the
p
-distinguished hypothesis in characteristic zero.

A summary of this section is as follows.
In §
7.1
, we adapt the Galois-theoretic arguments of Newton–Thorne
[
NT23
]
to
our setting.
In §
7.2
we set up the basic patching formalism required
for our argument,
and in §
7.4
, we show how this can be applied
in the setting of
\GSp_{4}
over
{\mathbf{Q}}
, by patching modules coming from higher
Hida theory, as recalled in §
7.3
. Note that there is quite a lot
of overlap between the arguments of §
7.4

and of similar ones in
[
BCGP21
, §7.8, 7.9]

— the difference being that the
latter worked under a more restrictive hypothesis on
\overline{\rho}

but also proved strong integral statements. Finally,
in §
7.5
, we prove
the desired multiplicity one theorem.
Note that our arguments certainly require understanding
the multiplicities of certain automorphic representations
in cohomology, which ultimately uses
Arthur’s
classification of discrete automorphic representations of
\GSp_{4}
.

7.1.
Taylor–Wiles primes

Let
H
be a compact subgroup of
\GSp_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
. After replacing
E
by a finite extension, we can assume that
for each element
h\in H
, the characteristic polynomial of
h
is
already split over
E
. We will assume this without comment from now
on. We write
\overline{H}
for the image of
H
in
\GSp_{4}(k)
. Throughout
this section, we write
\operatorname{ad}
,
\operatorname{ad}^{0}
for the Lie algebras associated
to
\GSp_{4}
and
\Sp_{4}
over
{\mathcal{O}}
.

Definition 7.1.1
.

A compact subgroup
H
of

\GSp_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
is
integrally enormous
if it acts absolutely
irreducibly on
E^{4}
, and if for all
simple
E[H]
-submodules

W\subset E\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}
, there exists an
element

h\in H
such that

•

1
is an eigenvalue for the action of
h
on
W
, and

•

the image
\overline{h}
of
h
in
\GSp_{4}(k)
is regular semi-simple (i.e. has
4
distinct
eigenvalues).

Lemma 7.1.2

(Examples of integrally enormous representations, I)
.

(1)

If
\overline{H}
contains a regular semi-simple element, and the
Zariski closure of
H
contains
\Sp_{4}
, then
H
is integrally
enormous.

(2)

Suppose that the action of
H\subset\Sp_{4}(\mathcal{O})
is absolutely irreducible but becomes
reducible after restriction to an index
2
subgroup
G
.
Suppose that
(\overline{H}\smallsetminus\overline{G})
contains
a regular semi-simple
element.
Suppose
that the Zariski closure of
G
is
\SL_{2}\times\SL_{2}
. Then
H

is integrally enormous.

Proof.

We first consider case (
1
).
Since
\Sp_{4}
acts irreducibly on both its standard
representation and on
\operatorname{ad}^{0}
,
H
acts
irreducibly on both
E^{4}
and
E\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}
. (Indeed if
H
preserves a subspace,
we obtain a partial flag which is stabilized by the Zariski closure
of
H
.) Furthermore every element
h\in\GSp_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
has
1
as an
eigenvalue on
E\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}
; so if
h\in H
is any element whose
reduction
\overline{h}
is regular semi-simple, then

h
satisfies the
conditions of Definition
7.1.1
.

We now consider case (
2
).
Our argument is essentially a characteristic zero version of the proof of
[
BCGP21
, Lemma 7.5.17]
(though note
that the roles of
G
and
H
are reversed).
If
h\in(H\smallsetminus G)\cap\Sp_{4}
, then the eigenvalues of
h

are of the form
(\alpha,\alpha^{-1},-\alpha,-\alpha^{-1})
, and by assumption
we may assume that the image of
h
in
\Sp_{4}(k)

lands in
(\overline{H}\smallsetminus\overline{G})
and
is regular semi-simple — the latter condition being equivalent to the condition
that
\overline{\alpha}^{4}\neq 1
.
Now, following the proof of
[
BCGP21
, Lemma 7.5.17]
,
the representation
E\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}
decomposes over the algebraic closure of
E
into two irreducible
representations
of dimension
6
and
4

on which
h
has eigenvalues
(1,-1,\alpha^{2},-\alpha^{2},\alpha^{-2},-\alpha^{-2})

and
(-1,1,\alpha^{2},\alpha^{-2})
respectively, both of which contain
1
as an eigenvalue.
∎

We now let
F^{+}
be a totally real number field in which
p
splits completely,
and we fix a continuous representation
\rho:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
satisfying the
following hypothesis.

Hypothesis 7.1.3
.

Assume that:

(1)

\rho
is unramified at all but finitely many places.

(2)

\nu\circ\rho=\varepsilon^{-1}
.

(3)

\overline{\rho}:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}(k)
is absolutely irreducible.

(4)

\rho(G_{F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})})
is integrally enormous.

(5)

\rho
is pure.

(6)

for all
v\in S_{p}
,
\rho|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
is
ordinary, semistable of weight
2
, pure, and
p
-distinguished. We choose a
p
-stabilization
(\alpha_{p},\beta_{p})
of

\rho|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
(and thus of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}
, so that by definition
\rho|_{G_{F^{+}_{v}}}

corresponds to a
point of
\Spec R_{p}^{\triangle}
).

(7)

If
p=2
then

\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}(i)})=\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}})
.

(8)

If
p>2
then

\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}})\smallsetminus\Sp_{4}({\mathbf{F}}_{p})
contains a regular semi-simple element.

We will use the following result to verify condition (
4
) in Hypothesis
7.1.3
.

Corollary 7.1.4

(Examples of integrally enormous representations, II)
.

Suppose that
\rho:G_{F^{+}}\to\GSp_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
satisfies either of the following two sets of conditions:

(A1)

the
Zariski closure of
\rho(G_{F^{+}})
contains
\Sp_{4}
, and

(A2)

\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}(\zeta_{p})})
contains a regular semi-simple
element.

Or alternatively:

(B1)

\rho
is induced from a quadratic extension
K/F^{+}
disjoint from
the compositum
F_{\infty}
of
F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
with the fixed field of the similitude character.
The
Zariski closure of
\rho(G_{F^{+}})
contains
\SL_{2}\times\SL_{2}
, and

(B2)

\overline{\rho}(G_{F_{\infty}})\smallsetminus\overline{\rho}(G_{K.F_{\infty}})
contains a regular semi-simple
element.

Then
\rho(G_{F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})})
is integrally enormous.

Proof.

We assume the first set of conditions.
Since the regular semi-simple elements have order prime to
p
, and
since
F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/F^{+}(\zeta_{p})
is a pro-
p
extension,

\overline{\rho}(G_{F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})})
contains a regular semi-simple
element. Since the Zariski closure of
\rho(G_{F^{+}})

contains
\Sp_{4}
by assumption, so does the Zariski closure of

\rho(G_{F^{+}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})})
(note that taking the derived
subgroup is compatible with taking the Zariski closure,
by
[
Bor91
, 2.1(e)]
). The result follows from Lemma
7.1.2
(
1
).

Now we assume the second set of conditions. The property of being integrally enormous is inherited from
subgroups so it suffices to show that
H:=\rho(G_{F_{\infty}})
is
integrally enormous. By construction
H\subset\Sp_{4}(\mathcal{O})

is absolutely irreducible but becomes reducible after restriction to
G=\rho(G_{K.F_{\infty}})
.
Moreover, as in the first case, we deduce that the Zariski closure of
H
contains
\SL_{2}\times\SL_{2}
.
Hence the result follows from Lemma
7.1.2
(
2
).
∎

We now use the notation for deformation rings that was introduced in Section
6.1
. Let
S
be a finite set
of places of
F^{+}
containing
S_{p}
and the places where
\rho
is ramified. We define a global deformation problem
{\mathcal{S}}
by

{\mathcal{S}}=(\overline{\rho},S,\{\Lambda_{v}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{O}}\}_{v\in S\smallsetminus S_{p}},\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}\}_{v\in S_{p}}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square}\}_{v\in S\smallsetminus S_{p}}),

(7.1.5)

where
\Lambda_{v}=\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},v}

and
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\triangle}
is as in Section
6.2
. As in Section
6.1
, we write
\Lambda_{\GSp_{4},F^{+}}=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p}}\Lambda_{v}
. Given a nonempty subset
T\subseteq S
, which we
assume contains
S_{p}
, we fix an extension of
\rho

to a
T
-framed lifting
(\rho,\{\gamma_{v}\}_{v\in T})

of
\overline{\rho}
(i.e. fix choices of
\gamma_{v}\in\widehat{\GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}})

for each
v\in T
). Write
\mathfrak{q}
(resp.
\mathfrak{q}_{S,T}
) for the kernel of the
homomorphism
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}
(resp.
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\to{\mathcal{O}}
) corresponding to
\rho
, and write

\mathfrak{q}^{\operatorname{loc}}_{T}
for the kernel of the composite

R_{{\mathcal{S}},T}^{\operatorname{loc}}\to R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}\to{\mathcal{O}}
.

Write
W=\operatorname{ad}\rho
,
W_{E}=W\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}E
,
W_{E/{\mathcal{O}}}=W_{E}/W
, and for
each
m\geq 1
write
W_{m}=W\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
; and we similarly
write
W^{0}=\operatorname{ad}^{0}\rho
,
W^{0}_{E/{\mathcal{O}}}=W^{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{E/{\mathcal{O}}}
,

W^{0}_{m}=W^{0}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{m}
. We write

H^{i}(F^{+}_{S}/F^{+},W)^{\prime}:=\im\biggl(H^{i}(F^{+}_{S}/F^{+},W^{0})\to H^{i}(F^{+}_{S}/F^{+},W)\biggr),

and similarly we write
H^{i}(F^{+}_{S}/F^{+},W_{E})^{\prime}

and so on. For any place
v
of
F^{+}
we also have cohomology groups

H^{i}(F_{v}^{+},W)^{\prime}
etc. defined in the analogous way. We write

h^{i}(F_{S}^{+},W_{m})^{\prime}
for the length of the finite
{\mathcal{O}}
-module

H^{i}(F_{S}^{+},W_{m})^{\prime}
, and similarly for
h^{i}(F_{v}^{+},W_{m})^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.1.6
.

Write
\mathfrak{z}

for the centre of
\operatorname{ad}
. Since
\operatorname{ad}^{0}

and
